## Summary
"Wa Nalqa al-Ahibba" (And We Meet the Beloved) is a lecture series by Amr Khaled, recorded on video and held in the Assabile archive, with 79 lessons by the series page's own count. It opens with the Rightly Guided Caliphs and with Abu Bakr al-Siddiq, setting out his lineage, his names and titles, his conduct before and after Islam, and the presenter's view of his character. What follows is the speaker's own speech as the machine transcript of the recording renders it. Anything not traced to a source, or read from a line that is unclear in the transcript, is marked as needing a source.

:::perspectives
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he holds that the key to Abu Bakr's personality is his love of the truth and his attachment to it, that truthfulness, mercy, wisdom and humility were among his most marked qualities, and that what set him ahead of others was not chiefly the number of his prayers and fasts but something that settled in his heart. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:31:30}
- **The sources he names:** he names books on Abu Bakr, among them a work by Ali al-Tantawi (the name is read from a garbled line) and al-Aqqad's "Abqariyyat al-Siddiq", together with "Usd al-Ghaba" and "al-Isaba fi tamyiz al-sahaba" as older standard references. These are the speaker's recommendations; the books were not checked for this entry. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:07:30}
:::

## Episode 1: the series and Abu Bakr al-Siddiq

- The episode announces that the series begins with the Rightly Guided Caliphs, the first of whom is Abu Bakr al-Siddiq, and the speaker calls it one of the hardest episodes he has given. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:05:30}
- The speaker asks what the miracle of Islam is. His answer is the Qur'an, not only for its eloquence but because whoever follows it and acts on it becomes a great figure in history. He gives [[person/sad-ibn-abi-waqqas|Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] as examples and presents Abu Bakr as another instance of this miracle. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:04:30}
- His name before Islam is given as Abdullah ibn Abi Quhafa, and his kunya is Abu Bakr. The speaker says his father's original name was Uthman and that his mother was Umm al-Khayr, who embraced Islam early in the house of al-Arqam ibn Abi al-Arqam. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:12:00} (needs_source: the mother's name is not given in the transcript, and the name Umm al-Khayr is read from a line that is only partly clear)
- He belonged to the Banu Taym of the Quraysh. The speaker says his lineage meets the Prophet's at the sixth forefather, and that the Banu Taym were responsible in the Jahiliyya for paying blood-money and for the sums due in such cases. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:13:00}
- His title "Atiq". The speaker says the title is reported as coming from the Prophet, in the sense of being freed from the Fire, and that some say he had the title before Islam for the beauty of his face or the goodness of his character. He explains that "atiq" means the noble, the fine and the old. The report he gives is not traced to a chain in the episode, and its wording is not reproduced here. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:11:00} (needs_source: the report is cited without a chain)
- Ages the speaker gives: born two years after the Year of the Elephant, or two and a half years according to a variant; began Islam at thirty-eight; migrated at fifty-one; held the caliphate for about two years and some months; died at sixty-three, on a Monday, the day the Prophet also died. These are the speaker's statements and arithmetic, not checked against the sources. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:15:30}
- Wives and children as the speaker lists them: Qutayla and Umm Rumman before Islam, Asma bint Umays after it, and Habiba bint Zayd. Among his children he names Abdullah, Asma, Abd al-Rahman, Aisha, Muhammad and Umm Kulthum, and gives Abdullah as his eldest. The names are read from overlapping lines of the transcript and need a source. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:19:00} (needs_source)
- Before Islam, as the speaker tells it, he never prostrated to an idol: his father brought him to his idols to prostrate, and he broke one with a stone. He never drank wine, and he explains this by saying that he guards his honour and keeps himself. He was the most knowledgeable of the Quraysh in genealogy, and the speaker gives an anecdote in which he introduces the Prophet to the tribes of Arabia by name. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:25:30}
- He was a friend of the Prophet before the mission. The speaker says he travelled with the Prophet and the Prophet's uncle Abu Talib on a journey to Syria, in which he places the meeting with the monk Bahira. This is a tradition the speaker gives without a source in the episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:27:30} (needs_source: the journey and the meeting are given without a source)
- The speaker opens with reports in which the Prophet describes Abu Bakr as the most truthful and the firmest in faith, and says that Abu Bakr surpassed others not by many prayers and fasts but by something that settled in his heart. He does not name the sources of these reports in the episode, and their wording is not reproduced here. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:28:00} (needs_source: the reports are cited without named sources)
- He describes Abu Bakr's appearance as thin, fair of face, with a dyed beard, a prominent forehead and a fine nose, and says that appearance reflects character. This is the speaker's own description, given without a cited report in the episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep01 00:21:30} (needs_source: no report is named for the description)

## Left out of this episode

Not written from episode 1: the sums of wealth the speaker gives in a passage that is garbled in the transcript, a line that repeats a single word and is not speech, and the name of Abu Bakr's mother as given in an overlapping line. These are listed for review in the lane ledger.

## Episode 2: Abu Bakr in the Hijra and after

- The episode continues the first. The speaker relates, as his own account, that Abu Bakr was the first man to accept Islam and that his call brought six of the ten companions promised paradise into the faith, naming Uthman, Talha, al-Zubayr, Sa'd, Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf and Abu Ubayda. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:01:30} (needs_source: the list of converts is the speaker's account and has not been checked against an independent source here)
- The speaker says Abu Bakr spent forty thousand dirhams freeing those who were weak in Mecca, among them Bilal ibn Rabah, whom he bought from Umayya ibn Khalaf. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:02:30} (needs_source: the sum and the details of the purchase are the speaker's account)
- The speaker relates that Abu Bakr was beaten until his face swelled when he defended the Prophet in Mecca, and that the Prophet asked about him and was reassured. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:05:00} (needs_source)
- On the Night Journey and the Ascension, the speaker says Abu Bakr affirmed what the Prophet told him, that this affirmation is the origin of the title al-Siddiq, and that the Prophet called him the truthful one. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:07:30} (needs_source: the origin of the title is given in the speaker's account)
- The speaker reflects that whoever affirms what is harder to believe will more easily affirm what is less hard. This is the speaker's own reflection. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:08:30} (speaker's view)
- The speaker relates that Ibn al-Dughunna, a notable of Quraysh, gave Abu Bakr his protection when he meant to leave for Abyssinia. Quraysh then asked Ibn al-Dughunna to stop Abu Bakr from reciting the Qur'an in public; Abu Bakr built an open space before his house and recited there, women and children gathered to him, and Ibn al-Dughunna returned his protection. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:11:30} (needs_source: the protection and the open space are the speaker's account)
- The speaker describes Abu Bakr weeping as he recited. He relates that when the Prophet was ill and asked him to lead the prayer, Aisha objected that he was too tender-hearted to be heard, and that a delegation from Yemen wept on hearing the Qur'an and Abu Bakr remarked that they had been like that before hearts hardened. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:13:00} (needs_source)
- The speaker relates Abu Bakr's wager with Ubayy ibn Khalaf on the Qur'anic prophecy that the Romans, defeated, would prevail within a few years; he says the Prophet advised him to extend the term, and that the outcome came within the extended period. The verse is not written here. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:15:00} (needs_source: the wager and its dates need an independent historical source)
- Before the Hijra, the speaker says, Abu Bakr prepared two riding camels, and Aisha is reported to have said he wept for joy when the Prophet told him he would accompany him. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:17:30} (needs_source: Aisha's account as the speaker gives it)
- The speaker says Abu Bakr took all his money on the Hijra. He states as his own maxim that religion is dearer than children. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:19:00} (needs_source for the sum taken; the maxim is the speaker's view)
- In the cave of Thawr the speaker relates that Abu Bakr entered first to check it, stopped the openings with pieces of his clothing, and that when the Prophet slept on his lap a creature bit Abu Bakr's leg; his tears fell on the Prophet, who woke, and the bite healed after the Prophet's saliva was applied. This is presented as a traditional narrative, not a confirmed historical event. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:22:30} (status: tradition; needs_source)
- The speaker relates Ibn Abbas's view that the tranquillity of the cave descended on Abu Bakr, and points to the Qur'anic phrase about "the second of two", which is not written here. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:23:00} (needs_source: Ibn Abbas's view as the speaker gives it)
- On the road, the speaker relates, Abu Bakr walked before, behind, and to the right and left of the Prophet to guard him, and when asked answered in the spirit of concern for him; when people asked who the man was, he said it was a man guiding the way. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:28:00} (needs_source)
- The speaker relates that the people of Medina did not know the Prophet by sight and took Abu Bakr for him, since he walked ahead of the group. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:29:00} (needs_source)
- At Badr the speaker relates that Abd al-Rahman ibn Abi Bakr, then not yet a Muslim, challenged to single combat; Abu Bakr asked to face him and the Prophet forbade it. He says Abd al-Rahman later accepted Islam and that his father said he would have killed him had he met him. The speaker also relates that Abu Bakr stood by the Prophet's shelter with a drawn sword to guard him. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:32:00} (needs_source)
- The speaker recounts a narrative in which the gates of paradise are open to each kind of good work and Abu Bakr asks whether one person can be called from all of them; the Prophet says yes and hopes he will be one of them. This is given in substance, not in wording. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:37:00} (status: tradition; needs_source)
- In one day, the speaker says, Abu Bakr fasted, visited a sick man, and gave a beggar a piece of bread he took from his grandson's hand, and the Prophet gave him the good news of paradise. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:39:30} (needs_source)
- The speaker relates the contest in giving before Tabuk: Umar brought half his property, Abu Bakr brought all of it, and when the Prophet asked what he had left his family, Abu Bakr answered that he had left them God and His Messenger. Umar then said he would never outdo him again. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:42:00} (needs_source)
- The speaker relates that Abu Bakr swept the house of a blind old woman each week, and that Umar discovered this. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:46:30} (needs_source)
- The speaker relates that the Prophet, asked whom he loved most, named Aisha, and then her father; that he held the hands of Abu Bakr and Umar and said that they would be raised so. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:48:00} (status: tradition; needs_source)
- The speaker relates a disagreement between Umar and Abu Bakr; Umar, regretting it, went to Abu Bakr's house, and then went to the Prophet, who told him that God had sent him and that Abu Bakr had affirmed him when others called him a liar, and asked that his companion not be harmed. Umar said he was the one in the wrong. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:50:00} (needs_source)
- The speaker states that, after the prophets, no one greater than Abu Bakr has appeared. This is the speaker's own view and tribute. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:24:30} (speaker's view)
- The speaker announces the next episode, which will cover the Prophet's love for Abu Bakr, the last moments of the Prophet, the pledge of allegiance, and the two years of Abu Bakr's caliphate. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep02 00:51:00}

## Left out of this episode

Not written from episode 2: passages about Palestine and the present conflict, including an appeal for donations, which are outside the subject of the series and are listed for review in the lane ledger; the speaker's remarks on how religious speech is labelled today, which concern the present; lines that are garbled in the transcript, including the wording of Umar's words about Abu Bakr's scent and the camel, the detail of the water and shade on the road, and an anecdote about a man in a wheelchair; and the Qur'an verses the speaker recites, which are not typed. The episode also contains a repetition loop, which is not speech and is not used.

## Episode 3: Abu Bakr's love of the Prophet and his death

:::perspectives
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he presents Abu Bakr's love for the Prophet, as a model of truthfulness, steadfastness and attachment, and holds that his conduct in the days of the Prophet's death shows that he was fit to bear the trust after him. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:31:00}
- **The speaker's account of the Saqifa (narrative):** he tells the meeting of the Ansar in the Saqifa of Banu Sa'ida and the pledge to Abu Bakr in the form common in the Sunni narrative, with Abu Bakr's arguments. No other accounts of the succession are set out or weighed in this episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:35:00}
:::

- The speaker says the episode follows Abu Bakr's life and his love for the Prophet, and gives new moments of that love. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:01:00}
- He relates that Abu Bakr defended the Prophet in Mecca when some of Quraysh attacked him, and was struck on the face with a sandal until his face swelled and he fainted; he names the attacker as Uqba ibn Abi Mu'ayt (the name is unclear in the transcript). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:02:30} (needs_source: the speaker's narrative; the name needs a listening check)
- He says that his people carried him home thinking him dead, that when he woke he asked after the Prophet, that his mother sent to Umm Jamil Fatima bint al-Khattab, Umar's sister, to reassure him, and that he swore not to eat or drink until he saw the Prophet with his own eyes. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:04:00} (needs_source: the speaker's narrative)
- He describes their meeting: the Prophet embraced Abu Bakr after seeing him weep, and the speaker counts this among the mutual loves between them. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:04:30} (needs_source: the speaker's narrative)
- The speaker's remark: the love of the Prophet is a provision from God, placed in the heart of whom He wills, and it is not reached by means alone. This is his reflection. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:09:30} (the speaker's view)
- He relates that Abu Bakr, stung in the cave, did not cry out from the pain but wept until his tears fell on the Prophet and woke him, and that the Prophet then spat on the place and it healed. This adds detail to the same story told in episode 2. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:10:30} (status: tradition; needs_source)
- He relates that on the road to Madina Abu Bakr milked a shepherd's ewe for them, and that the Prophet drank first. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:11:00} (status: tradition; needs_source)
- He relates that on the road the Prophet asked Abu Bakr whether he loved him and would die for him, that Abu Bakr answered yes, and he reads this answer as a lesson in understanding and attachment. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:12:00} (needs_source)
- He tells the story of Rabi'a ibn Ka'b al-Aslami: Abu Bakr said a word that angered him; Rabi'a asked him to take it back, Abu Bakr refused, Rabi'a complained to the Prophet, and the Prophet rebuked him and told him to ask God's forgiveness for Abu Bakr. The word itself is not written, because it is unclear in the transcript. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:14:30} (needs_source: the story as the speaker tells it)
- He says the Prophet was sixty-three at this time (the speaker's figure for the Prophet's age in his last illness), and that Abu Bakr's love shows most clearly at the end of the Prophet's life. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:15:30} (needs_source)
- He relates that a woman came to the Prophet during his illness and asked him something, and that he told her that if she did not find him she should go to Abu Bakr. He reads this report as a sign that the Prophet was pointing to Abu Bakr for the caliphate; this is the speaker's reading, and other readings of the report are not set out in this episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:16:00} (needs_source: the report and the speaker's reading of it)
- The speaker recites the verse of the completion of religion from the Farewell Pilgrimage, calls it among the most beautiful verses, and relates that a Jewish man once told Umar that they would have made the day of its revelation a festival; Umar answered that it was revealed on the day of Arafah, on a Friday, so it was two festivals. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:17:00} [[passage/quran-5-3|Surat al-Ma'ida, verse 3]] (needs_source: the Jewish man's remark and Umar's answer as the speaker tells them)
- He relates that Abu Bakr wept on hearing this verse and said it was the announcement of the Prophet's death, since the completion of the message meant its end drew near. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:17:30} (needs_source: Abu Bakr's words as the speaker relates them)
- He describes the Prophet's illness in Aisha's house, the Muslims gathering in the mosque, and the Prophet's last sermon from the pulpit, in which he is said to have urged care for prayer and for women, and warned against competition for worldly things. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:19:30} (needs_source: the sermon as the speaker gives it, in substance and not in wording)
- The speaker says that the Prophet offered a servant the choice between this world and meeting God, that the servant chose meeting God, and that the servant was Abu Bakr; he says that Abu Bakr alone understood this and wept. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:19:30} (needs_source: the identification of the servant is the speaker's reading)
- He relates that the Prophet said no one had done him a kindness without his repaying it, except Abu Bakr, whose kindness God would repay for him, and that all the doors of the Companions' houses opening onto the mosque were closed except Abu Bakr's door. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:21:30} (needs_source)
- He relates the Prophet's last greeting to the Muslims: that he sends his greetings to all who follow him until the Day of Judgement. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:22:00} (needs_source)
- He relates that the Prophet ordered Abu Bakr to lead the people in prayer; that Umar led the prayer first; that the Prophet objected that Abu Bakr should lead; and that when the Prophet saw the Muslims praying behind Abu Bakr he smiled, and the speaker says that this was the Prophet's last smile, for the prayer. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:24:00} (needs_source: the order of the prayers and the Prophet's words as the speaker relates them)
- He relates, on Aisha's account, that the Prophet died with his head resting on her chest, and that she did not know what to do and went out to tell the Muslims. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:25:30} (needs_source: Aisha's account as the speaker relates it)
- He describes the grief of the Muslims, of Ali and Uthman, and of the Prophet's daughter Fatima (the name is unclear in the transcript); he relates that Umar raised his sword and said that whoever said the Prophet had died would have his head cut off, and that the Prophet had gone to his Lord as Moses did and would return. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:26:30} (needs_source: the speaker's narrative)
- He relates that Abu Bakr entered, uncovered the Prophet, kissed him between the eyes, and said that he was good in life and good in death, and then went out to the people. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:27:00} (needs_source)
- He relates Abu Bakr's address to the people: whoever worshipped Muhammad, Muhammad has died; whoever worshipped God, God is living and does not die. In the same speech Abu Bakr recites a verse of the Qur'an, and the speaker says the people had not heard it understood in that sense. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:28:30} [[passage/quran-3-144|Surat Al 'Imran, verse 144]] (needs_source: the address as the speaker relates it)
- The speaker says that Umar, on hearing the verse, lost his voice and felt as if he were hearing it for the first time. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:29:00} (needs_source)
- The speaker's reflection: he imagines the Muslims in confusion had Abu Bakr not been there, and holds that the nation needed someone to stand up for it on that day. This is his reflection. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:30:00} (the speaker's view)
- The speaker's view: Abu Bakr grieved deeply, but held back his grief because the whole nation needed someone to stand firm, and he presents this as a lesson on bearing responsibility in calamity. (the speaker's view) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:31:00}
- The speaker's view: the Companions loved the Prophet for his person and character, not only because he was the Messenger, and he describes him as modest, gentle and generous. (the speaker's view) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:31:30}
- He cites God's words on the Prophet as a model for believers and calls for following him in character and conduct, not only in commands and prohibitions. (the speaker's view) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:32:00} [[passage/quran-33-21|Surat al-Ahzab, verse 21]]
- He relates a narration from Abu Bakr that prophets are buried where they die, and that the Prophet was buried in the place where he died, in Aisha's house. The narration is given in substance, not in wording. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:33:00} (status: tradition; needs_source)
- He relates the gathering of the Ansar in the Saqifa of Banu Sa'ida, and that Sa'd ibn Ubada, whom the speaker calls a leader of his people, was the candidate they agreed upon. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:34:00} (needs_source: the Saqifa account as the speaker gives it)
- He relates that Abu Bakr, Umar and Abu Ubayda went to the Saqifa, and that Abu Bakr told the Ansar that the Arabs would accept this matter only for a man of Quraysh; when one of them proposed a leader from each side, Abu Bakr did not accept it. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:35:30} (needs_source: Abu Bakr's argument as the speaker relates it)
- He relates that Bashir ibn Sa'd of the Ansar pledged to Abu Bakr, that Umar pledged after him, and that the people then pledged. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:36:30} (needs_source)
- The speaker's view: he describes the choice of the caliph as a form of consultation and consent, which he compares to modern rule by consent, and holds that a soul joined to God accepts such a choice with ease. (the speaker's view) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:37:00}
- The speaker lists, in his view, Abu Bakr's qualifications for the caliphate: bearing responsibility in the hardest moments; the Prophet's order that he lead the prayer; being the first man to believe; being the Prophet's companion in the cave and on the Hijra; and being the eldest of the Companions in that period (the speaker's figures for his age are not checked). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:39:00} (needs_source: the qualifications are the speaker's weighing; the ages need a source)
- The speaker says that the Prophet respected the opinions of the people and let them speak freely on matters, and presents this as the building of character; this is his view. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:40:00} (the speaker's view)
- He reads from a written text the first sermon of Abu Bakr as caliph, "O people, I have been given authority over you and I am not the best of you"; he asks the people to help him when he is right and correct him when he is wrong, and says that the weak among them is strong with him until he gives him his right and the strong is weak with him until he takes back what is due. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:42:00} (needs_source: the speaker reads from a written copy, which has not been checked against an independent text)
- He says the sermon ends with a call to striving in God's way, and that a people who abandon it are humbled, as the speaker understands the sermon. (needs_source: the speaker's gloss on the sermon) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:42:30}
- He describes Abu Bakr's gentleness with children, widows and orphans: children clung to his robe and he carried them; he milked the ewes of widows and of the widows of martyrs with his own hands; when he became caliph, the women said he would no longer milk for them, and he said he would milk for them until he died. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:44:00} (needs_source: the speaker's narrative)
- He summarises the caliphate: the Arabian peninsula returned to Islam, the conquests began in his time, and the Qur'an was collected, in a period which the speaker counts as two and a half years. This is the speaker's summary and its dates have not been checked against sources in this episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:46:00} (needs_source: the speaker's summary and its dates)
- He relates that Umar advised Abu Bakr not to fight those who withheld zakat, that Abu Bakr refused, and said that if they withheld even a young goat they used to give to the Messenger he would fight them for it, and that religion should not be diminished while he was alive. The account is given as the speaker tells it; other readings of the wars of ridda are not set out in this episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:48:00} (needs_source: the speaker's account; see [[event/ridda-wars|the Ridda wars]])
- The speaker closes with a prayer that God grant his listeners love of the faith like Abu Bakr's, sincerity like his and truthfulness like his, and he announces that the next episode will treat Abu Bakr's caliphate and his death. (the speaker's view) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep03 00:49:00}

## Left out of this episode 3

Not written from episode 3: a reference at the start to the postponement of the two previous episodes because of events in Jerusalem; passages about the people of Palestine and of Egypt and the present; calls for support in the present, since they concern a contemporary conflict outside the subject of the series and are listed for review in the lane ledger; a remark on the number of words people use on the phone today, which concerns the present; a dispute between Abu Bakr and Aisha in one story, part of which is unclear in the transcript, so no judgement from it is written; a story about a grant of land to two men, because it is not clear who gave it and who tore up the paper; the word the speaker calls offensive in the Rabi'a story, which is unclear; the speaker's figures for Abu Bakr's age at death, which differ from episode 1 (sixty-one here, sixty-three in episode 1), so neither figure is written until checked and the difference is logged for the founder; the Qur'an verses the speaker recites that are not clearly named, which are not typed; the name and age of a young member of the audience, who is a private person and is not named; and repetition loops, which are not speech and are not used.

## Episode 4: Abu Bakr's caliphate, the wars of apostasy and his death

- The speaker sets the episode as the last of the Abu Bakr series, covering his caliphate up to his death. He recalls that the three earlier episodes described him, his conduct with the Prophet, and the Hijra, and that the Prophet's death and the start of his caliphate were carried into this one. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:02:00}
- He states his own assessment: after the prophets, he holds Abu Bakr to be the greatest of God's creation, and the steadiest; he says that this is his personal view and that he is relaying what the sources give. (the speaker's view, opinion) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:03:00}
- He states the aim of the programme as he sees it: the return of the community to attachment to the Prophet and his Companions as models. (the speaker's view) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:04:00}
- He recounts the scene at the Prophet's death as an account: Umar is said to have drawn his sword and declared that whoever said the Prophet had died would have his head cut off; Abu Bakr is said to have spoken to the people that whoever worshipped Muhammad should know that Muhammad had died, and whoever worshipped God should know that God lives and does not die. The speaker sets out the principle that the message continues after the Prophet. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:05:30} (needs_source: the speaker's narrative of the scene; the Qur'an verse he recites there is not typed)
- He says that Bilal tried to call the adhan and could not complete the testimony that Muhammad is the messenger of God, and that the Companions wept. (needs_source: the speaker's narrative) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:05:00}
- He relates that Umar, hearing the verse about the messengers who passed before the Prophet, said that he was hearing it for the first time, and that Abu Bakr was the one who steadied the community. (needs_source: the speaker's account of Umar's reaction) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:06:30}
- He describes Abu Bakr's first address after taking the oath of allegiance: he said that he had been placed over them though he was not the best of them; if they saw him right they should help him, and if they saw him wrong they should correct him; that obedience is due to him only as long as he obeys God among them; and that the strong among them is weak to him until he restores the right from him, and the weak is strong to him until he restores the right to him. (needs_source: the wording of the address, read from a machine transcript) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:08:00}
- He sums up the address as three principles: consultation, justice, and striving in God's way. He treats them as the foundation of any caliphate. (the speaker's gloss) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:09:00}
- He describes the wave of revolt after the Prophet's death as the speaker tells it: some tribes withheld zakat, some refused to pray, some abandoned the religion, and several men claimed prophethood, among them Musaylima in Yamama and al-Aswad al-Ansi in Yemen. He says that only Makkah and Madinah remained firm. (needs_source: the list of revolts and the names, read from a machine transcript) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:10:30}
- He says that in Makkah Suhayl ibn Amr, an orator of the Quraysh, held the people firm, and that Umar had once asked the Prophet to let him pull out the teeth of Suhayl, and the Prophet refused. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:11:30}
- He recounts the expedition of Usama ibn Zayd, which the Prophet had ordered shortly before his death, and the disagreement over whether to send it while Madinah was threatened. He says that Usama, about eighteen years old, went to Umar and asked him to speak to Abu Bakr about leaving the city. (needs_source: the age and the sequence are the speaker's account) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:16:00}
- He says that Umar and others urged Abu Bakr to replace Usama as commander, because of his young age, and that Abu Bakr refused. He reports Abu Bakr's statement that he would carry out what the Prophet had ordered even if birds snatched and beasts roamed around Madinah. (needs_source: the speaker's account) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:19:00}
- He says that the army left, that the tribes which had planned to attack Madinah turned back on hearing of it, and that the army returned after a short campaign on the frontier of Syria, victorious. The place he names at one point is Palestine; the place is not set here (see the left-out list). (needs_source: the expedition's route and outcome) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:23:00}
- He describes Abu Bakr's stand on zakat: Umar asked him to deal gently with the tribes, and Abu Bakr refused, saying that he would fight anyone who separated prayer from zakat, and that religion should not be diminished while he is alive. He says that Umar then saw the wisdom of the decision. (the speaker's account and view) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:26:00}
- He states the principle behind it in his own words: Islam is a whole system that covers worship and also economy, society, family and the unity of the community, and it is not to be taken in parts. This is his reading of Abu Bakr's position, not a museum judgement. (the speaker's view) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:28:00}
- He recounts that Abu Bakr set guards at the approaches of Madinah when delegations and armies were reported to be approaching, and gathered the Companions in the mosque. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:28:00}
- He reports that a force reached Dhu al-Qissa near Madinah, that Abu Bakr went out himself, and that the enemy were defeated after a night attack; he says that the enemy frightened the Muslim camels with water-skins set alight, and that the Muslims retreated until the night attack. (needs_source: the battle's details are the speaker's narrative; see the place name, Dhu al-Qissa, read from a machine transcript) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:32:00}
- He lists the commanders sent out across Arabia within about two years, as his account: Khalid ibn al-Walid to Buzakha, Tulayha, al-Butah and Malik ibn Nuwayra, then to Yamama against Musaylima; Ikrima ibn Abi Jahl and Shurahbil ibn Hasana toward Yamama; Amr ibn al-As toward Tabuk and Dumat al-Jandal; al-Ala ibn al-Hadrami to Bahrain; Hudhayfa ibn Mihsan to Oman; Muhajir ibn Abi Umayya and Suwayd ibn Muqarrin to Yemen. Names that are unclear in the transcript are not set down. (needs_source: each name and route, read from a machine transcript) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:35:30}
- He summarises the caliphate: the Arabian peninsula returned to Islam, zakat was restored, armies were sent to Iraq and to Syria, and the Qur'an was first collected in his time under the direction of Zayd ibn Thabit and Umar. This is the speaker's summary and its dates have not been checked against sources in this episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:37:30} (needs_source: the summary and its dates)
- He tells that Abu Bakr went each week to the orphan girls of Madinah to milk their ewes for them, that when he became caliph they said he would no longer come, and that he replied that he would milk for them until he died. (needs_source: the speaker's anecdote) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:38:00}
- He tells that children held Abu Bakr's robe in the street and called him father, and that Umar was said to kiss Abu Bakr's head and to say that without him they would have been lost. (needs_source: the speaker's account of what he was told) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:38:30}
- He recounts Abu Bakr's illness: he is said to have fallen ill after a cold bath and to have suffered for fifteen days. (needs_source: the speaker's account of the cause and the duration) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:42:00}
- He recounts how Abu Bakr asked Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf and others about Umar. Abd al-Rahman is said to have answered that Umar was better than what Abu Bakr thought of him, and Uthman that Umar's inner nature was better than his outward one. The people, he says, praised Umar. (needs_source: the speaker's account of the consultation) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:44:30}
- He says that Abu Bakr had Uthman write the appointment of Umar, then fainted before the name was written, and that Uthman wrote Umar's name after he recovered. Abu Bakr then told the people to hear and obey Umar, and that if Umar changed, the fault would not be his. (needs_source: the account of the written instruction) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:47:00}

:::perspectives
- **The speaker's answer to the charge that Abu Bakr imposed Umar by force (the speaker's view):** the speaker says the charge is not accurate, that Abu Bakr did consult, and that the consultation was narrower than the common picture of a shura of all the people; he presents Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf's and Uthman's replies as evidence of the people's trust in Umar. This is his reading. The museum does not decide between accounts of the succession here, and the Saqifa account given in episode 3 remains his too. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:44:00}
- **The report of poisoning (report, not affirmed):** the speaker reports that some say Abu Bakr died of poison and others say not; he gives both and does not decide between them in the transcript. The claim is recorded only as a report. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:42:00}
:::

He closes the account with Abu Bakr's last days: he is said to have died in Madinah on a Monday night, to have asked on the day of his death that God let him die that night, and to have been buried at night. Umar led the funeral prayer; Ali ibn Abi Talib and Abu Ubayda lowered him into the grave. Ali's words at the grave, as the speaker gives them, praised Abu Bakr as the first to accept Islam, the most sincere in faith, and the closest to the Prophet in character. (needs_source: the death day, the prayer, the burial and the eulogy wording) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:52:00}
- He reports Abu Bakr's last words as a prayer to die as a Muslim and to be joined to the righteous. The verse that Aisha recited at his bedside is not typed here. (the speaker's account; the Qur'an text is not written) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:50:30}
- He reports Ali's remarks at the grave about Abu Bakr's steadfastness, his support of the Prophet when others withheld it, and his strength in God's cause. (needs_source: the speaker's rendering of Ali's words) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep04 00:53:30}

## Left out of this episode 4

Not written from episode 4: the opening remarks on the two earlier episodes about Palestine, the letters sent to the people of Palestine, and the calls for support in the present; these concern a contemporary conflict outside the subject of the series and are listed for the founder. The speaker's place name for the Usama expedition, which he gives as Palestine and as a fortress in Gaza, is not written here because the historical route is not set by the transcript; the expedition itself is written. The passage in which the speaker attributes the deaths of several prophets, of Umar and of Uthman to a named group, a polemical claim outside the subject of the series, and the claims that follow it; repetition loops, which are not speech; the passages on the modern day, including the illustration about a religious student and the remarks about how a Muslim's life is judged today; the speaker's figures for Abu Bakr's age at death, which read 63 here while episode 3 gives 61 and episode 1 gives 63, so neither figure is written and the difference is logged for the founder; the Qur'an verses the speaker recites, which are not typed, and the verse of mortality, which he does not name by number so no passage link is added; the Hadith about the weighing of Abu Bakr against the community, which is not written as scripture; the names of commanders that are unclear in the transcript; and the lines that are garbled in the machine transcript, which are not written from.

## Episode 5: Umar ibn al-Khattab before Islam and his conversion

:::perspectives
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he proposes Umar ibn al-Khattab as the figure the twenty-first century most needs, one in whom strength is joined to justice and mercy. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:04:00}
:::

- The speaker proposes Umar as the figure most suited to the present age, because strength, justice and mercy are joined in him. This is the speaker's view. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:04:00}
- He gives Umar's lineage as Umar ibn al-Khattab ibn Nufayl ibn Abd al-Uzza ibn Ka'b ibn Lu'ayy, and says he meets the Prophet at Ka'b ibn Lu'ayy, the fourth ancestor. (needs_source: the names are read from a partly garbled line) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:05:00}
- The speaker says Umar was responsible for the office of envoy among Quraysh, handling disputes between Quraysh and other tribes, and that Quraysh trusted his judgement. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:06:00}
- He relates a well-known anecdote from Umar's time before Islam: Umar made an idol from dates, worshipped it until he grew hungry, and ate it. The speaker presents it as an example of what Islam changed. This is a traditional anecdote given without a source in the episode. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:08:00}
- The speaker says the mission began when Umar was twenty-seven and that Umar accepted Islam at thirty-two. These are the speaker's own calculations. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:12:30}
- He names Abu Jahl as Umar's maternal uncle and Khalid ibn al-Walid as his cousin. The names are read from lines that are not clear in the transcript. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:14:30}
- He describes Abdullah ibn Umar as the companion who most closely followed the Prophet's conduct, and says Umar gave three of his sons the same name, Abd al-Rahman. The details of the names are garbled in the transcript. (speaker's view and account; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:15:30}
- He relates that Umar asked Ali for the hand of a daughter of Ali, and that Ali agreed; Umar then defended the marriage before the people in the mosque. The transcript names the daughter as Umm Kulthum, and the name needs a source. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:18:30}
- The speaker gives two explanations of the kunya Abu Hafs: one through the lion cub, the other through his daughter Hafsa. The second line is clear enough to record only as his account. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:22:00}
- He relates that the title Amir al-Mu'minin arose when a delegation from Iraq, including Adi ibn Hatim al-Ta'i, came to Medina, and he notes the difficulty of the title Caliph of the Caliph of the Messenger of God. This is the speaker's account. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:25:00}
- He describes Umar as tall, fair with some redness, and bald, and says that his qualities are also mentioned in the Torah, without naming the passage. The description is his own. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:29:30}
- He relates that Umar's sister Fatima and her husband Said ibn Zayd had accepted Islam. Umar struck his sister when she would not give up her new faith, and she asked him whether the truth could lie outside his religion. The episode presents this as a traditional narrative. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:41:00}
- The speaker relates that Umar, after reading a surah he names as Surah Taha, went to the Prophet at the house of al-Arqam, and that the Muslims' cry of takbir was heard in Mecca. The verses are not written here. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:45:00}
- The speaker closes by asking the audience who Umar ibn al-Khattab was, and announces the next episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep05 00:51:00}

## Left out of this episode 5

Not written from episode 5: a comparison of present-day religious conduct and an aside on other faiths (00:03:00 to 00:03:30), a remark on how a learned man should be addressed by his title (00:24:00), and a line in which the speaker describes himself with a modern political label, which is also garbled (00:49:00). These are logged for the founder as contemporary asides. The Qur'an verses recited and one line quoted from the Qur'an (00:34:30, 00:42:00 to 00:42:30) are not typed, and no passage link is added because the verses are not clearly named. A saying of the Prophet about kinship (00:19:00) and a supplication (00:30:30 to 00:31:00) are given in wording that is not reproduced. The speaker's age for Abu Bakr at death, given as sixty-two at 00:12:00, differs from the figure in episodes 1 and 4 and is logged for the founder. Repetition loops at 00:13:30 and 00:48:30 are not speech and are discarded.

## Episode 6: Umar's open emigration, Badr, Hudaybiyya and Mecca

:::perspectives
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he reads Umar's conduct through one thread, a jealous zeal for Islam that governs his deeds from his conversion to the Prophet's death, and proposes it as a model for believers to follow in faith and character. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:03:00}
:::

- The speaker states the thread of the episode: Umar's zeal for Islam, which he takes to explain the stands Umar took from his conversion until the Prophet's death. This is the speaker's view. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:03:00}
- He says that, of the Prophet's companions who emigrated to Medina, Umar alone left openly, and that Ali ibn Abi Talib described it so. This is the speaker's account of the tradition. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:06:30}
- He explains the Prophet's secret departure as a lesson to the community that a weak people should not expose itself, and Umar's open departure as a personal stand that the speaker says Umar later moderated as ruler, leading the prayer lightly for others. This interpretation is the speaker's. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:08:00}
- He relates that Umar armed himself, went to the Kaaba in view of the Quraysh, circled it seven times, and prayed two cycles behind the station of Ibrahim. The account is traditional and is given without a source in the episode. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:09:30}
- He reports Abdullah ibn Mas'ud's statement that the Muslims could not pray at the Kaaba until Umar accepted Islam. This is a companion report given by the speaker. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:10:30}
- He describes Umar going round the Quraysh gatherings and telling them that their faces would be humbled, and challenging anyone who wished to leave his child fatherless or his wife widowed to meet him beyond the valley, since he was emigrating the next day. No one moved to stop him. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:11:30}
- He recounts that Ayyash ibn Abi Rabi'a, who had emigrated with Umar, was turned back near Medina by his brothers, al-Harith and Abu Jahl, who argued that their mother had sworn an oath about him. The names of the brothers are read from a partly garbled line. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:15:00}
- He reports Umar's counsel to Ayyash not to return, and says Umar offered him half of his wealth to stay with the Muslims; Ayyash refused, and Umar gave him his she-camel. The offer of wealth is the speaker's account. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:17:00}
- He relates that Umar then wrote to Ayyash a passage of divine mercy toward those who have wronged themselves, which the speaker says Ayyash could not understand at first, and then did. The passage is not written here. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:18:30}
- The speaker draws the lesson that the door of return stays open to anyone who has fallen into wrongdoing. This is his exhortation, not a ruling. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:19:00}
- At Badr, he says, Umar sought out his own kin in the battle, and that Umar killed his maternal uncle; the uncle's name is garbled in the transcript. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:21:00}
- He relates an exchange with Said ibn al-As, whose father died an unbeliever at Badr, who thought Umar had killed his father; Umar answered that he had killed only his own uncle. Said is said to have replied that a man who had killed would not apologise. This is the speaker's account. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:22:30}
- He relates that Suhayl ibn Amr, a Quraysh orator who had urged war at Badr, was captured; Umar asked the Prophet's leave to remove his teeth, and the Prophet refused to permit mutilation of anyone. The Prophet's reply is given here in summary only. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:24:00}
- He says that after the Prophet's death Suhayl stood firm among the people of Mecca and that Umar wept at the report of it. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:25:00}
- He describes the Badr captives: Abu Bakr advised ransom out of kinship and Umar advised killing the captives, and the speaker says the Prophet weighed the two views. The Prophet's reply is garbled in the transcript and is not written. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:26:00}
- He relates the conversion of Umayr ibn Wahb, whose son was a captive of the Muslims, after a plot with Safwan ibn Umayya at the Kaaba, and says that Umar seized Umayr and brought him to the Prophet, who asked him to speak the truth. Umayr accepted Islam, and Umar is said to have loved him more than any of his own children. The figures of the plot and the speaker's last sentence are garbled in parts. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:33:00}
- At Uhud, he says, Abu Sufyan called out from below the mountain, after rumours that the Prophet, Abu Bakr and Umar had been killed. The Prophet told the Muslims not to answer. Abu Sufyan then claimed victory and the Muslims answered that their dead are in Paradise and his are in the Fire. Abu Sufyan replied that the three men were alive, and he recognised Umar's voice. This is the speaker's account. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:38:00}
- At Hudaybiyya, he relates that Umar objected to the terms of the treaty, that Abu Bakr accepted the Prophet's decision, and that Umar afterwards gave much time to prayer, fasting and charity as atonement. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:46:30}
- He describes Abu Jandal ibn Suhayl, who had embraced Islam and came to the Muslims in chains. Suhayl asked that he be returned under the treaty, and the Prophet asked Abu Jandal to be patient. Umar moved toward Abu Jandal with a sword and urged him to kill his father; Abu Jandal refused and the speaker reports Umar's later regret. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:45:30}
- At the conquest of Mecca, Abu Sufyan sought protection through Abu Bakr and Uthman, and then Umar, who refused him and called him the one who had broken the covenant. Abbas took Abu Sufyan to the Prophet, and Umar objected at the gate to Abbas's protection and to the seizure of Abu Sufyan's head. The speaker presents Abbas's reply as a rebuke of Umar's tribal reasoning. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:50:00}
- Umar's reply to Abbas, as the speaker gives it, is that Abbas's Islam gave the Prophet more joy than his own father's would have. The speaker presents this as a sign of Umar's love for the Prophet. This is the speaker's account. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep06 00:50:30}

## Left out of this episode 6

Not written from episode 6: a passage on Palestine and calls for support in the present (00:03:00 to 00:03:30), a remark on the present conflict and on terrorism introduced in the Hudaybiyya passage (00:40:30 to 00:41:00), and a contemporary remark on media and dress (00:09:00 to 00:09:30 and 00:25:00 to 00:25:30). These are logged for the founder as contemporary asides. Qur'an verses recited or quoted by the speaker (00:10:00 to 00:11:00, 00:17:30 to 00:18:00, 00:27:30 to 00:28:00, 00:52:30 to 00:53:00) are not typed, and no passage link is added because the speaker does not name each verse by number. Words attributed to the Prophet about the hearts of Abu Bakr and Umar (00:26:30 to 00:28:30) are not reproduced as scripture. A line Umar is said to have spoken about a pig (00:33:00 to 00:33:30) is garbled and is not written. A slogan of the Quraysh at Uhud (00:36:00 to 00:37:00) is not reproduced. Repetition loops (00:28:30 to 00:29:00, 00:30:30 to 00:31:00) are not speech and are discarded. The age and the Badr figures are not given where the transcript is unclear.

## Episode 7: Umar's love for God and for the Prophet, the agreements with the revealed text, and the well of Aris

:::perspectives
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he reads this episode through love: love of God that shows in weeping and in deeds, and love of the Prophet that puts the Prophet above one's own self, children and wealth. He offers Umar's conduct as a training for the hearts of his listeners. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:03:30}
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he takes the times when Umar's words matched a revealed text as a sign of Umar's closeness to God, and cites a tradition about inspired people in earlier nations. He gives the scholars' explanation that such figures were needed before the religion was completed for this community, which he says is no longer in that need. These are his views; the tradition is cited to the narration as he gives it. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:09:30}
:::

- The speaker opens by saying that the earlier episodes covered Umar's conversion and his stands with the Prophet, and that this one is built on love: love of God and love of the Prophet. This is the speaker's framing. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:01:30}
- He says Umar's fear and love of God showed in his weeping, and he speaks of dark marks under Umar's eyes from weeping out of fear of God. This is the speaker's account (status: tradition; needs_source). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:04:00}
- He tells of a man who had done wrong and whom Umar threatened with a stick. The man was reminded of God, and Umar said he could not harm him after those words. This is the speaker's narrative (status: tradition; needs_source). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:05:30}
- He says that the heavens rejoiced at Umar's Islam, and that Umar's words came to match the revealed text on several occasions. This is his account. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:06:00}
- He gives three occasions he calls agreements (muwafaqat): Umar's suggestion that the Station of Abraham be used as a place of prayer; his suggestion that the Prophet's wives be veiled when speaking to people; and the revelation on the creation of man, where Umar is said to have completed the words the Prophet was reciting. The verses are not typed here. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:10:30}
- He reports the tradition that among the nations before there were inspired people, and that if there is one in this community it is Umar. The scholars, as he gives them, explain that the earlier nations needed such people, while this community's religion was completed. The tradition is cited to his narration. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:09:30}
- He tells of the prohibition of wine in stages. Umar is said to have asked God twice for a clear ruling, and to have repeated "we have ended" when the final prohibition came; the speaker says the streets of Medina were full of spilled wine. The verses are not typed. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:12:30}
- He tells that Umar asked God to bar uninvited entry at midday, when his clothing had shifted; the speaker presents this as a sign of Umar's sensitivity to the revelation. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:13:00}
- He tells of a Jewish man who said that a verse on the completion of the religion would have made his people take its day as a feast. Umar answered that the day of its revelation was already a feast for the believers. The verse is not typed. (speaker's account; status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:14:00}
- He tells that Umar wept at a verse about the foremost among the earlier and the later believers, and then laughed and said "all praise is to God" when another verse gave the balance. The verses are not typed. (speaker's account; status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:15:00}
- He cites Ibn Umar's report of his father: the Prophet took Umar's hand as they walked, and Umar spoke of his love for the Prophet unprompted. The Prophet asked whether Umar loved him more than his sons, family, wealth and self; Umar said yes to each until the last, where he said no, and then that the Prophet was dearer to him than his own self. The Prophet told him that now his faith was complete. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:18:00}
- The speaker tells how Ibn Umar was moved by his father's sudden change of heart, and that Umar explained his own reasoning: he had been in darkness, and the Prophet had been the means of his guidance, so he found he needed the Prophet more than his self. This reflection is the speaker's (status: tradition). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:19:00}
- He reports that Umar, on the Islam of the Prophet's uncle Abbas, said he was happier at Abbas's Islam than he would have been at his own father's, and that the Prophet rejoiced even more. This is the speaker's account (status: tradition; needs_source). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:20:30}
- He tells that Umar asked the Prophet's leave for an umrah, and the Prophet told him not to forget him in his prayers there. The speaker uses this to show the Prophet's humility, and Umar's answer to his son that he never forgets such a moment. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:21:30}
- He tells that Umar was troubled by the Prophet's displeasure with his wives, and spoke to his daughter Hafsa about it. The passage is partly garbled in the transcript, so the details are not written and the account is marked for a listening check. (status: needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:23:00}
- He tells that Umar and an Ansari neighbour took turns to work an orchard at al-Awali and to be with the Prophet in Medina. This is the speaker's narration (status: tradition; needs_source). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:25:00}
- On his turn in the orchard, Umar heard news that the Prophet had withdrawn from his wives, and went to the mosque. There he was told that the Prophet had gone into a room with Bilal guarding it, and that no one knew whether he had divorced them. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:26:30}
- He tells that Umar asked Bilal several times to admit him. When he entered, he found the Prophet lying on a mat that had marked his side, and said that the rulers of Byzantium and Persia enjoyed wealth while the Prophet slept on a mat. The Prophet asked whether he was in doubt, and then cautioned him against rejoicing in the swift worldly reward of others. The caution is paraphrased, not reproduced. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:29:00}
- Umar asked whether the Prophet had divorced his wives, and the Prophet said no; Umar then said "praise be to God".
