The speaker tells of Umar's remark on the Ansari women who answer their husbands, which made the Prophet smile. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:30:30}
- The speaker relates the report of Abu Musa al-Ashari at the well of Aris, where the Prophet sat with his legs in the water. Umar wanted to guard the gate; Abu Bakr came and was admitted, then Umar, then Uthman, and each was given the good news of paradise, with Ali coming last. The speaker presents it as a narration (status: tradition; needs_source). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:34:00}
- He cites the Prophet holding Abu Bakr's hand and Umar's hand and saying that they would be raised on the Day of Resurrection in this way. The companions, he says, used to name Abu Bakr and Umar as the best after the Prophet while he listened. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:36:30}
- The Prophet asked a companion whom he most loved; he named Aisha, then, asked about men, Aisha's father Abu Bakr, then Umar. The speaker quotes Hasan al-Basri's image of Islam coming to each person on the Day of Resurrection and reaching Umar. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:38:30}
- He reports the Prophet's dreams: a palace in paradise, which was said to belong to Umar, and a woman performing ablution beside another palace, also said to be Umar's, which Umar is said to have feared to enter because of his jealousy. He also reports the dream of the shirts, in which Umar's shirt dragged behind him; the Prophet interpreted the shirt as religion. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:42:30}
- The speaker relates that the Prophet said God places the truth on Umar's tongue and in his heart. The point is paraphrased, not reproduced as wording. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:43:30}
- He tells that Hudhayfa, who kept the Prophet's secrets about the trials to come, told Umar that a closed door stood between Umar and the trials. Umar asked whether the door would be broken or opened; Hudhayfa said it would be broken, and that the door was Umar. The speaker adds that after Umar's death a woman in Medina cried out that the door had been opened. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:47:00}
- He tells that women's voices were raised in the Prophet's presence, and they hid when Umar entered; the Prophet laughed, and Umar said the Prophet had more right to be feared. The speaker reports the Prophet's remark that Satan flees from Umar's path. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:49:30}
- The speaker's view (opinion): Satan avoids a person whose path leads to many good deeds, because each whisper would add to that person's reward, so Umar's strength against Satan is a model for believers. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:50:00}
- He reports the Prophet's dream of the weighing: the Prophet outweighed the community, then Abu Bakr outweighed it, and then Umar outweighed it. This is the speaker's account of a dream (status: tradition; needs_source). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:51:00}
- The speaker closes by asking God for sincerity for his listeners and says the next episode will look at Umar's strength. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep07 00:51:30}

## Left out of this episode 7

Not written from episode 7: a passage on Palestine and the captive al-Aqsa mosque (00:32:00), and a call to pray in Jerusalem and to free al-Aqsa (00:40:30). Both are modern-conflict asides outside the subject of the series and are logged for the founder. Contemporary remarks on the month of Ramadan just past and on present hearts (00:02:30 to 00:03:00), on addressing listeners who live for Islam (00:41:00), on the naming of people in the twenty-first century (00:34:30), and on protecting a company, a college and a work section from trials (00:46:30 to 00:47:00) are logged as contemporary asides. Qur'an verses recited or quoted by the speaker are not typed, and no passage link is added because the speaker does not give their numbers: the verse of love and following (00:03:30), the verse on the punishment of the Lord (00:04:00), the verse of mercy (00:05:00), the verse on the Station of Abraham (00:06:30), the verse on veiling (00:07:30), the verses on creation (00:10:00 to 00:10:30), the verses on wine (00:11:00 to 00:12:00), the verse on permission to enter (00:13:00 to 00:13:30), the verse on the completion of the religion (00:13:30 to 00:14:00), the verses on the foremost (00:14:30 to 00:15:00) and the verse on divorce quoted by Umar (00:23:30 to 00:24:00). Prophetic sayings are paraphrased or left out and are not reproduced as wording: the sayings on muhaddathun (00:08:00), on faith and love of the Prophet (00:17:30), on worldly reward (00:29:00), on truth on Umar's tongue (00:43:30) and on the weighing (00:50:30). Garbled lines are not written: a report about a companion named Nu'aym and a battle called Dhat al-Thawabin (00:36:30 to 00:37:00). Repetition loops or non-speech are discarded and are not exclusions of content; none was clearly identified in this episode. The ages and figures of the episode are not given where the transcript is unclear.

## Episode 8: Umar's first decade as caliph: government, the army, the treasury and the conquests

:::perspectives
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he presents the caliphate of Umar, which he gives as ten years, six months and four days, as a period of achievements in every field of public life, and offers these as a model for Muslims. He treats the episode as practical, a list of what Umar did, rather than as a lesson in devotion alone. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:03:30}
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he says that a religious person is not defined by one fixed manner, and that firmness is right where a situation calls for it while gentleness is right where it calls for gentleness. He presents Umar's conduct as an example of both. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:16:00}
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he states as a principle that a rule without justice cannot last. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:15:00}
- **The speaker's view (opinion):** he says that choosing the Hijra as the starting point of the calendar shows Umar's view of it as the founding of the state, rather than a birth or a personal event. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:22:00}
- **Contested and polemical, labelled:** the speaker's account of the Jews of Khaybar, of the expulsion of the People of the Book from the Arabian Peninsula, and of the tax on the People of the Book, is recorded as his narrative and not as a museum judgement. The speaker attributes plotting to the Jews of Khaybar; this museum does not reproduce that generalisation about a group and records only the narrative of the events, which needs an independent account before any historical claim is made. The speaker's statement that religions lived in peace only under Islam is his view, and other accounts, including scholarly ones, are not set out in this episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:40:30}
:::

- The speaker says the series has run for more than a year, that it opened with the companions and the ten promised paradise, and that Umar has taken three episodes so far. This episode begins the account of his caliphate, its achievements and its government; his character and his death are reserved for later episodes. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:03:00}
- He gives the length of Umar's caliphate as ten years, six months and four days. This is his figure and is recorded as his. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:02:30}
- He describes Abu Bakr's last illness and the question of his successor. Abu Bakr asked whom the Muslims should be given, and the speaker names Umar as the closest in judgement and in the Prophet's sayings. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:04:00}
- He reports the saying that the Prophet, entering the mosque, placed Abu Bakr on his right and Umar on his left, and the speaker takes it as a sign of their standing. The wording is paraphrased, not reproduced. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:04:30}
- He reports the Prophet's question, put to Aisha, on whom he loved most, with the answer Abu Bakr and then Umar. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:06:00}
- He says that when Umar took power people feared him more than they had feared him in the Prophet's time, and that children who had clung to the gentle Abu Bakr now hid from Umar in the streets. This is his narrative. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:07:30}
- He describes Umar's address to the people in the mosque. Umar said he had been the Prophet's servant and helper and then Abu Bakr's, that he had set his harshness to the Prophet's gentleness, and that he would stay as an unsheathed sword in the service of what is right. The speaker reports this as Umar's words; the wording is paraphrased. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:09:30}
- Umar is said to have promised that he would be severe with those who wrong the weak and the just, and that he would place his cheek in the dust before the honest. The speaker says this was the first time the people had heard him speak of how he thought about his rule. The passage is paraphrased. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:11:00}
- He lists four undertakings Umar made to the people: that he would not take their money or that of his family; that he would increase their stipends and their shares; that he would not keep them long on distant campaigns; and that they should correct him if he erred, and enjoin good and forbid evil. The speaker presents this as Umar's programme. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:12:30}
- He says Umar's first public address as caliph was a plan of government and that it displayed the wisdom of a leader who lays down principles of mercy, justice and equality. This is his view. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:14:00}
- He gives the title Commander of the Believers. In his account, a delegation from Iraq led by al-Ahnaf ibn Qays asked to be admitted to the commander of the believers, and Amr ibn al-As greeted Umar with the title, Umar accepted it, and it became his official title. The speaker says this was the first time the title was used by a caliph. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:20:30}
- He says that Umar was the first to set a date for official letters, so that the people could be dated and the administration measured. He says Umar considered the birth of the Prophet, the night journey and the Hijra, and chose the Hijra as the beginning of the state. The account of the choice is the speaker's. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:22:00}
- He says Umar walked the streets of Medina at night to know the conditions of the people, and that he marked houses where he found a problem so as to return by day. The speaker promises stories of these nights for a later episode. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:23:00}
- He tells of a woman who mixed water into milk for sale, while her daughter refused because Umar had forbidden it. The mother said Umar would not see; the daughter answered that God sees. The speaker reports that the daughter was later married into a line from which Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz descended, and he presents the story as an example of sincerity. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:25:00}
- He says Umar held an annual meeting at the Hajj in Mina with his governors and army commanders, to question them and hear their views and to look into their worship and their affairs. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:25:30}
- He says Umar used a small whip for correction, not to harm, and quotes a companion who said its effect was greater than swords. The companion's remark is attributed to the narration. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:26:30}
- He says Umar planned cities. He describes the founding of Kufa, with the mosque at the centre and arrows shot from the centre to mark the boundary, and credits him with the layout of Basra and of Fustat in Egypt. The speaker also gives a method for choosing a site for a hospital, which he states as Umar's; the method is not established and is recorded as his claim. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:28:30}
- He says Umar was the first to plan roads between the Muslim lands, to keep them level and free of steep curves, and he gives the saying that Umar would be asked for a mule that stumbled in Iraq. The saying is paraphrased and not reproduced as wording. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:30:00}
- He tells of Umar's care for animals: a servant brought fish from the coast on a camel, the camel tired on the journey, and Umar refused to eat the fish, saying an animal worn out for his sake would be questioned. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:30:30}
- He says the Prophet prayed the night prayer of Ramadan with the people for some nights and then stopped, out of concern that it might be made obligatory, and that Umar gathered the people to pray the Ramadan night prayer behind one imam. The speaker presents this as the origin of the taraweeh congregation. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:32:00}
- He says Umar fixed the funeral prayer at four takbirs, whereas the Prophet's funeral prayers are reported with different numbers, and that Umar did so so that the community would act together. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:33:00}
- He tells of the widening of the Prophet's mosque. Umar asked the Prophet's uncle Abbas to give up his house for the enlargement; Abbas refused; Umar complained to Ali, who said the house could not be taken without consent. Umar offered to pay, Abbas refused payment, and in the end Abbas gave the house for the sake of the sanctity of Muslims' property and of the mosque. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:34:30}
- He says Umar moved the Station of Abraham back from the Ka'ba so that people could circle the Ka'ba with room. The Qur'an is not cited in this passage. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:35:30}
- He says that when the caliphate came to Umar, the choice was made by a council of six, instead of leaving the choice to everyone. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:36:00}
- The speaker remarks that the list of Umar's undertakings would merit a Nobel prize, if such a prize existed for a single work. This is his rhetorical comparison, not a museum statement. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:36:30}
- He says Umar ruled that no two religions should remain in the Arabian Peninsula. He links this to the Jews of Khaybar, whom the Prophet had left on the land under an agreement to share its produce, and says that, when Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf was counting the harvest to divide it, one of their senior scholars offered him a larger share with a part for Umar, that Abd al-Rahman struck him on the head with his sandal, and that Umar then ordered their expulsion. The accounts of the offer, the blow and the expulsion are the speaker's narrative and need an independent account. (status: tradition; needs_source; sensitivity: contested) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:38:00}
- He says Umar took the poll tax from the People of the Book in tiers according to their means, exempted the poor, the elderly and the disabled, returned what had been taken from them, and paid them from the public treasury. He also says the poll tax was less than the zakat paid by Muslims. The poll tax figure is recorded as his claim. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:40:00}
- He tells that Umar would not pray inside the church in Jerusalem, lest the Muslims later take the church as a mosque, and prayed outside it. The speaker presents this as a sign of respect for other faiths. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:40:30}
- He lists Umar's military measures: the first permanent military camps in Damascus, Palestine and Jordan; conscription; posts on the frontiers; and a rule that no soldier stay away from his wife for more than four months on campaign. He gives a figure of 30,000 horses kept in reserve. The figure is his and is unchecked. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:41:30}
- He reports Umar's letter to Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas, asking to be written to as if Umar could see the army, with news of the troops, of the enemy, and of their worship, since the speaker says victory comes from worship. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:42:30}
- He lists army administration: a register of soldiers with their names and stipends; fixed pay; doctors, judges and teachers attached to the army; and a supply department with food stores. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:43:00}
- He says Umar set up the registers (diwans) and institutions of the state, each with a director and staff. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:44:30}
- He lists further firsts ascribed to Umar: a ministry of supply; the first ministry of endowments; the audit of governors' and officials' wealth, asking where it came from and comparing it with what they had before taking office; a treasury for public funds; coins struck to a set weight (this line is partly unclear and is not written beyond that); zakat levied on horses used for trade; and fixed stipends for the people so that they need not beg. All of these are his list and are unchecked. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:46:00}
- He says Umar was the first to survey land and fix its measure, and gives a figure of 32 million plots. He says the measure called the Umari measure remained in use for about a hundred years after him. The figure is his and is unchecked. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:47:00}
- He says Umar distributed food between the lands, moving grain from regions of plenty to poorer ones; set up rest-houses along the roads for travellers, with food, water and lodging; forbade hoarding of food; and set places for the slaughter of camels and goats. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:48:30}
- He tells that Umar lent money from the public treasury to traders who asked for it, and that the first such loan went to Hind bint Utba, who is named in the narration as the killer of Hamza. (status: tradition; needs_source; listening check at the anchor) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:48:00}
- He lists the conquests in Umar's caliphate: Iraq in full, the Levant in full, Jerusalem and its mosque, Egypt, and Azerbaijan. He says the work of ten years, six months and four days was achieved by these. The list is his summary; each conquest is dated by the historical sources, not by this episode. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep08 00:49:00}

## Left out of this episode 8

Not written from episode 8: a passage on present-day Muslim relations with other civilisations and a contrast with the concept of a clash of civilisations (00:13:30 to 00:14:00); an address to contemporary Western civilisations (00:15:00); a remark on the administrative problems of present-day states (00:43:00 to 00:43:30); and a call to Muslims today to stop imitating other civilisations (00:49:00). These are contemporary asides outside the subject of the series and are logged for the founder. The speaker's generalisation about the habits of the Jews, used to explain the Khaybar plots (00:37:00), is not reproduced; the narrative of the events is kept as his account above. Qur'an passages recited or discussed by the speaker are not typed, and no passage link is added because the verses are not clearly named (the passages on the Station of Abraham at 00:35:00 are described without any verse). Prophetic sayings are paraphrased or left out and are not reproduced as wording. Garbled lines are not written: the anecdote about Umar's daughter and the distance a woman can see (00:44:30), the names of the rest-houses on the Cairo to Alexandria road (00:47:00), and an anecdote about a ruler and a rival at 00:43:00, which the transcript garbles. Repetition loops are discarded and are not exclusions of content (00:05:00, 00:16:30 to 00:17:00, 00:24:30, 00:46:00). The ages and figures not set out clearly in the transcript are not given.


## Episode 9: Umar's strength, justice and mercy together

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He proposes Umar ibn al-Khattab as the one figure he knows who joins strength, justice and mercy in a single character, and he asks listeners to take the three together as a model. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:07:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He reads Umar's strength as strength of the spirit, drawn from faith, worship, remembrance of God and the company of the righteous, and not as bodily strength alone. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:20:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He says that justice is what sustains a people, and he gives as a saying he calls famous, without naming its author in the episode, that a just state can endure even without Muslim rule while an unjust state does not endure even under Muslim rule. (needs_source: the saying is given without its author) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:36:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He presents the account of the Muslim's love of Islam in the story he attributes to al-Hasan al-Basri, in which Islam comes on the Day of Judgment and Umar takes its hand, as an image that should move listeners to make Islam beloved. (needs_source: the image is given without a checked source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:38:00}
:::

- The speaker opens by saying that the episode returns to Umar after two earlier episodes were delayed by a change of venue, and that the series will take three qualities, strength, justice and mercy, one at a time through stories. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:03:30}
- He lists the books he drew on for the episodes on Umar: "Akhbar Umar" by Ali al-Tantawi, "Manaqib Umar ibn al-Khattab" by Ibn al-Jawzi, "Abqariyyat Umar" by al-Aqqad, and a study of Umar's political firsts by Dr. Ghalib al-Qarashi. The author of the last title is read from a machine transcript and is marked needs_source. The other titles in his list are garbled and are not written. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:04:30}
- On strength, he describes the reach of Umar's rule: Greater Syria, Iraq, Egypt and Palestine were under the Muslims, and the expansion went on through Asia as far as Azerbaijan. He presents the extent of the realm as proof of strength. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:09:30}
- On mercy, he relates that an old man was begging in the street and, when Umar asked him, said that he was a Jew who had to collect money to pay the poll tax. Umar is said to have sent him back to the treasury for what he had paid, and to have ordered that the needy among the People of the Book in the Muslim provinces be given support from the public treasury. The account is the speaker's narrative and needs a source. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:11:00}
- On justice, he relates that Umar stood on the pulpit before the people and asked what they would do if he turned his head to one side like this, and that a man answered that they would strike him with their swords; Umar praised God for it. The wording of the exchange is partly garbled in the transcript, so only the sense is written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:12:30}
- He relates a narration in which the Prophet speaks with his companions about the questioning in the grave by the two angels, and Umar asks whether his faculties will be with him at that time; the Prophet answers yes. The speaker reads it as a sign of Umar's strength of heart. The wording of the narration is not reproduced here. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:14:30}
- On mercy towards children, he relates that Umar, walking the streets of Medina, heard a child crying and went to the child's mother, and that she said that he did not receive his share from the public funds until he was weaned. The speaker then gives Umar's decision that he would give a share to every child born in Islam, so that he is not questioned before God about them. The sequence is partly garbled and the figures are not given. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:17:00}
- He states that the strength of Umar was not only bodily but of the spirit, and advises that the spirit is nourished by remembrance of God in the morning and evening, by good company, by prayer at night and by avoiding the major sins. This is his advice to listeners. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:19:30}
- He relates that Umar lay down to rest under a tree after his inspection, with his sword under his arm, and that a visitor sent by a Persian ruler, who found him there, remarked on his justice and his security and praised him for it, and that the visitor repeated the remark to his own people. The episode presents this as a narrative of the speaker and needs a source. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:22:30}
- The speaker states that a rule of God in the world is that error does not endure. This is his statement of a principle. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:23:00}
- **Speaker's view (exhortation):** He calls on rulers, owners of companies and heads of households to be just in all their affairs, so that they may be secure. This is his exhortation to listeners and is not a rule the museum draws from it. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:25:00}
- He relates that Umar, walking with men behind him, turned round suddenly and asked whether he had wronged any of them, and that they said no. The sequence is partly garbled and is written in outline only. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:25:30}
- He relates a dream reported from the Prophet in which Umar's garment is dragged, and gives its interpretation: the garment stands for religion. The wording of the report is not reproduced here; the point is paraphrased. (status: tradition; needs_source; hadith wording not reproduced) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:27:30}
- He relates a night visit by Umar to a woman whose children were crying with hunger. She was heating water in a pot so that the children would fall asleep, and she said Umar did not know of their state. Umar went to the public store, took a sack of flour and food on his back and carried it to her, and said he would carry his own burden to God; he told the woman to come to him in the morning. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:30:00}
- He relates that Umar was found weeping behind a wall, and that when asked why he said he feared that the Muslims would enter Paradise and he would not, because he had not served them fully. The transmitter is named in the transcript as Ibn Mas'ud, but the opening is garbled and is marked needs_source. He relates this as a warning to anyone with responsibility for others. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:34:00}
- The speaker applies the same warning to any person with responsibility for an institution, a company, a household or children, and calls on each listener to see the responsibility he holds over others. This is his application. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:34:30}
- He relates a story of a horse race in Egypt, in which a rider from among the Copts won, and Amr ibn al-As's son struck him, saying that he was the son of the noble. The Copt complained to Umar. Umar called Amr and his son to Medina, had the Copt strike the son, and the narrative includes a further blow that the transcript garbles. The name of the Copt is garbled and is not written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:41:30}
- He attributes to Umar a rebuke to Amr ibn al-As: since when do you enslave people, when their mothers bore them free. The rebuke is given in the transcript as Umar's words, and the speaker calls it famous. The modern comparisons the speaker draws from it are not written here. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:42:30}
- He relates a story about the devil and the path: that when Umar takes a path, the devil takes another, and that the devil is not defeated by the hardness of a path but by avoiding the paths that feed the appetite, so that the devil does not gain strength from them. The point is paraphrased, not the wording of the report. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:47:30}
- He relates that Umar gave his wife musk to distribute to the poor. A drop of musk fell on her garment, and Umar washed the garment, dried it, and smelt it again and again, to make sure that no trace of public money was left on it. The speaker presents this as an example of scrupulousness with public money. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:49:00}
- He relates a dispute between Umar and a Jewish seller of a horse: Umar bought a horse, found it lame, and the two went to the judge Shurayh, who ruled that the caliph was an ordinary citizen before the law and that the horse must be returned to its seller as it was before the sale. The speaker takes this as the sign of a just rule. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:52:00}
- He quotes Ibn Mas'ud as saying that the Muslims were not able to pray openly at the Kaaba before the people of Quraysh until Umar embraced Islam. The quotation is given by the speaker and needs a source. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:53:00}
- The speaker closes by saying that he loves Umar ibn al-Khattab, and announces the next episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep09 00:54:00}

## Left out of this episode 9

Not written from episode 9: the opening remarks on the West's roots in history, on fictional heroes in films and on present-day media, which concern the present and are logged for the founder as contemporary asides (00:00:30 to 00:02:30); the remarks on the law of the strong in the world today and on civilisations from Adam to the present (00:05:00 to 00:07:30); the calls on employers, sons and doctors in the present (00:07:30 to 00:08:30); the passage on the present-day Muslim world, Palestine, and the calls to support Jerusalem and liberate al-Aqsa (00:23:30 to 00:24:00), which concern a contemporary conflict outside the subject of the series and are listed for the founder; the remarks on modern work, computers and the West (00:31:00 to 00:32:30); and the reference in one passage to a modern United Nations slogan (00:42:00 to 00:42:30), of which only the saying attributed to Umar is written. Qur'an verses recited by the speaker, including a verse about the heart's humility at 00:24:00 to 00:24:30, are not typed, and no passage link is added because the speaker does not name the verse. Prophetic and hadith wording (the questioning in the grave, the dream of the garment, the saying on responsibility, the report on the devil's path, and a saying about speaking in verse at 00:44:30 to 00:45:30) is paraphrased or left out and is not reproduced as scripture. Garbled lines are not written: the names of the kings and their entourage (00:20:30 to 00:21:30), the barber anecdote (00:25:30 to 00:26:00), the Bedouin with his daughters (00:42:30 to 00:44:30), a Companion's dispute with his wife and his sword at the door (00:49:30 to 00:51:00), and the figures and places of the Egypt story beyond what is set out above. Repetition loops, which are not speech and are not used, are at 00:18:30 to 00:19:30, 00:21:00, 00:31:00, 00:42:00 to 00:42:30 and 00:53:30 to 00:54:00. Discarded: these are not exclusions of content.

## Episode 10: Umar's humility, scrupulousness, fear of God and care for the Muslims

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He holds that Umar's humility did not contradict strength and dignity, and that humility must be told apart from servility. He closes the point with a report he gives about a woman who saw men walking weakly and speaking in low voices as worshippers and said that Umar, when he spoke, was heard, when he walked, walked fast, and when he struck, hurt, and that he was the true devotee. (needs_source: the report is given without its transmitter) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:17:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He presents Umar's concern for the affairs of the Muslims as a trait to imitate, and reads it beside a saying he attributes to the Prophet that whoever takes charge of the Muslims' affairs and then does not strive for them will not be with them in Paradise. The wording is partly garbled, so only the sense is written. (needs_source: the saying is given without its source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:36:00}
- **Audience comment (opinion):** A studio guest remarks that self-accounting is the practice open to everyone today: Umar is presented as holding himself to account daily and apologising when wrong, and listeners are urged to account themselves, firmly but without despair. The whole comment is the studio guest's; the speaker thanks them for it and does not claim it as his own. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:32:00}
:::

- The speaker opens by saying that two regular episodes on Umar ibn al-Khattab remain before the episode on his killing and death, and recalls the previous episode's three traits of strength, justice and mercy, which he says came together in Umar. He then sets out to take Umar's other qualities one by one, beginning with humility, and notes the extent of Umar's rule and the awe in which the rulers of the time held him. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:01:30}
- On humility, he relates that an old woman stopped Umar in the street and reminded him that he had once been called Umayr, a boy playing with other boys in Mecca, and then Umar, and was now called Commander of the Believers, and told him to fear God. When those with him rebuked her, he told them to be quiet and said she was Khawla bint Tha'laba, whose complaint God had heard from above the seven heavens; Umar is said to add that he would not have moved had she kept him a year. The woman's name is read from a garbled line and is marked needs_source. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:04:00}
- He relates that Umar climbed the pulpit and called the people to prayer together, then told them that as a boy he had pastured sheep for his maternal aunts in Mecca for a handful of dates, asked if they knew it, and came down. Ali ibn Abi Talib told him that he had only belittled himself; Umar said that his soul had told him he was Commander of the Believers, so he wanted to humble it. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:05:30}
- He relates that Umar, on the pulpit, announced that dowries for women should not exceed forty uqiyyas, and a woman called out from the back of the mosque that this was not his right, citing a Qur'anic verse about giving a woman a great sum, which the speaker does not name. Umar answered that the woman was right and Umar was wrong. The verse is not typed and no passage link is added because the speaker does not give its reference. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:07:30}
- The speaker says the purpose of recounting Umar is twofold: to know the stature of these men, and to ask each listener to look at his own pride and at whether he can bear to be told he is wrong. He calls Umar's "the woman was right and Umar was wrong" a mark of greatness. This is his application, not a rule the museum draws. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:08:30}
- He relates that Umar passed under the house of al-Abbas, the Prophet's uncle, and water from its spout fell on him; he pulled the spout down. Al-Abbas told him that the Prophet himself had put it there, and Umar swore that al-Abbas must stand on his back and put it back in its place with his own hands, which he did. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:10:00}
- On his plain living, he relates that a servant counted more than twenty-one patches on Umar's garment, four of them on the shoulder. Late for the Friday prayer, Umar apologised repeatedly and explained that he had only one garment and had been washing it. The figures are the speaker's, from a machine transcript. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:11:30}
- He relates that cloth came from Yemen and was shared among the people, each getting just enough for one garment. When Umar rose to preach in a garment longer than the others', a man told him that they would neither hear nor obey until he explained where it came from. Umar called his son Abdullah ibn Umar, who said that he had given his father his own share because one piece was not enough for him, and the man then said he would hear and obey. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:13:00}
- He relates that Umar, returning on foot across the desert towards Medina, met a boy on a donkey who offered to dismount; Umar refused and insisted that the boy ride in front on the smoother place while he rode behind on the rougher, and they entered Medina so. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:14:00}
- He relates that a woman called Umm Aban bint Utba was proposed to by both Umar and Talha ibn Ubayd Allah, and that she declined Umar, saying that whenever she saw him it was as though he were looking at God with his own eyes and she could not live with such a man. The sequence in the transcript is partly garbled, and her name is read from it and is marked needs_source. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:15:00}
- In the studio exchange, audience members add two reports about Umar: that he struck a man with a small staff and said he feared the man's own admiration of himself, and that he struck another whom he saw walking affectedly and told him not to kill the religion for them. The speaker takes from them that obedience must not become servility. The lines are partly garbled and the points are written in outline. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:17:00}
- On scrupulousness, he relates that when the treasures of the Persian emperor Kisra were brought to Medina and placed in the mosque without guards, Umar was struck that those who carried them had held back nothing, and said that those who delivered this were trustworthy. A remark is then attributed to a companion of his: you were chaste, so they were chaste, and had you grazed they would have grazed. The speaker of the remark is garbled in the transcript and is marked needs_source. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:19:30}
- He relates that Umar had ordered a stipend from the Muslim treasury for the old, the women and the children of the Jews and Christians, and had appointed a man to distribute it. When that man brought him a gift of an embroidered cloak, Umar asked whether it was from his own property or from the funds of the People of the Book, and found that a thread of it came from those funds; Umar insisted on seeing where the thread was and pulled it out. The man's identity and parts of the exchange are garbled. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:22:00}
- He relates that the camels of Abdullah ibn Umar were found grazing in the public pasture, and that Umar said they had been favoured because of whose son he was, took only the capital and sent the profit to the Muslim treasury, and struck those who kept the pasture. The opening of the story is lost in a repetition loop and only the outline is written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:24:00}
- He relates that when stipends were distributed Umar gave a young man whose father had fought at Uhud more than he gave his own son Abdullah, and said that the young man's father had stood firmer at Uhud than his own. The figures are garbled and are not written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:25:00}
- He relates that Umar struck a man with his staff and then, finding the man at peace with it, came to his door at dawn and put the staff in his hand to take retaliation. When the man said not today, Umar said that he had not slept that night and that the man must either retaliate or forgive, and the man forgave. The details of the first blow are garbled. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:26:30}
- On his fear of God, the speaker says Umar wept so much that two dark lines were left under his eyes. This is a description by the speaker, without a cited report in the episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:27:00}
- He relates that Umar, on his night rounds, heard a man reciting [[passage/quran-52-7|Surat al-Tur, verse 7]] in his house and fell to the ground, and was carried home and visited as an invalid for a month without anyone knowing what ailed him. He adds that Umar often led the dawn prayer with Surat Yusuf and could not finish the verse of [[passage/quran-12-86|Surat Yusuf, verse 86]] for weeping, so that people four or five rows back heard him. The verses are not typed. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:28:00}
- He relates that Umar asked the Companions about the fear of God: he asked Abu Musa al-Ash'ari to recite to make him long for God, and asked another Companion about the agony of death. The answer given is the image of a thorny branch drawn through the body, after which Umar said he understood. The Companion who answers is not named clearly, and the passage on piety asked of Ubayy ibn Ka'b is lost in a repetition loop. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:29:30}
- He gives a saying he attributes to Umar, that if the sky were torn open and he saw Paradise and Hell his certainty would not increase, and he says Umar did not want a single wrong done to any Muslim to stand against him on the Day of Judgment, so he asked the Companions to tell him of any. The attribution is the speaker's and is marked needs_source. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:30:30}
- He relates that Umar asked [[person/hudhayfa-ibn-al-yaman|Hudhayfa ibn al-Yaman]], whom he says the Prophet had trusted with the names of the hypocrites, to swear whether the Prophet had named him among them. Hudhayfa refused to name anyone, and told Umar that he would never tell any person after him. The opening of the story is lost in a repetition loop. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:34:00}
- An audience member remarks that, hearing these stories, the early Muslim community seems small and very close, with everyone knowing everyone, a closeness he says is missing today. This is a studio comment and not part of the episode's account of Umar. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:34:30}
- On his care for the Muslims, the speaker relates that Umar said he did not know when to sleep: if he slept at night he would lose the right of his Lord, and if he slept by day he would lose the right of his subjects. He says Umar slept in short pieces, an hour at night and a quarter of an hour by day, perhaps two or three hours in all. An audience member adds that Umar fell unconscious from exhaustion, which the speaker says may be an exaggeration. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:37:00}
- He relates that [[person/al-ahnaf-ibn-qays|al-Ahnaf ibn Qays]], a chief of Iraq, found Umar treating the camels of the charity herd, and Umar told him to take off his cloak and help, since the work earned reward. Asked by a man from the Iraqi delegation whether he could not have told a servant, Umar answered that no servant was more a servant than he and al-Ahnaf, and the speaker reads this as the saying that whoever takes charge of Muslims' affairs stands to them as a servant to a master. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:38:30}
- He relates that Umar visited the wives of men away on the conquests of Iraq, Syria and Egypt every Friday, knocking at their doors, asking whether they needed anything, collecting their requests and buying what they needed in the market himself with the sons of those men behind him. He says Umar offered to write letters to their husbands for women who could not write, and to carry the letters of those who could. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:41:00}
- He relates that when delicious food came to Umar he would not let his own household or the Muslims eat it, and would carry it to the children of the dead of the conquests, eat with them and tell them that what their fathers now taste in Paradise is sweeter. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:41:30}
- He relates that Umar, passing a tent where a woman was crying out in labour and her husband sat outside, took his own wife to help with the birth and sat with the husband until the woman called from inside to give her friend news of a son, at which the man realised he was talking to the Commander of the Believers, and Umar told him not to mind, for he was his brother. The names and details are garbled and only the outline is written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:43:00}
- He relates that Talha ibn Ubayd Allah, seeing Umar go out at night, followed him and watched him enter a house, and found in it a blind old woman who said that a man had come to her every week for ten years, cleaning the house and bringing what she needed, and when asked who he was said only that he was a servant of God who asked for her prayers. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:44:00}
- He says that when Umar led or read Surat al-Ahzab he raised his voice at the verse addressed to the Prophet's wives, and when asked why he said it was to remind them of their covenant. The verse is not named or typed. The account is partly garbled and is written in outline. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:45:00}
- He relates two further cases. Umar saw a young man walking in the streets of Medina in a limp and affected way, struck him lightly with the whip, and told him to walk with strength. He also looked for a blind man who had missed the dawn prayer, found that he had no one to guide him, and arranged a guide for him from the Muslim treasury. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:46:30}
- He relates that in the Year of Ashes, the famine, Umar prayed that God not destroy the community of Muhammad at his hands, and that some Companions prayed for the famine to end out of fear for Umar's life. The passage is partly garbled and is written in outline. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:47:00}
- He relates that when an army was away, Umar went out to the gates of Medina to wait for the messenger, and that when a rider arrived who did not know him and said he would tell only the Commander of the Believers, Umar ran beside him until they reached the mosque and then said that he was the one. On victory he prostrated in thanks. The sequence is partly garbled and is written in outline. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:48:00}
- He relates two further habits. Umar walked through the city with provisions of dates and raisins on a camel and told anyone who came to ask him something to eat first. After the market closed he walked through it at night gathering what people had dropped, and on Friday he came with a camel hung with their lost things and told them they had wearied him and should keep their belongings. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:50:00}
- The speaker closes by saying that Umar counted and weighed the zakat camels himself with paper and pen, and applies to him the Qur'anic description of the strong and the trustworthy, [[passage/quran-28-26|Surat al-Qasas, verse 26]], which is not typed here. He announces that the next episode is on the death of Umar ibn al-Khattab. A repetition loop at 00:50:00 follows the counting of the camels and is not used. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep10 00:51:00}

## Left out of this episode 10

Not written from episode 10: the opening remarks on the West's claim about strength and right and the remark on department managers in companies (00:00:30 to 00:02:30), the speaker's comparisons with the present day, such as the pretended humility of modern officials (00:05:30 to 00:06:00), and a comparison with the expulsion of the Muslims from al-Andalus (00:20:00), which concern the present or a later conflict; the remarks about the YouTube image of Umar (00:26:30 to 00:27:00), the call for the Muslims of the present to support the families of fighters in Palestine and the audience comments on it (00:48:00 to 00:48:30), the remarks on the Muslim world of today (00:44:00 to 00:44:30), and an aside on a man who would have been seized by Umar if he were present now (00:45:30), which are contemporary asides and are listed for the founder. Qur'an verses recited by the speaker are not typed: the verse on the woman's dowry (00:07:00), the verse addressed to the Prophet's wives (00:44:30), and the verses in the speaker's recitations; only the three verses he names are linked. The wording of the saying on responsibility for the Muslims (00:35:30) is paraphrased and not reproduced as scripture. Garbled passages are not written: the answer on piety from Ubayy ibn Ka'b (00:28:00 to 00:28:30), the children lined up in front of Umar (00:45:30 to 00:46:00), the story of the woman and the stipend of the Christian (names and exact figures), the figures in the story of the stipend of Uhud (00:24:30), and the opening of the story of the camels (00:22:30 to 00:23:00). Repetition loops, which are not speech and are not used, are at 00:07:30, 00:22:30 to 00:23:00, 00:28:30, 00:33:00 and 00:50:00 to 00:50:30. Discarded: these are not exclusions of content.

## Episode 11: Umar's last years, his killing and his death

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He holds that Umar was killed in a plot because he stood as a symbol of Islam and as the door holding back discord, and reads the killer's choice of the mosque as proof that Umar lived among the people and for them. The plot is given without a source and the views are his. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:38:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He argues that Umar's refusal to act against Abu Lu'lu'a on suspicion, his refusal to kill the foreign workers in Medina after the attack, and his order not to mutilate the body show a justice that held even towards a man who threatened him. This is the speaker's reading of the reports. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:38:30}
- **Studio guest comment (opinion):** The guest's closing remark on the Sunna and the Book of God is the guest's own, not the speaker's. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:50:30}
:::

- The speaker opens by saying this is the last episode on [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]], and reviews the earlier ones: his personal card and conversion, his encounters with the Prophet, the reports on his merits, his achievements as caliph, and the traits of strength, justice and mercy; he says the tales of Umar cannot be exhausted and that this episode reaches his death. Before it he tells some quick episodes from the end of his life. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:01:00}
- On the Year of Ashes (18 AH, as the speaker gives it), he says it was a severe drought that struck Medina and the Hijaz and not all Muslim lands, and that the ground turned the colour of ash. He says Umar himself suffered most, and that the Companions prayed for the famine to end for Umar's sake, since he would be the first to die of it. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:02:30}
- He relates that Umar saw a watermelon in his son's hand and rebuked him for eating fruit while the community of Muhammad was wasting away, and that when a feast of slaughtered sheep was set before him he said he would be a bad ruler if he ate the best of it and fed the people the bones; he then told his stomach that it would have only bread and oil until the people recovered. The speaker adds that this is why some biographers call Umar fair-skinned and others dark ("black" in the machine transcript), and that both are right: he was fair until the famine and his skin changed when he lived on bread and oil. The explanation is the speaker's own. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:04:30}
- He relates that Umar wrote to [[person/amr-ibn-al-as|Amr ibn al-As]], governor of Egypt, asking whether Amr and the people of Egypt would live in comfort while Medina starved, and that Amr answered by sending caravans, twenty or more, and more than ten ships laden with food and clothing. The speaker adds an aside on Egypt's role, which is not written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:06:00}
- He relates that Umar fed the people each day from sunset until after the evening prayer, that when the food from Egypt and Syria did not suffice he borrowed on his own account, and that he died owing 86,000 dirhams. He says the crowds at the daily feast grew to many thousands (the figures in the transcript are unclear and are not written), that Umar went among the diners telling them where to add bread, meat or broth, and that he then went home and raised his hands in prayer for an hour or two that God not destroy the community of Muhammad. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:08:00}
- He relates that in the last third of the night Umar went round the houses, asking for bread and oil and sending it to families he had not visited for three days, and ate only what was left. The Companions, he says, prayed for the drought to lift for Umar's sake. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:09:00}
- The speaker says that at the age of sixty-three Umar was still so strong that he challenged the young [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdullah ibn Abbas]] to see who could hold his head under water longer, and that a report has him at the end of his last pilgrimage standing on a height and praying that his years had grown old, his body weak and his flock widespread, and asking God to take him without his having fallen short in the rights of the Muslims. The ages and the challenge are the speaker's and the opening lines are partly garbled. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:10:30}
- He applies the prayer: while many people live for themselves and their children, Umar's children are absent from every account of him because he lived for the community; the speaker asks listeners to live for Islam. This is his application, not a rule the museum draws. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:11:30}
- He relates that on returning to Medina Umar told the people of a dream in which a rooster pecked him twice, and took it to mean his death or martyrdom, and that his killer would be a non-Arab. He then prayed repeatedly for martyrdom in God's path and death in the city of His Messenger. When his daughter [[person/hafsa|Hafsa]] asked how that could be, since Medina was the fortress of Islam, he answered that if God willed He would bring it about. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:14:00}
- He gives a saying he attributes to Umar in those days: a complaint to God of the strength of the wicked and the weakness of the trustworthy, and he reads it as Umar's fear for the community before leaving it. He relates that Umar asked the people to point him to a man he could appoint, one who, when among the people, is not taken for their leader, and when he leads is not felt to be so. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:15:30}
- He relates that a few days before his death Umar asked [[person/hudhayfa-ibn-al-yaman|Hudhayfa ibn al-Yaman]], whom he describes as keeper of the Prophet's secret, to swear whether the Prophet had named him among the hypocrites; Hudhayfa answered that he had not, and Umar thanked God. Umar then asked him about the trial that would surge over the community; Hudhayfa answered that between Umar and it stood a closed door; Umar asked whether the door would be opened or broken, and was told broken, and so it would not be closed again. The speaker explains the door as Umar himself, and Umar wept. The first part of the exchange is in the speaker's own paraphrase and is partly garbled. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:19:00}
- He relates the events leading to the killing. Umar had forbidden non-Muslims past the age of puberty to live in Medina; [[person/al-mughira-ibn-shuba|al-Mughira ibn Shu'ba]], a Companion, wrote asking leave to keep a young slave craftsman in the city, a blacksmith, carpenter and engraver, and Umar agreed. The slave, named Abu Lu'lu'a, was allowed to work for the people of Medina on payment of a hundred dirhams a month to his master. He complained to Umar that the sum was beyond him, and Umar told him to fear God and treat his master well, and then spoke to al-Mughira to take less. The sums and the details of the exchange are garbled in the transcript and are the speaker's. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:22:00}
- The speaker presents the killing as a plot and not a personal grievance. He relates that [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-abi-bakr|Abd al-Rahman ibn Abi Bakr]] came upon several men conferring, one of them Abu Lu'lu'a, and that a double-edged dagger with a grip in the middle fell from among them, and that he understood its meaning only after Umar's death. The speaker says Umar was killed because he stood as a symbol of Islam. The identities of the other men are not written; the speaker's attribution of them to groups is left out (see below), and the plot itself is his account and unchecked. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:24:00}
- He relates that Umar once met Abu Lu'lu'a among some Companions and told him he had heard that he could make a mill turned by the wind; the man glared and answered that he would make Umar a mill the people would talk about. The Companions took it as a threat and offered to kill him, but Umar said he would not kill a man on suspicion nor exile him, and that what God decreed would come to pass. The speaker praises the restraint as justice towards a man who had only threatened. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:26:00}
- The attack, as the speaker tells it: at the dawn prayer, in the Prophet's mosque in Medina, with [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] leading, the report comes from Amr ibn Maymun, who stood in the second row with [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdullah ibn Abbas]] between him and Umar. Before Umar began the recitation the attacker stepped out and stabbed him six times, in the shoulder, back and belly; Umar cried that the dog had killed him. The attacker then struck out left and right, wounding thirteen and killing nine according to the speaker, and when [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Ibn Awf]] (the speaker says "Abdullah ibn Awf"; the identification is the museum's) threw a cloak over him and he knew he was caught he killed himself. The speaker gives the date as the dawn of the 23rd of Dhu al-Hijja, 23 AH, which is unchecked. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:29:30}
- He relates that Umar, bleeding, asked for [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf]] and told him to lead the prayer, and that Abd al-Rahman led the dawn prayer briefly while the people behind, who did not know what had happened, said glory be to God; Umar himself prayed beside him.
