Afterwards Umar asked [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]] to lay him on the ground and not on a cushion, saying perhaps his Lord would have mercy on him, and told him to shroud him modestly, since if God was pleased with him He would give him a better one and if angry would strip it from him. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:31:30}
- He relates that Umar lay unconscious for a long time, and that [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdullah ibn Abbas]] woke him by announcing the time of prayer; Umar asked if the people had prayed, asked for water to make ablution and prayed. When a physician gave him milk and it came out through the wounds, he told Umar that he would die, and Umar thanked him for his honesty. As the physician left, Umar saw his garment trailing on the ground and told him to lift it, as that was cleaner for the garment and more pious before his Lord. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:33:30}
- He relates that on the third day Umar asked [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdullah ibn Abbas]] to find out who had struck him; Ibn Abbas returned and said it was the slave of [[person/al-mughira-ibn-shuba|al-Mughira ibn Shu'ba]], Abu Lu'lu'a. Umar said he had told people to treat him well, then thanked God that his killer was not a Muslim who could argue against him before God with a prostration. He asked whether any Muslims had conspired, and Ibn Abbas went out to ask the Muslims, and the outcome of the question is unclear in the transcript. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:34:30}
- He relates that the Emigrants and the Ansar wept and said they wished to give part of their own lives to lengthen Umar's, and that the women of Medina said they would rather their children die than Umar. He gives a report that Umar's wife cried out that he had put an end to discord and revived the sunna, and that the people said no calamity worse than this had struck them, since the door that held back discord was gone. The report of Aisha weeping continuously is the speaker's. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:36:00}
- He observes that the killer chose to strike in the mosque, among the people, because Umar lived among them and could be reached nowhere else; the speaker draws from this that Umar never lived for himself. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:38:00}
- He relates that Umar then said to [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdullah ibn Abbas]] that he and his father al-Abbas had wanted more non-Muslim slaves in Medina; Ibn Abbas answered that if Umar wished they would kill them, and Umar refused, saying they had entered the land, eaten of its food and spoken its language. He added that if Abu Lu'lu'a was alive or dead, his body must not be mutilated, and left his case to the Day of Judgment. The remarks are given in the speaker's paraphrase and the opening is partly garbled. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:38:30}
- He relates that a young man came to Umar with glad tidings of Paradise, recalling his emigration, his support of Islam and that the Muslims prayed at the Kaaba after he entered Islam, and that the Prophet and Abu Bakr died pleased with him; Umar wept and said he feared only two things, the terror of the grave and God's questioning on the rights of those he ruled. [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdullah ibn Abbas]] then consoled him that he had judged by the Book of God and divided justly, and Umar asked him to bear witness to that before God; [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] added his own witness at the door. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:41:00}
- He relates that Umar then asked for his debts to be counted, found them to be 86,000 dirhams and asked how they could be met. He told [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]] to ask, after his death and not before it, first the house of Umar, then Banu Adi, then Quraysh, to settle them. The speaker says the sum was gathered within a week of his death and paid to [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]], so that Umar met God free of debt, and urges listeners to take debt seriously. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:44:00}
- He relates that Umar sent [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]] to [[person/aisha|Aisha]] to ask permission to be buried beside his two companions, telling him to say Umar ibn al-Khattab and not Commander of the Believers, since he was no longer their commander. The speaker says Aisha, who had meant the place for herself, said she would prefer Umar to herself, and that she wept for Umar more than for her father Abu Bakr, which the speaker reads as her sense that the door of discord would open after him. Umar, told of the permission, thanked God and said that nothing had weighed on him for years as much as this; he told Ibn Umar that after his death he should ask her again, since she may have agreed out of regard for him while he lived. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:48:00}
- He says Umar died on a Wednesday in 23 AH after ruling for ten years, six months and four days, and was buried beside the Prophet and Abu Bakr. The speaker says Umar named six men to choose from among them after him, [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]], [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], [[person/talha-ibn-ubaydullah|Talha ibn Ubayd Allah]], al-Zubayr ibn al-Awwam, Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas and [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Ibn Awf]] (the transcript says "Abdullah ibn Awf"); when asked to include his son [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]], he said one of his house being questioned about the caliphate on the Day of Judgment was enough. He ordered [[person/suhayb-ibn-sinan|Suhayb]] to lead the prayer until they chose. The figures are the speaker's and unchecked. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:49:30}
- A studio guest closes with a remark that Umar's greatest concern was to live Islam and to be buried beside the Prophet, which the guest reads as his love of him and his following of the Sunna and the Book of God, and urges people to return to both. The remark is the guest's, not the speaker's, and the transcript of it is partly garbled. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:50:30}
- The speaker closes by saying Umar is a remarkable figure by every measure, and takes from him the lesson that each person is a shepherd and responsible for his flock. He ends with two sayings and announces that the next episode is on [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]]. The first is the Prophet's saying, as he gives it, that on the Day of Judgment he would raise Abu Bakr on his right and Umar on his left and say that so would they be raised, and that the three lie buried together. The second is a saying of [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]: that he sees Islam on the Day of Judgment asking about those who helped and those who failed it, until it comes to Umar and raises his hands. The first saying's wording is not reproduced and both are given without a chain. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep11 00:52:30}

## Left out of this episode 11

Not written from episode 11: the aside on Egypt's place in the region (00:05:00 to 00:05:30); the speaker's appeals to listeners on how to live and on devoting themselves to Islam, with remarks about athletes and the screen (00:10:30 to 00:11:30); and his remarks on the claim in the West that Umar is a fictional figure, on the imitation of Western dress and manners by young people today, and on Muslims' debts (00:41:30 to 00:43:30), which are contemporary asides listed for the founder. The speaker's naming of the other men in the plot by religion or group (00:22:30 to 00:23:30) is not written, as a polemical claim about groups from a partly garbled line, and is listed for the founder. Qur'an verses and the prophetic sayings quoted are not typed or reproduced as scripture; the two sayings at the close are paraphrased. Garbled lines are not written: the length of the Year of Ashes (00:02:00), the figures of the diners (00:07:00), the age of Ibn Abbas and the ages in the water contest (00:09:30), Umar's account of the knife, the physician's name and details at 00:36:00, the reasons for Aisha's weeping and the details of the succession at 00:46:00 to 00:46:30. Repetition loops, which are not speech and are not used, are at 00:31:30 to 00:32:00 and 00:45:00. Discarded: these are not exclusions of content. The speaker's age of Umar at death (63) agrees with episodes 1 and 4; the figures 61 and 62 given in episodes 3 and 5 are not repeated here.

## Episode 12: Uthman ibn Affan, his lineage, wives, character and conversion, and what the Prophet said of him

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He holds that modesty (haya') leads a person to faith, that Abu Bakr read this trait in [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman]] and so sent him to the Prophet, and that modesty is the thread that lets a person complete the other branches of faith. He calls Uthman the greatest of the community in this trait. The sayings he uses for this are not sourced in the recording and the scholars he cites are not named. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:32:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He argues from the wealth of Uthman, of [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf]] and of [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] the trader that religion does not prevent wealth or success in life, and says Uthman's money later served Islam, which he promises to tell. The figures are his, from a machine transcript, and unchecked. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:09:30}
- **Speaker's stated purpose (opinion):** He says he is telling the sayings in which the Prophet speaks of Uthman so that when the series reaches the time of discord no one will say that Uthman may have been wrong. The museum records this as the speaker's stated purpose and sets out no other account of the period here. (needs_source; sensitivity: contested) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:39:00}
- **Studio guest and audience remarks (opinion):** The remarks at 00:23:30 to 00:27:30 (women who led their relatives to Islam) are an exchange between the speaker and the studio audience, and the transcript does not separate the voices; the closing remarks at 00:46:30 to 00:47:30 are a studio guest's, not the speaker's. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:47:30}
:::

- The speaker begins by saying that the previous episode ended with the death of [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] and that from this episode the series moves to the third caliph, [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]]. The line that places the episode in the calendar is garbled and is not written. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:00:30}
- He reflects on Medina: a visitor who greets the Prophet, [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and Umar recalls the events told in the series and feels love for the Companions, and finds that Medina holds much of Paradise, naming [[place/uhud|Uhud]] and the Rawda. He says scholars asked whether the Rawda is a piece of Paradise in a figurative sense or in fact, and that they concluded it is so in fact; he adds a remark from one scholar whose wording is garbled, in which he hopes that a person God lets sit there will be admitted to Paradise in the next world. A saying of the Prophet about Uhud is cited with garbled wording and is not written. The speaker's account; the scholars are not named. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:02:00}
- He says that every spot in Medina saw something great of Islam: the Friday prayer was established there, the first mosque, zakat and fasting were prescribed there, as were jihad and the penal rules, and the Medinan Qur'an was revealed there. He refers to a saying of the Prophet, which he paraphrases, that faith draws back to Medina as a snake draws back to its hole. The wording is not reproduced. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:03:30}
- On sources, he says the series names its sources at the start of each Companion so that listeners can check them, and he shows a list on the screen: a book the transcript titles "al-Amir al-Amin Dhu al-Nurayn" by Mahmud Shakir; a book on the third caliph under the name Abd al-Rahman ibn Uthman, after which the line becomes a repetition loop that is discarded, so its title and author are not clear; the older reference work the transcript renders "Azd al-Ghaba" (the museum reads it as [[work/usd-al-ghaba|Usd al-Ghaba]]), with another title in the same line that is garbled; and "Ashab al-Rasul" by Mahmud al-Misri. These are the speaker's list and the books were not checked for this entry. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:05:00}
- The personal card begins with the name and line as the speaker gives them: Uthman ibn Affan ibn Abi al-As ibn Umayya ibn Abd Shams ibn Abd Manaf ibn Qusayy, meeting the Prophet's line at Abd Manaf, which the speaker counts as the sixth grandfather (the count is his and is not checked). His father Affan died in the Jahiliyya, before the Prophet was sent. His mother was Arwa bint Kurayz ibn Rabia, who accepted Islam, emigrated, pledged allegiance to the Prophet, lived in Medina, outlived the Prophet, Abu Bakr and Umar, and died in the caliphate of her son. His mother's mother, whom the transcript names al-Bayda (the line is garbled and the name is not identified further), was a daughter of Abd al-Muttalib and so an aunt of the Prophet. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:06:30}
- His appearance, as the speaker gives it from the traditional description: of middle height, close to the Prophet's; thick hair and a full beard; the finest mouth and one of the handsomest faces; fine skin, because he was wealthy all his life; a colour the transcript reads as inclining to "al-sumra" (a machine reading of an unclear word); full legs and long arms; much hair on the hands, feet and body. The speaker remarks that each Companion he describes turns out to be handsome, unlike the image some people imagine. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:08:00}
- On his wealth, the speaker says Uthman inherited a very large fortune from his father, who was very rich, and that at one time his estate reached thirty million dirhams as the transcript gives it ("thirty thousand thousand"); he says Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf died leaving eight million dirhams, and that Abu Bakr was a well-known successful trader. He adds that Uthman put the inherited money into trade and became a very rich merchant, and that his money would later serve Islam, which is not told in this episode. The figures are the speaker's and unchecked. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:10:00}
- He says Uthman never lied in trade, before Islam or after it, and was dearly loved even in Mecca before Islam: mothers, he says, would bless a child by wishing that al-Rahman would love him as Quraysh loved Uthman. He reads this as the reason Uthman was close to Islam: his character. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:11:30}
- Wives, first Ruqayya and then Umm Kulthum, both daughters of the Prophet: [[person/ruqayya-bint-muhammad|Ruqayya]] and [[person/umm-kulthum-bint-muhammad|Umm Kulthum]] had been betrothed by contract, before the Prophet's mission, to two sons of [[person/abu-lahab|Abu Lahab]] (their names are garbled). The speaker relates that when Abu Lahab turned to bitter enmity after the mission began and after the revelation about him (not typed), he told his sons to divorce them, and they did, which the speaker reads as a way to wound the Prophet in his own family. Uthman then married Ruqayya, before the Hijra. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:13:00}
- Ruqayya bore him Abdullah, born, the speaker says, two years before the Hijra. He places her death in 2 AH, on the day [[person/zayd-ibn-haritha|Zayd ibn Haritha]] entered Medina with the news of the Muslims' victory at [[event/battle-of-badr|Badr]], and says Abdullah died four years after the Hijra, at the age of six (asked how long after his mother's death, he gives two years, in a partly garbled line); he also says that she died of grief over her son's illness. These statements do not agree in order, and the museum records them as the speaker's and does not reconcile them. The line that follows on the cause of the child's illness is garbled and is not written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:17:00}
- He lists the Prophet's bereavements: his father, mother, grandfather, uncle, his beloved wife, and his children and grandchildren, among them Abdullah the son of Ruqayya (a number of children is given in the transcript and is not written). From this he draws the lesson that a child's death is not a sign of God's anger. This is his application. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:15:00}
- A year after Ruqayya's death, Uthman married Umm Kulthum, who lived with him from 3 AH and died in Sha'ban 9 AH, a year before the Prophet's death (the speaker's dates). He gives a saying of the Prophet that had he had a third daughter he would have married her to Uthman (the wording is not reproduced), says that no one in humankind but Uthman married two daughters of a prophet, and says this is why he was called Dhu al-Nurayn, the possessor of two lights. He adds that the Arabs knew no couple more devoted than Ruqayya and Uthman, and that he will tell their story in detail later. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:16:30}
- He says Umm Kulthum bore Uthman no children, and that she had been divorced by Abu Lahab's son and stayed unmarried until Uthman married her. He offers this as comfort to women whose marriage is delayed, adding that the Prophet's own daughter waited and then married one of the men dearest to him. This is his application. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:17:30}
- Later wives, as the transcript reads the names: "Fakhtha bint Ghazwan" (the museum reads Fakhita), a woman it reads "Fatina bint al-Walid" (a machine spelling) and Umm al-Banin bint Uyayna ibn Hisn. The speaker says [[person/uyayna-ibn-hisn|Uyayna ibn Hisn]] had a long hostility to the Prophet and sees Uthman's marriage into his family as a great act; his explanation of why is garbled and is not written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:18:00}
- His last wife, Na'ila bint al-Furafisa (the transcript reads "al-Furatsa"), accepted Islam before Uthman consummated the marriage and her Islam was good; the transcript gives a word about her origin that is garbled and is not written. The speaker says she took a great stand on the day of his death, which he calls martyrdom, and that he will tell it when the series reaches that point. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:18:30}
- On his children, the speaker says Uthman had nine sons and six daughters. Two sons were named Abdullah, the elder and the younger, which he compares with Umar naming three sons Abd al-Rahman and reads as the Companions' attachment to that name. The other sons the transcript lists are Umar, Khalid, al-Walid, Said and Abd al-Malik (Umar appears twice and the names do not reach nine), and the daughters Maryam, Umm Said, Aisha, Umm Umar and Umm al-Banin; he says he is not giving all the names. The counts are his and unchecked. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:19:00}
- On dates, the speaker says Uthman was born six years after the Year of the Elephant, six years younger than the Prophet, and so thirty-four when the Prophet, at forty, was sent; and fifty-seven when the Prophet died (the figure is garbled in the transcript and agrees with the speaker's arithmetic, sixty-three less six). He says the Prophet, Abu Bakr and Umar each died at sixty-three, as in episodes 1, 4 and 11. He says Uthman was seventy when allegiance was pledged to him after Umar (the transcript repeats the figure and the words after it are unclear), that his caliphate lasted twelve years less twelve days, and that he died at eighty-two; the date of the pledge and his age at the Hijra are garbled and are not written. He says Uthman was killed on 18 Dhu al-Hijja in 35 AH, close to the date of Umar's death on 23 Dhu al-Hijja, and that [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] came after him. All dates are the speaker's, in the Hijri calendar, and unchecked. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:21:00}
- On his conversion, the speaker says Uthman was busy with his trade when, one night, he heard a voice in a dream calling the sleepers to wake because Muhammad had appeared in Mecca. He did not understand and went to a maternal aunt whom he loved, who answered that Muhammad ibn Abdullah had been sent by God with a revelation from God, guiding to God. (status: tradition; dream account; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:23:00}
- Prompted by the aunt's part in the story, the speaker puts a question to the studio audience: who knows women who led their fathers, brothers or husbands to Islam? The examples named in the exchange, which the transcript does not assign to one voice, are [[person/fatima-bint-al-khattab|Fatima bint al-Khattab]] in the story of Umar's conversion; and [[person/umm-sulaym|Umm Sulaym]], who, in the account given, refused to marry [[person/abu-talha-al-ansari|Abu Talha]] while he worshipped idols, asking him whether he worshipped what a carpenter makes; the exchange adds that no woman has had a greater dowry than hers (the line stating it is partly garbled). The speaker adds that a woman can be very strong and influential. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:25:00}
- The exchange goes on to women who stood by their husbands or became Muslim before their families, again without separating the voices: [[person/khadija|Khadija]] steadying the Prophet after the first revelation; [[person/zaynab-bint-muhammad|Zaynab]], the Prophet's daughter, granting protection to her husband in Medina and telling her father she had done so, and the exchange calls her a reason for her husband's Islam (the transcript gives him as "Abu al-As ibn Rabia", and the museum reads this as [[person/abu-al-as-ibn-al-rabi|Abu al-As ibn al-Rabi]]; the line is partly garbled); [[person/umm-habiba-bint-abi-sufyan|Umm Habiba bint Abi Sufyan]], Muslim before her father; [[person/asma-bint-abi-bakr|Asma bint Abi Bakr]], before her mother; and [[person/umm-kulthum-bint-uqba|Umm Kulthum bint Uqba]], whom it describes as emigrating alone on foot to Medina although her father [[person/uqba-ibn-abi-muayt|Uqba ibn Abi Muayt]] was among those who most harmed the Prophet (the transcript reads the father's name 'Uqba ibn Abi Muadh' and the woman's first name is garbled; the museum reads Umm Kulthum bint Uqba ibn Abi Muayt from the mention of the camel entrails thrown on the Prophet's back, unchecked). The speaker draws from these the value of women in the first age of Islam, against the idea that they had no place. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:27:30}
- Returning to Uthman, the speaker says he took the aunt's words to his close friend Abu Bakr, who said his aunt had spoken truly, that he saw Uthman as a man of modesty, that this trait would lead him only to Islam, and that he should go and listen to the Messenger of God, since a modest man would be guided. The speaker calls this an important point and turns to the question of modesty. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:29:00}
- On modesty, he gives two sayings of the Prophet in paraphrase: that modesty and faith go together and when one is removed the other goes; and that faith has some sixty or seventy branches (he gives both numbers) and modesty is one of them. He says scholars asked why modesty alone is named among the branches and answered that a person who has modesty can bring the rest, so that it is like the thread that holds the others together. He also refers to a saying about those who publicise their wrongdoing (the line is partly garbled). The wording of the sayings is not reproduced. (status: tradition; needs_source; hadith wording not reproduced) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:32:30}
- After a studio participant observes that someone who is modest before people will be modest before God, the speaker develops the point: a person may commit a wrong and not want people to see it, which shows that he feels it as wrong. He defines modesty as being ashamed to do wrong and to be seen in a weak or faulty state, and faith and Islam as applying whatever keeps one from deficiency, so that modesty has to lead to faith. This is the speaker's own reading. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:32:00}
- He says Uthman then went to the Prophet, who put Islam before him, and that Uthman says (in the speaker's rendering of his words) that modesty would not let him hold back, and he made the declaration of faith before the Prophet; the speaker counts him as the eighth, or one of the first eight, to enter Islam. The count is the speaker's. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:33:30}
- The first saying about Uthman he tells is a report he attributes to [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ashari]] (the transcript places his remark that a report is agreed upon and found in al-Bukhari, Muslim and the other sound collections after this account and before the next, and does not make clear which of the two it refers to). Abu Musa went out one morning looking for the Prophet, asked at his house and the mosque, and was told he was at the well of Aris, where he found him with his legs dangling in the well. Abu Musa sat at the gate as doorkeeper; when Abu Bakr came he asked him to wait, and the Prophet said to let him in and give him glad tidings of Paradise; the same happened with Umar. Abu Bakr sat at the Prophet's right with his legs in the well, and Umar at his left. When Uthman came the Prophet said to let him in and give him glad tidings of Paradise with a trial that would befall him; Uthman replied with words the transcript renders as that he would be patient, God willing (the first line is garbled). Finding no place beside the other two, he sat on the rim of the well on the opposite side with his legs hanging in it. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:38:00}
- The speaker then gives an interpretation which the transcript attributes to "Said ibn Musayyib" (the museum reads the name as [[person/said-ibn-al-musayyab|Said ibn al-Musayyab]], unchecked): that the arrangement shows the three will be buried together and Uthman alone, on the opposite side. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:38:30}
- A second saying, which he says is in the Musnad of Ahmad and in a second collection whose name the transcript garbles (it reads "al-Tilmidhayn"; the museum reads al-Tirmidhi, unchecked), has the Prophet say of ten men that each is in Paradise. The speaker names them: Abu Bakr, Umar, Uthman, Ali, [[person/talha-ibn-ubaydullah|Talha ibn Ubaydullah]], [[person/zubayr-ibn-al-awwam|al-Zubayr ibn al-Awwam]], Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf, Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas, [[person/said-ibn-zayd|Said ibn Zayd]] and [[person/abu-ubayda-ibn-al-jarrah|Abu Ubayda ibn al-Jarrah]]; the names are recalled in a quick exchange with the studio audience. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:40:30}
- A third saying, which he gives on the authority of [[person/aisha|Aisha]] and says is in the Musnad of Ahmad: the Prophet sat with his foot on Uthman's foot while revelation came down and Uthman wrote it, and Aisha wiped the sweat from the Prophet's forehead; the Prophet said to Uthman to write. Aisha says that God would not have placed a man in such a position unless he were dear to Him. The speaker comments on the closeness this shows. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:42:00}
- A fourth report: the Prophet sat in his house reclining with part of his clothing raised; Abu Bakr asked leave and came in and the Prophet stayed as he was, and so with Umar; when Uthman asked leave the Prophet sat upright and arranged his garment. Asked why afterwards by the narrator (whose name is garbled in the transcript), he said that Uthman is a modest man and he feared that if he came in so he would be too shy to state his need, and added that he should be modest before a man before whom the angels are modest. The wording is not reproduced. (status: tradition; needs_source; hadith wording not reproduced) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:43:30}
- A fifth report, which he gives without naming a source: when a matter of importance came up, the Prophet sat with Abu Bakr at his right, Umar at his left and Uthman before him (the sentence that follows is garbled and is not written). (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:44:00}
- A sixth report, which he says is in the Musnad of Ahmad with a garbled narrator: Uthman came in and the Prophet had him come close and spoke to him quietly and at length, then asked whether he had understood; Uthman said he had, and this was repeated three times, the third time with Uthman saying that his ear had heard it and his heart had kept it. The speaker links it to the day Uthman was besieged: the Companions, among them Ali ibn Abi Talib as the transcript has it, offered to fight for him, and Uthman refused, saying the Prophet had made a covenant with him and he would hold to it until he met him. The speaker says no one knows what the Prophet said, and offers three guesses: about the time of discord, about what would happen, or that he should be patient as long as he could. The links to the siege and the guesses are the speaker's. (status: tradition; needs_source; sensitivity: contested) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:46:00}
- A studio guest adds two remarks, which are the guest's and not the speaker's: that Uthman said he would be patient in the trial only after he had heard from the Prophet that he was in Paradise, and, on modesty, the Prophet's description of it as guarding the head and what it holds and the belly and what it contains and remembering death and decay (in paraphrase), together with the observation that the Prophet himself was extremely modest (the comparison is garbled). The transcript of the guest's words is partly garbled. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:47:30}
- The speaker closes by saying this was the first episode on Uthman, a personal card and the sayings of the Prophet about him, and that the next will be on Uthman's stands in the Prophet's lifetime. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep12 00:47:30}

## Left out of this episode 12

Not written from episode 12: the speaker's appeals to the audience (to be among the first in their own circle to pray at night, to study the Qur'an or to wear the headscarf, 00:33:30), his comments on religious people with poor manners (00:11:00 to 00:11:30), his advice to parents who lose a child and to women whose marriage is delayed beyond the one-line application above (00:14:30 and 00:17:30), which are contemporary appeals and asides listed for the founder. Qur'an verses and the wording of sayings of the Prophet are not typed or reproduced as scripture; the sayings are paraphrased and marked as needing a source, and the revelation about Abu Lahab is mentioned only and not typed. Garbled lines are not written: the line placing the episode in the calendar (00:00:00), the scholar's remark on the Rawda (00:01:30 to 00:02:00), the saying about Uhud (00:01:00), the title and author of the book at 00:04:30 (a repetition loop), the second title at 00:05:00, the maternal grandmother's name (00:06:30), the names of Abu Lahab's two sons (00:12:00), the cause of the child's illness (00:14:00), the line on the origin of the last wife (00:18:00), Uthman's age at the Hijra and the date of the pledge of allegiance (00:20:00), the aunt's reply details (00:23:00), the line on Zaynab and her husband (00:26:30), Uthman's first words to the Prophet (00:38:00), the narrator of the fourth and sixth reports (00:42:00, 00:44:30) and the comparison for the Prophet's modesty (00:47:30). The repetition loop at 00:04:30 is not speech and is not used; it is discarded and is not an exclusion of content. The speaker's age of Umar at death (63) agrees with episodes 1, 4 and 11.

## Episode 13: Uthman ibn Affan's stands with the Prophet: emigration, Badr, Uhud, Hudaybiyya and Tabuk

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He holds that the Prophet did not deal with everyone in one way: some people need firmness and others gentleness, according to what each needs. He illustrates this with the address he says the Prophet used to Uthman at his conversion (the speaker's rendering, not sourced in the recording), and sets it against the way he says Umar ibn al-Khattab had to be dealt with (the transcript's line on that is garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:03:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He reads the Prophet's words at Tabuk that nothing Uthman does after this will harm him not as a statement that Uthman would err but as a measure of the size of the reward, which he says outweighs any later slip; and he reads the angels' modesty before Uthman as reverence and high regard, not embarrassment. The sayings are given in the speaker's rendering and are not sourced in the recording. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:50:30}
- **Studio participants' remarks (opinion):** From 00:42:00 to 00:48:00 the studio participants comment in turn. The transcript does not name them or reliably separate their voices from the speaker's, so each remark below is written as a participant's, not the speaker's. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:46:00}
:::

- The speaker opens by restating the aim of the programme. He says its title comes from a word of Bilal, spoken as he was dying, that on the morrow they would meet the beloved (the rest of that line is garbled and is not written). The aim, he says, is not to tell stories for their own sake but to reach a meaning he hopes the community will share: that the listeners belong to the generation of the Companions, who are the dearest people to them and their models; that they will not manage to be like them or to do as they did, but can try to walk in their footsteps; and that they take pride in belonging to this community. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:01:00}
- He recalls that the previous episode began the series on Uthman with his personal card, and says that this episode will tell all his stands with the Prophet, from his conversion to the Prophet's death. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:01:00}
- On his kunya, the speaker says that in the Jahiliyya it was Abu Amr, and that after Islam he was called Abu Abdullah, after his son Abdullah, the child of Ruqayya, the Prophet's daughter; he kept it until Abdullah died, and the transcript's account of what followed is garbled and is not written. The speaker counts the kunya among the beautiful things about him. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:02:00}
- He adds a detail he says he did not tell earlier from Uthman's conversion: when Uthman came in to the Prophet, the Prophet spoke to him with great confidence and mercy, inviting him to answer God's call to His Paradise, saying that he saw him to be a man of sense, and that he was God's messenger to him and to all people. The speaker notes that the Prophet said "to you" as well as "to all people", a word aimed at one person. He compares this with the way he says Umar ibn al-Khattab had to be approached; the transcript's lines on that are garbled and the details are not written. Uthman, in the speaker's rendering of his words, says he could not restrain himself and made the declaration of faith; the speaker counts him one of the first eight to enter Islam. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:03:30}
- He says that Uthman then asked for the Prophet's daughter [[person/ruqayya-bint-muhammad|Ruqayya]] and lived with her for several years in Mecca, until shortly before the emigration to Abyssinia. He says Uthman was very rich and held a high place in Quraysh, and recalls from the last episode the mothers who blessed their children by wishing that al-Rahman would love them as Quraysh loved Uthman. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:04:00}
- On the harm he met after his conversion, the speaker says Quraysh were reluctant to harm Uthman because he was so loved, and that, as in Quraysh's custom, the harm had to come from a relative: an uncle (the transcript gives a name that is garbled and it is not written) bound him in chains and fettered him, and said he would not release him until he gave up this religion for the religion of his fathers. Uthman answered that he would never leave it, even if he were left like that all his life. The speaker says that when the uncle saw his firmness and persistence he released him, and describes Uthman as gentle yet very strong in his religion. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:05:30}
- From this he draws a one-line application for anyone who meets opposition at home over religion: such opposition does not necessarily mean the family hates the faith, since they may fear for the person or not yet understand; he advises a respectful, gentle manner, success in one's own life and steadiness in obedience. While doing so he recites a verse on the testing of those who say they believe, which is not typed here. This is his application. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:07:30}
- He says that because harm had no effect on Uthman, who was very rich, Quraysh began to try not to deal with him (the transcript's following words are garbled and are not written), and that he stayed steadfast beside the Prophet. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:08:30}
- When the harm to the Companions in Mecca grew severe, the Prophet told them to emigrate to Abyssinia. The speaker says the first to emigrate with his family was Uthman, with his wife Ruqayya, the Prophet's daughter. His comparison with Suhayb in the transcript is garbled and is not written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:09:00}
- He says news of the pair stopped reaching the Prophet, who went out asking after his daughter and her husband, until a woman came and told him she had seen Uthman and Ruqayya on the road, with Ruqayya riding a donkey and Uthman driving it. The Prophet said, in paraphrase, praise be to God, may God be their companion. The speaker says (the transcript does not make clear whether he is giving the Prophet's words or his own) that Uthman was the first to emigrate with his family for God's sake since Lot. The speaker remarks on the part women play in the Prophet's life. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:11:00}
- He says Uthman stayed in Abyssinia and that two years before the emigration to Medina his son Abdullah, the Prophet's grandson, was born to him by Ruqayya; Uthman then emigrated to Medina with the emigrants. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:11:30}
- The well of Ruma: he says the Muslims who reached Medina, emigrants and Ansar, had no money, and that there was a well there called Ruma (the transcript's spelling), which belonged to a Jewish man who sold its water to the Muslims for money. They paid large sums, and the Prophet said, in paraphrase: who will buy the well of Ruma for us, and I guarantee him a place to drink in Paradise. The speaker calls the episode an episode of spending. Uthman went to the owner and said he wished to buy it; the owner, thinking he wanted it to put pressure on the Muslims, set the price at fifty thousand dirhams (the speaker's figure). (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:13:00}
- Uthman said he did not need the whole well and proposed to buy half, so that the well would be one day his and one day the owner's. The owner agreed, and Uthman paid twelve thousand dirhams for the half. He then told the Muslims to drink as they wished on his days and to take enough for the next day, so that they would not need to come on the owner's day. On the owner's day nobody came, and he went back to Uthman and offered to sell the other half, which Uthman bought for eight thousand dirhams, so that the total paid, the speaker says, was twenty thousand of the fifty thousand. The speaker calls this a wise decision made with planning, not careless lavishness, and says he has made the point so that people who want to give do so with good judgement. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:15:30}
- He says Uthman never reproached the Muslims over the well and never mentioned it, being a man of modesty, until a few days before he was killed, when he asked those present, as the speaker renders it, whether they did not know that he had done such and such; the speaker promises to tell that story when the series reaches it. Recalling the previous episode, he says the Prophet did not adjust his posture for Abu Bakr and Umar but did so for Uthman, saying, in paraphrase, that he was ashamed before a man of whom the angels are ashamed; the transcript has the speaker say that [[person/aisha|Aisha]] was the one who asked the Prophet about it, which the previous episode's card leaves unnamed. The speaker says he hopes to see Uthman in Paradise to see what such a man is like. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:17:30}
- Badr: the speaker says Uthman did not go out to [[event/battle-of-badr|Badr]], and that the Prophet had set out for the caravan, not for war, so staying behind was not desertion as it would be in other campaigns. Ruqayya was ill, and her son Abdullah was ill as well, and Uthman was their only support, so the Prophet told him to stay, saying, in paraphrase, that he would have a share like those who went out with him and would take his portion of the spoils like any other man. The speaker adds that his absence from Badr was among the things later promoted against Uthman by those he calls the people of the discord, who told ordinary people without saying that the Prophet had told him to stay (the transcript's clause on who was behind them is not written). Uthman stayed with Ruqayya until the Muslims' victory; when the Prophet sent [[person/zayd-ibn-haritha|Zayd ibn Haritha]] to Medina with the news, Ruqayya died at that same time. The Prophet gave Uthman his share, and Uthman says he sat weeping for Ruqayya. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:19:30}
- The speaker says Uthman and Ruqayya loved each other deeply and were in great harmony, so that a poet (not named) said they were the best pair a person had seen. He says the Companions' homes were full of love, and names as another example [[person/umm-sulaym|Umm Sulaym]] and her husband [[person/abu-talha-al-ansari|Abu Talha]] (the other examples in the transcript are garbled). He ends the passage with an application on obedience in the home, which is not written. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:20:30}
- The speaker says that when the Prophet found Uthman weeping and asked him why, Uthman said he wept for the loss of Ruqayya and for something else: the cutting off of his tie by marriage with the Prophet, which had given him kinship with him. The speaker adds that the Companions competed for kinship with the Prophet. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:22:30}
- As an example of that competition he tells that Umar ibn al-Khattab asked [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali]] to give him his daughter Umm Kulthum in marriage (the transcript calls her "your daughter"; the museum has [[person/umm-kulthum-bint-ali|Umm Kulthum bint Ali]]). Ali said she was young; Umar said no one knew her standing as he did and swore it, and Ali married her to him. The speaker then renders what he says Umar said in the mosque: that people might wonder at his marrying a girl, but he had done so because of what he heard from the Prophet, that every tie of lineage and of marriage will be cut on the Day of Resurrection except the Prophet's, and he had companionship with the Prophet and a tie through his daughter and wished to add another (the last lines are garbled). (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:24:00}
- Returning to Uthman, the speaker says the Prophet, moved by Uthman's loss of the tie (the transcript's line on what Uthman said is garbled), told him that if he wished he would marry him to [[person/umm-kulthum-bint-muhammad|Umm Kulthum]], her sister, and Uthman said yes. The speaker says the marriage was in 3 AH, that she stayed with him until 9 AH, and that she bore him no children; she died in 9 AH, and Uthman, who was gentle, pure and modest, wept bitterly. The Prophet then said to him, in paraphrase, that had he had another daughter he would have married her to Uthman. These dates are the speaker's. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:24:30}
- He gives the death of his son Abdullah as two years after his mother's, the child suffering until he died aged six (the cause the transcript gives is unclear and is not written). The speaker takes this before the battle of Uhud; the order is his. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:25:00}
- Uhud: Uthman went out with the Prophet and fought. The speaker says [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]] circled the Muslim army and came up the hill after those on it came down to take the spoils (the transcript's name for the hill and what Khalid then called out are garbled), and that the Muslims' ranks began to break. He says a man the transcript calls Abdullah ibn Qami'a (the spelling is the transcript's) came upon the standard-bearer [[person/musab-ibn-umayr|Mus'ab ibn Umayr]], who closely resembled the Prophet, cut off his right hand and then his left and killed him, and, thinking he had killed the Prophet, cried that he had killed Muhammad. Some Muslims, in confusion, thought the battle was over and began to leave it to go back and protect Medina, which the speaker calls a mistake made in that belief; Uthman was among those who went back, and the speaker says the people of the discord later held this against him. He says the verse revealed about those who turned back on the day the two armies met, saying that God pardoned them, answered this; the transcript attributes the reminder to a name that is garbled and the verse is not typed ([[passage/quran-3-155|Qur'an 3:155]] is the museum's reading). (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:27:30}
- Hudaybiyya: the speaker says the Prophet and the Companions set out intending the lesser pilgrimage, not fighting, because the Prophet had seen in a vision that he was circling the House with the Companions shaven or shortened, and he refers to the verse about entering the Sacred Mosque in safety, which is not typed ([[passage/quran-48-27|Qur'an 48:27]] is the museum's reading). At [[place/hudaybiyya|Hudaybiyya]] Quraysh stopped them, and the Muslims answered their messengers that they had come for the lesser pilgrimage only. The Prophet called Umar to go in and tell them so. Umar said that Quraysh knew how harsh he had been to them, that he had no one of his clan, Banu Adi, in Mecca to protect him, and that he feared he would not deliver the message, and proposed Uthman as a man better suited to be sent. The Prophet agreed and sent Uthman to tell them they had come for the lesser pilgrimage and to go to Abu Sufyan (the speaker's rendering). (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:29:30}
- The speaker notes that the Muslims were about fourteen hundred (the transcript says 1400) in the dress of pilgrims, not ready for war, so the mission was dangerous. Uthman went into Mecca and put the message to [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan]] and Quraysh. The speaker reflects that Uthman had not seen the Ka'ba for more than six years, perhaps ten if he counts the time in Abyssinia (the speaker's estimate). he was told that he might circle the Ka'ba if he wished (the transcript does not make clear whether the speaker is Abu Sufyan or Quraysh), and he answered, in the speaker's rendering, that he would not circle the House until the Messenger of God did. The speaker praises such following of the Prophet. When some Companions outside said, in congratulation, that Uthman could now circle the House, the Prophet said, in paraphrase, that even if he stayed in Mecca for years he would not circle until I did. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:32:00}
- Quraysh then decided to keep Uthman with them for a while, in honour, until they decided what to do, and word reached the Prophet that Uthman had been killed (the transcript falls into a repetition of numbers here, which is discarded). The speaker says the Prophet called the Companions to pledge themselves, to the death in the speaker's rendering, until they obtained justice for Uthman. Each man wanted to give his hand first; the Prophet struck one hand on the other and said, in paraphrase, that this was for Uthman, and the speaker takes it that the Prophet's hand for Uthman was better than the Companions' hands for themselves. He says the verses on God's being pleased with the believers who pledged under the tree and on the hand of God being above their hands were revealed ([[passage/quran-48-18|Qur'an 48:18]] and [[passage/quran-48-10|48:10]] are the museum's reading; not typed). When it became known that Uthman had not been killed and nothing had happened to him, the [[event/treaty-of-hudaybiyya|treaty of Hudaybiyya]] followed. The pledge is recorded in the museum as [[event/bayat-al-ridwan|Bay'at al-Ridwan]]. The speaker's remark on the value of Muslim blood, and his appeal that follows it, are not written. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:34:30}
- After a break the speaker turns to the expedition of Tabuk, called the expedition of hardship. He gives the reasons for the name: the heat in Medina was severe, the fruit was not yet ripe and the harvest not yet gathered, the Companions had no money, and the distance was very long, about a thousand kilometres by his figure. He says a verse names the hour of hardship and that it was revealed about the Prophet and the emigrants and Ansar who followed him in it ([[passage/quran-9-117|Qur'an 9:117]] is the museum's reading; not typed). People who wished to go and had no money to spend came to the Prophet in tears, and the army reached thirty thousand (the speaker's figure). The expedition is recorded as [[event/expedition-to-tabuk|the expedition to Tabuk]]. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:36:00}
- The speaker says the Prophet stood on the pulpit and called out: who will equip the army of hardship, and he will have Paradise. [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] brought all his wealth, which was not enough; [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] brought half of his; others gave what they had, down to a morsel of bread, and the army was still not equipped. When the Prophet called again, Uthman stood in the middle of the mosque and said, in the speaker's rendering, that he would give a hundred camels with their saddle cloths and packs (the speaker explains these as what is carried on the animal's back). The Prophet was glad and prayed good for him (the transcript's wording of the prayer is garbled and is not written), and then came down a step on the pulpit and called again. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:38:00}
- Uthman then pledged two hundred camels with their gear, and when the Prophet came down a third step and called again, three hundred. The speaker says the Prophet turned over (the transcript's word is unclear) and said, in paraphrase, that nothing Uthman did after this would harm him, and the speaker reads this as above. Uthman then pressed the Prophet for more and brought, as the transcript gives it, nine hundred and fifty camels more and fifty horses, "completing the thousand" (the speaker's reading of the total is not clear from the transcript); the transcript's list of gold and dinars that follows has garbled units and figures and is not written. The Prophet turned over in his hands what Uthman had laid before him and said, in paraphrase, may God forgive Uthman what he sent ahead and what he held back, and repeated that nothing Uthman did after this would harm him. The speaker says that Uthman alone equipped a third of the army, putting it at thirty thousand. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:42:00}
- A woman among the studio participants (not named in the transcript) comments on two points. First, sincerity: when Uthman was sincere in his intention to buy the well for the Muslims, she says, God made it easy for him to buy it for little and in an easy way. Second, spending: she says God links repentance with giving, and she cites the words of a verse in al-Tawba, which are not typed ([[passage/quran-9-104|Qur'an 9:104]] is the museum's reading); so, she says, whoever wants God to forgive and turn to them should give. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:43:00}
- A participant says that a group she belongs to plans four things drawn from the series so far: to help orphans, because the Prophet was an orphan; to be just with themselves and with people, after Umar; to be gentle in dealing with Muslims, after Abu Bakr; and to spend, after Uthman. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:46:30}
- Another participant links Uthman's spending at Tabuk to a saying that charity extinguishes sin as water extinguishes fire (paraphrase). What follows reads in the transcript as the speaker's own words (the transcript does not separate the voices): he recalls a similar saying, that charity extinguishes anger (the next word is unclear) as water extinguishes fire, and adds a second, given in his own words: that when someone gives charity from honest earnings, God receives it with His right hand and rears it for the giver, as one of you rears a foal, until it meets him on the Day of Resurrection as large as Mount Uhud, even if it was a single morsel. He also gives a third saying, that a person who gives charity so secretly that his left hand does not know what his right has spent is among those in God's shade. The speaker's own appeals to give regularly, which follow, are not written. None of the three sayings is named to a source in the recording. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:49:00}
- The last moments of the Prophet's life: the speaker says the Prophet died on [[person/aisha|Aisha]]'s chest, and she went out to the Muslims saying that the Messenger of God had died. He says the most distraught was Uthman, who was like a child, taken by the hand this way and that, and that the people greeted him and he could not answer. He says that Umar, himself shaken that day, went to Abu Bakr (the transcript reads "Abu Dhakr" for Abu Bakr; the museum reads Abu Bakr) and said he feared for Uthman, who seemed to have lost his awareness. The speaker concludes that Uthman loved the Prophet deeply and that the Prophet loved him and married him to two of his daughters and would have married a third to him had he had one. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:50:00}
- He closes with the saying, in paraphrase, that the Prophet asked whether he should not be ashamed before a man of whom the angels are ashamed, and says the angels' modesty before Uthman is reverence and high esteem, not embarrassment. He says the next episode will begin Uthman's caliphate after Umar. (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep13 00:51:00}

## Left out of this episode 13

Not written from episode 13: the speaker's appeal on obedience and angels in the home (00:20:30), his advice to those who face opposition at home beyond the one-line application above (00:05:30 to 00:07:30), his remark on how some people picture traders of a certain religion (00:13:00), his remarks on the value of Muslim blood, with an appeal for those living through present-day conflict, and his appeals to the rich to give to orphans, the sick and others in need, with the audience's remarks on current events (00:33:00, 00:38:00 to 00:38:30, 00:41:30 to 00:42:30, 00:43:30 to 00:46:00, 00:48:30 to 00:49:00); and the participants' remarks on present-day events, which the transcript does not separate by speaker. These are contemporary appeals and asides listed for the founder. Qur'an verses and the wording of sayings of the Prophet are not typed or reproduced as scripture; the sayings are paraphrased and marked as needing a source. Garbled lines are not written: the uncle's name (00:04:30), the rest of the account of the kunya (00:01:30 to 00:02:00), the comparison with Umar's conversion (00:03:00), the comparison with Suhayb (00:09:00), the speaker's clause on who was behind those who raised Uthman's absence from Badr (00:18:30), which is legible but charges a community and is listed for the founder, not written, the cause of Abdullah's illness and death (00:25:00), the hill's name and Khalid's call at Uhud (00:25:00 to 00:25:30), the speaker's lines at 00:26:30 on the flight from the battle, the name to whom the reminder about the verse is credited (00:27:00), the names in the Umar and Umm Kulthum story (00:23:00 to 00:24:00), Uthman's words to the Prophet about the lost tie (00:24:00), the units and figures for gold and dinars (00:41:00) and the second half of the speaker's remark at 00:42:30. The repetition loops at 00:21:00 and 00:32:30 are not speech and are discarded. The ages and dates in this episode are the speaker's: Uthman's marriage to Umm Kulthum in 3 AH and her death in 9 AH, Abdullah's death at six, the distance to Tabuk, and the army of thirty thousand.

## Episode 14: how Uthman ibn Affan became caliph: Abu Bakr's last testament, Umar's six and the pledge

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He says his purpose in the Uthman episodes is that listeners should come to love him deeply, and he says that all who knew Uthman agreed on two qualities in him, beauty of appearance and modesty. He reads the angels' modesty before Uthman as reverence for him and not embarrassment, and he calls modesty one of the greatest qualities of faith. The saying about the angels and the saying about the branches of faith are given in the speaker's rendering and are not sourced in the recording. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:05:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He regards the way the six were chosen and the way the caliph was then picked as a careful procedure, and he is struck that it was laid down by Umar while he was dying. He reads Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf's taking himself out of the contest as a way of removing any suspicion of self-interest, and he suggests, as his own reading, that most people leaned to Uthman because people tend to prefer the elder in such a position. The rules and the exchanges are the speaker's account and are not sourced in the recording. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:41:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** On Uthman's stand beside Abu Bakr's deathbed he stresses Uthman's sincerity and plainness, since he was himself a man of about sixty who might have hoped for the office. He says that Uthman wrote Umar's name out of fear for the community, and the speaker sets Abu Bakr's reply as a tribute to him. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:19:00}
:::

- The speaker recalls that the previous episodes began the series on Uthman ibn Affan, the first as a full personal introduction, and that the last told of his stands with the Prophet and above all his giving. He says his intention is that people should love him greatly: the third caliph, one of the ten given the good news of Paradise, and, as the transcript has it, the husband of the Prophet's two daughters (the line is partly garbled). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:01:00}
- He says that all who lived in Uthman's time agreed that he had two main qualities, one in his character and one in his looks: beauty and modesty. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:01:30} (needs_source: the claim that all his contemporaries agreed is the speaker's)
- A story the speaker tells to show his beauty at an advanced age. He says Uthman became caliph at seventy (the transcript's second figure is garbled). A boy of the Muslims tells that he was in the Prophet's mosque and saw an old man asleep with his shoes under his head, and, not knowing him, wondered whether any old man could be so handsome. When the man woke and asked who he was, the boy named himself; the speaker adds that the boy was poor and that Uthman was among the richest. Uthman asked him to wait, went into his house and brought a new garment suited to a child, dressed him in it and put a thousand dirhams in it. The boy went back to his father and found the money in the garment, and the reply that follows (its first words are garbled) names the man as the caliph of the Messenger of God, the Commander of the Faithful [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]], very handsome at seventy. (The transcript at first gives the name as Abdullah ibn Affan; the speaker means Uthman.) (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:04:00}
- He says many people have beauty of appearance but lack inner beauty, while Uthman had both. He recalls the saying, in paraphrase, that the Prophet asked whether he should not be ashamed before a man of whom the angels are ashamed, and says he longs to see Uthman; he reads the angels' modesty as reverence, not embarrassment. He then says that modesty is one of the greatest qualities of faith and recalls, in paraphrase, that faith has some seventy branches and modesty is one of them; he says the Prophet singled it out because whoever has modesty has the other branches. (The transcript's line comparing Uthman with another figure here is garbled and not written.) (status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep14 00:05:00}
- He announces that this episode begins with [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] as caliph after the Prophet's death and will tell how Uthman was chosen. Asking where Uthman was in Abu Bakr's two and a half years, he says Abu Bakr kept the senior Companions, the elders of Badr who had been the Prophet's close advisers, from going out to the fighting, and held six or seven of them near him. As an example he says that when the army of [[person/usama-ibn-zayd|Usama ibn Zayd]] was to set out (the transcript reads "Zayyad"; the museum reads Zayd), and Usama was, by the speaker's figure, eighteen, Abu Bakr asked him for leave to keep [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] as his adviser. The transcript renders Abu Bakr here several times by a word that reads "Mubarak"; the museum reads Abu Bakr.
