The speaker says Ibn Masud then said that he would hear and obey the Commander of the Believers and that if Uthman ordered him to come crawling he would come (the line on what happened to his Mushaf is garbled). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:39:00} (the account is the speaker's; needs_source)
- The third: that he exiled [[person/abu-dharr-al-ghifari|Abu Dharr al-Ghifari]] (the transcript writes "Abu Zali al-Ghifari", then "Abu Zabil", then "Abu Dharr al-Ghuthali" at 00:40:30, so the form of the name varies; the museum reads Abu Dharr) to "Rabda" (as the transcript has it; the museum reads al-Rabadha, with no link here as the reading is not certain). The speaker's account: Abu Dharr had a very strong, truthful tongue, and the Prophet is reported to have said that nothing under the sky was more truthful in speech than Abu Dharr; yet the Prophet told him, in the speaker's paraphrase, that he was weak, that office, in a partly garbled line, will be a disgrace and regret on the day of resurrection, that he loved for him what he loved for himself, and that he should not take command over even two people (the sayings are paraphrased; needs_source). The speaker says "weak" here means weak for command, not lacking in courage. In Uthman's time, he says, Abu Dharr saw much of what he disliked in people and took a severe line, saying that anyone who held wealth beyond his need for the day was a hoarder, and he recited the verse on hoarding gold and silver (not written). He went to Syria, where [[person/muawiya-ibn-abi-sufyan|Muawiya]] began to feel trouble coming and informed Uthman (the verb is garbled), who said to send him to him in the best way (the line is partly garbled). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:41:00} (status: tradition; needs_source)
- In the speaker's telling, Uthman welcomed him as a brother, said he had wanted him near the Companions and the Prophet's place and had feared for him from the ignorant, and that in Medina among the Companions he could say what he wished, whereas there it would be a problem, so he should stay. The transcript writes "Abu Bakr" for the one who wept, which the museum reads as Abu Dharr from the context; he said that if Uthman ordered him to come from Syria to Medina on his head or on his knees he would obey, that perhaps Uthman feared he would be a cause of fitna, and that he never would be. Uthman answered that if he wished to be near him he could stay; Abu Dharr wept again, and the transcript then puts in Uthman's mouth "you reminded me of the Messenger of God", followed by a first-person account in which the Prophet addresses the speaker as "Abu Dharr", so who is speaking in this report is not clear in the transcript; its gist, as the speaker gives it, is that Abu Dharr would live after the Prophet and that when he saw building rise at Mount Sal he should leave (a line that Abu Dharr would live, walk and die alone follows, and the transcript does not make clear whose words it is). Abu Dharr asked leave to go to a place the transcript writes "Ghurda" (the museum reads al-Rabadha, named earlier), Uthman offered him money, he refused and went alone, and he died in the desert, where, the speaker says, a tribe (the word describing it is garbled and is not read) buried him. The speaker says the words had passed between Abu Dharr and the Prophet the rest of the line is garbled and appears to clear Uthman of what was said. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:44:00} (status: tradition; the report is the speaker's retelling; needs_source)
- The fourth: that they called him "the burner of the Mushafs" and spread this between the cities (the line is partly garbled). The speaker answers by quoting [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] as saying, by God, that he did it in front of the Companions and that had Uthman not done it, he himself would have (the saying is as the speaker gives it and is not sourced). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:44:30} (needs_source)
- The next, he says, spread very widely: that he gave office to his relatives. He grants that it happened, and says the point to see is that Uthman was of Banu Umayya, which he calls the best-known and most numerous clan of Quraysh, with the most wealth and the most capable men, so that anyone choosing officials would use them, and that most of the Prophet's own officials were from Banu Umayya, not Banu Hashim or another clan (his claim, not checked). He adds that under Umar, Muawiya (the transcript writes "Muawiya ibn Abs Fin", which the museum reads as ibn Abi Sufyan) and [[person/said-ibn-al-as|Said ibn al-As]], both Umayyads, had been used, and says the issue is not a fault in Uthman but how people seized a chance (the line is partly garbled). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:46:00} (the speaker's account; needs_source)
- The fifth: that he gave much money to his relatives. He answers that Uthman did give Banu Umayya a great deal, and says, in a line whose attribution is garbled, that Uthman was the most dutiful of the Companions to his kin. He recalls that Banu Umayya were slow to accept Islam and at first among those who denied the Prophet, that Uthman was the only one of them to accept at the beginning ("a day came when I was a quarter of Islam", as the speaker gives his words, the same line as in episode 15), and that at [[event/treaty-of-hudaybiyya|Hudaybiyya]] the Prophet sent Uthman into Mecca because most of his own kin there were still not Muslim (the transcript's wording is partly garbled). He says Uthman stood and said, in the speaker's rendering, that whoever knew that he had given anyone among his relatives a payment from the Muslims' treasury should come and do to him as he wished, for he had given them only from his own wealth, as a rich man; the speaker adds that his aim was to bring the most influential clan of Quraysh to Islam. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:48:30} (the saying of Uthman is as the speaker gives it and is not sourced; needs_source)
- He says the man behind the fitna then turned to the strict, and went on to charges about the minbar: that Uthman sat on the Prophet's step. The speaker's account: the Prophet sat on the top step, Abu Bakr on the one below and Umar on the one below that, and when Uthman sat down he went back up to the Prophet's step; the speaker explains that if each caliph had kept going down the minbar would have vanished, and that Uthman feared the idea of the minbar would be dropped; and that as the community had widened the minbar had grown distant from the people, who needed to see it (the line is partly garbled). (The transcript names the man behind the charges "Abdullah ibn Saraq", which the museum reads as Abdullah ibn Saba from the context.) The speaker also says the Prophet had one call to prayer on Friday and Uthman made two, because Medina's Muslims had increased, so that people in the far places would come in time. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:51:00} (the speaker's account; needs_source)
- A further charge: that at [[place/mina|Mina]] Uthman completed the prayer instead of shortening it as the Prophet, Abu Bakr and Umar had done. He says some Companions came to him about it and that Uthman's reply, in the legible part of the line, gives as a place where he lived a name the transcript writes "Mana" (read as Mina) (the rest, including a mention of a marriage, is garbled and not written). The speaker's lesson is that this is a difference of branches on which two views are possible. He adds that [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdullah ibn Masud]] went in to Uthman and blamed him, then came out and prayed the full prayer in front of the people, saying a sentence the transcript writes garbled, and the speaker takes this as showing his care not to cause trouble. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:52:30} (the speaker's account; needs_source)
- The ninth charge, he says, was that Uthman missed Badr, fled on the day of Uhud and was absent from the pledge of Ridwan. He tells it through [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]]: a man came to him among the Muslims and asked whether he had been with the Prophet, and then whether Uthman was absent from [[event/battle-of-badr|Badr]], fled on the day of [[event/battle-of-uhud|Uhud]], and was absent from the [[event/bayat-al-ridwan|Pledge of Ridwan]]; Ibn Umar said yes to each and the man exclaimed "God is greatest", and Ibn Umar told him to sit and explained. Badr: Uthman was married to the Prophet's daughter and she was ill, and the Prophet told him not to go out with them and said he would have the reward and the share of those who went. Uhud: he says many Companions did the same when they thought the Prophet had been killed (the line is partly garbled), and the speaker adds that a Qur'anic verse says God pardoned them (not written). Ridwan: the lines giving the reason for his absence are garbled, and the speaker goes on to say that the Prophet held up his right hand saying that it was Uthman's hand, and struck it with his left saying it was for Uthman, and the speaker says the Prophet's hand for Uthman was better than the Companions' own hands for themselves. (the story is the speaker's retelling; the words of Ibn Umar are as he gives them; the transcript's description of the questioner's origin is unclear and is not written; status: tradition; needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:55:00}
- The last charge, he says, is that Uthman was a weak personality, and he answers with what is in the speaker's-view note above. He closes by saying he loves Uthman, that the Prophet gave him two of his daughters in marriage (the line is partly garbled) and said he was among the people of Paradise, and that he hopes the community will love him more; the next episode, he says, follows. He ends with the closing greeting. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep16 00:56:00} (needs_source: the speaker's outline of the next episode)

## Left out of this episode 16

Not written from episode 16: remarks at 00:29:30 to 00:30:30 on the people the speaker links with the fitna and a present-day political dispute; a question at 00:26:30 about whether Muslims now live through a fitna like that one; a garbled line at 00:22:30 to 00:23:00 containing a contemporary reference; the speaker's contemporary remarks at 00:34:30 on people in the twenty-first century who still believe the accusations; the repetition loops at 00:01:00, 00:10:00, 00:10:30 (within a line), 00:21:00, 00:25:30 and 00:43:30 and the stretched word at 00:47:30, which are not speech. Garbled lines are not written: a line on a defeated state turning from arms to plots (00:25:00), the lines on troop figures in the first recap (00:02:00), the names of Companions who died at 00:16:00 and the sum after Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf's death (00:15:30), the verdict lines on how Uthman disciplined Ammar and the other Companion and the Companion's name (00:36:30 to 00:37:00), the lines on what became of Ibn Masud's Mushaf (00:39:00), the fourth young Companion of the copying committee (00:08:00), the lines on the letters between cities (00:34:00), and the participants' words at 00:21:30 to 00:22:30 that are not clear. Qur'anic verses recited by the speaker and the participants, and the verse on hoarding, are not written. The sayings of the Prophet and the words given to Uthman, Abu Dharr, Ibn Masud, Ibn Umar and al-Hasan al-Basri are paraphrased as the speaker gives them. The dates, numbers and names in this episode are the speaker's.


## Episode 17: the end of Uthman ibn Affan: the besiegers, the refusal to fight or resign, the last days and the killing, as the speaker tells it

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He says the lesson of the fitna is not to take part in one: whoever joins in what others say, without understanding, may be laughed at as the simple Muslims were, and he adds that the fitna sifted the bad from the good and showed who was attached to God (the lines are partly garbled). He also stresses, from the story, that Muslims should verify news before acting on it. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:15:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He argues that [[person/abdullah-ibn-saba|Abdullah ibn Saba]], whom he again describes as a Jew from Sana'a who entered Islam, aimed less at the killing of Uthman than at ending the caliphate, so that the Muslims would have nothing left to unite them; he says this was the plan, and that the besiegers' groups were each steered towards a different candidate so that a quarrel over succession would follow. The account is the speaker's and has not been checked against the historical sources. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:18:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He says that those working against the community learned that open armed confrontation would not succeed with it, and that plots from within were therefore needed (the line is partly garbled); he places the letter found by the groups at this point. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:22:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He asks why Uthman would not fight, whether from weakness or fear, and answers through Uthman's own words in the episode: that he would not be the first to shed blood in the community of Muhammad, that he would not meet God with a Muslim's blood, and that he held to what the Prophet had told him. He calls Uthman's conduct the height of patience, truthfulness and courage in old age. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:41:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** On Uthman's refusal to resign he says that if every caliph were pressed to step down by those who disagreed with him, the caliphate would lose its standing and its worth. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:43:00}
- **Remarks the transcript reads as a studio participant's (opinion):** After the speaker's question on why Uthman would not fight, the transcript carries remarks in a different manner, which the museum reads as a participant's; they are not named. One says the fitna was a new situation for the community and for Uthman, and that the answers open to him would have led the community into civil war (the lines are partly garbled; the remarks on the capital and on later practice are not written). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:29:00}
- **Speaker's closing (opinion):** He says he loves Uthman very much and does not accept or believe the slanders against him, and that the next episodes will be on [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:55:00}
:::

- The speaker opens by saying this is the last episode, on the death of [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]]; he recalls that the last one dealt with the account of Abdullah ibn Saba and the accusations against Uthman, which he calls lies and fabrications, and says today he will take the story to the besiegers reaching Medina and the killing of the caliph ([[event/siege-of-uthman-35|the siege of Uthman, 35 AH]]; [[event/murder-of-uthman|the killing of Uthman]]). He says the aim of the series is to attach people to the Companions, and that Uthman's name recalls modesty, good manners, righteousness and spending. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:01:00}
- He recaps, as the speaker has it, that Abdullah ibn Saba was from Yemen, from Sana'a, entered Islam, and (the line is garbled) aimed to bring down the caliphate, not merely to remove Uthman or to turn people against the Ansar. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:03:30} (needs_source: the speaker's account of the man and his aim)
- On the man's course he repeats that he went first to Medina (the line on how it went is garbled), then said something about Syria (also garbled), then went to [[place/kufa|Kufa]] and [[place/basra|Basra]], where he set up men who plotted, then to Egypt, where he stayed. Of the three instructions he gave his followers: to call to good and forbid wrong in order to win hearts; to turn people against the governors; and to demand the removal of Uthman on the ground that the caliphate was not rightly his. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:06:30} (needs_source)
- On Kufa, he says the unrest was strongest there, because its governor was [[person/al-walid-ibn-uqba|al-Walid ibn Uqba]], whom he calls a great man. As he tells it, some young men of Kufa were punished for offences, agreed among themselves to go to Uthman and say that al-Walid had drunk wine, and swore to it; Uthman applied the penalty to al-Walid (the line is partly garbled), removed him and appointed [[person/said-ibn-al-as|Said ibn al-As]]. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:07:30} (needs_source: the whole account is the speaker's)
- When Said came towards Kufa, the speaker says, men came out against him with swords and said he would never enter as governor, at a place the transcript writes "al-Qudaysiyya" (read as al-Qadisiyya; not linked). They asked for [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ashari]]; Uthman sent him, and when they asked him to lead the prayer he agreed on the condition of hearing and obeying Uthman (the words are garbled and are read from the line), and they accepted. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:08:30}
- The speaker reads out a letter of Uthman to the people of Kufa after this, which he describes as very tender and says moved the righteous to tears and increased the anger of the rebellious; he names a transmitter the transcript writes "Sayf" (not identified; the line is garbled). As the speaker gives it in substance: that he had appointed over them the one they wanted, that he would give of his own standing for them, would not spare his patience or effort for their reform, and would grant whatever they asked in which God was not disobeyed, so that they would have no argument against him. The wording is paraphrased, not quoted. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:10:00} (needs_source)
- As the news reached Medina and the situation became serious, Uthman gathered the senior Companions to ask their advice, and they advised sending senior Companions to the cities. The speaker lists, in the transcript's forms: "Ammar ibn Yasir" (the transcript writes "Ammal ibn Yasir"; the museum reads Ammar; not linked) to Egypt; al-Qa'qa' "ibn Umar" with Muhammad ibn "Salama" (the museum reads al-Qa'qa' ibn Amr and Muhammad ibn Maslama; not linked) to Kufa; "Abdullah ibn Amr ibn al-Khattab" (not identified) to Syria; and [[person/usama-ibn-zayd|Usama ibn Zayd]] to a place the transcript writes "al-Baqtara" (garbled; not identified). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:11:00}
- He says the Companions found the man moving with great cunning and keeping his people quiet (the lines are partly garbled), and that they came back saying nothing was said against Uthman. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:11:30}
- Uthman then wrote to all the cities, as the speaker gives it: that the pilgrimage season was near, and whoever had a grievance, or had been insulted or struck, should come to him and take his right from the governors or from Uthman himself, who would pay the cost of the journey from his own pocket. The speaker says no one came. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:12:00} (needs_source)
- He says the rebels were few, not a large number, and that Uthman gathered the governors: [[person/muawiya-ibn-abi-sufyan|Muawiya ibn Abi Sufyan]], [[person/amr-ibn-al-as|Amr ibn al-As]], a governor the transcript writes "Abdullah ibn Sad ibn Abi al-Sarih" (the museum reads Abdullah ibn Sad ibn Abi Sarh; not linked), Abu Musa and Said ibn al-As, and asked them what the matter was. Muawiya answered, as the speaker has it, that it was something creeping by night that they could not see, and the speaker adds that nobody then knew Abdullah ibn Saba was behind it. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:13:00}
- Said ibn al-As advised searching each city for those who led the movement and striking off their heads. Uthman answered that this was the opinion, but that he would not do it, for the Messenger had made a covenant with him and he was patient on it until he met him. The speaker says he will come back to this covenant. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:14:00} (needs_source)
- The speaker says the matter stood so until Shawwal of 35 AH, when people began to move in all the Muslim lands for the pilgrimage. He says Abdullah ibn Saba stirred up three lands, Kufa, Basra and Egypt, not Syria or the Hijaz (the reason he gives is garbled). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:14:30}
- As the speaker tells it, they set out as if for the pilgrimage but turned towards Medina instead of Mecca, intending (the line is partly garbled) to make Uthman step down or to kill him. He says some among them were hot-headed or deceived (the line is partly garbled; a characterisation of some of them is left out). He says each land sent its men in four groups so that they would not seem many (the figures he gives are garbled and not written), and that they reached the edge of Medina at the end of Shawwal and the beginning of Dhu al-Qada. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:16:30}
- He says they camped a mile or two from the edge of [[place/medina|Medina]] and sent men to learn its news and whether it held an army. Many Companions had gone on the pilgrimage; those still there, he says, included [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], [[person/zubayr-ibn-al-awwam|al-Zubayr]] and [[person/talha-ibn-ubaydullah|Talha ibn Ubaydullah]], and al-Hasan and al-Husayn. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:17:30}
- The speaker says the groups were made to lean to three candidates: the men of Kufa to al-Zubayr (the transcript writes "al-Zubayda" and later "Zaghay"; the museum reads al-Zubayr), those of Basra to Talha and those of Egypt to Ali. Each group sent a delegation to its favoured man (the wording of the offer is garbled and not written). He says that Ali, then al-Zubayr, then Talha each answered in the same words, that the people knew this army was cursed on the Prophet's tongue, and told them to go back (the closing words are garbled and not written). The saying attributed to the Prophet is the speaker's report and is paraphrased. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:20:00} (needs_source)
- He says they reported back that Medina was firm and that they had seen Ali take up a sword among a group of people (the lines are garbled). People began to turn back; he says Ali could not go out, feeling the matter might still have remains and the fitna would not end so easily. The Egyptians, he says, reached a place whose name is garbled in the transcript, on the boundary between the Hijaz and Egypt, and the Iraqis dispersed, each group going back by its own road. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:21:30}
- Then, the speaker says, a man carrying a letter came to each group, saying he had a letter from the commander of the believers to his governor in Egypt, and to his governor in Kufa. The letter was opened and, in the speaker's account, was from Uthman to Abdullah ibn Sad ibn Abi Sarh (the transcript's form is garbled) and ordered that when these men came back their hands and legs be cut and they be put to death on palm trunks in front of the people. He says this was one of the plots he describes; the lines that follow, on a seal, are garbled and not written. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:23:00} (needs_source)
- Next, he says, the groups entered Medina and called out the takbir, alarming its people, and the siege of Uthman's house began near the Prophet's Mosque; in his words they spread corruption. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:24:30}
- He says Uthman gathered the senior Companions and asked their advice; fighting the besiegers was advised (the speaker's line is garbled as to who said it), and Uthman refused. [[person/zayd-ibn-thabit|Zayd ibn Thabit]] came to say that the Ansar were outside under arms and asked to go out and fight; Uthman said no by God. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:25:00} (needs_source)
- Muawiya wrote from Syria, as the speaker has it, inviting him to Syria where he would be safe; Uthman replied that he would not leave the neighbourhood of the Messenger until his neck was cut, and that he would not have it said that he fled to Syria. Muawiya then offered troops from Syria to protect him, and Uthman answered that he would not frighten the people of Medina with troops from outside; Muawiya said he would then be killed, and Uthman's reply to that is garbled. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:26:30} (needs_source)
- The speaker says Ali was preparing Medina for a confrontation, and that three courses were put to Uthman, the transcript naming [[person/al-mughira-ibn-shuba|al-Mughira ibn Shuba]], Ali and Talha ibn Ubaydullah in a garbled line: to put on the pilgrim's garb and declare he was leaving for the pilgrimage, to go to Syria, or to let them fight. He answered, as given: his answer on the pilgrim's garb is partly garbled and, where legible, rests on a characterisation of the besiegers, so it is not written; going to Syria meant leaving the neighbourhood of the Messenger and having it said that Uthman fled and left the Prophet's city alone; and he would never fight. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:28:00} (needs_source)
- The speaker says Uthman answered the Companions and governors who asked him to use force in a letter; as given in substance, every matter has a door and the door of this one is gentleness and conciliation, force is not how this fitna is entered, and if the fitna was to open the door, it would open (the closing lines are garbled and are not written). He says Ali then called God to witness (the words are garbled). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:31:30} (needs_source)
- He says this was the first twenty days of the siege; as the pilgrimage approached it became harsher until water and food were kept from Uthman, and Uthman asked them by God for water and then for food (the replies are garbled). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:32:30}
- The speaker gives these as the last 21 days before the killing and gives Uthman's age at this point as 88 (the transcript later reads 82 and 98: the figures are not consistent and are not endorsed here). He says a woman the transcript calls "Umm Habiba" tried to bring him water, that her mule's girth was cut and she fell and nearly died, and that Ali carried water to try to reach him; whether some got in is garbled. The museum does not link "Umm Habiba", the transcript giving no further name. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:33:30} (needs_source)
- He says Uthman refused to have any blood shed for him: that he would not meet God with the blood of a Muslim or be a cause of it. Young men of the Companions, he says, were at the door, al-Hasan ibn Ali, al-Husayn ibn Ali, [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]] and [[person/abdullah-ibn-al-zubayr|Abdullah ibn al-Zubayr]], and Uthman told them to go home and said he wanted no one to defend him. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:35:00} (needs_source)
- He says Uthman kept going out to lead the prayers until he was pressed on the way; one man threatened to kill him and to set him on a mangy camel and drag him through the streets of Medina. Another time, leaning on the staff the Prophet had used when preaching, a man from the besiegers took it from his hand and broke it over his thigh; as the speaker tells it, a splinter entered the man's foot and it was eventually cut off. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:36:30} (needs_source)
- The speaker then gives Uthman's appeals to the Companions, asked of those present (the first question is garbled; he asks whether Ali, al-Zubayr and Talha are there and addresses Aisha as Mother of the believers), each answered "yes": that the Prophet had said whoever bought the well of Ruma and gave its water to the Muslims would have the like in Paradise, and he bought it with his own money and now was kept from its water ([[place/bir-ruma|Bir Ruma]]); that whoever enlarged God's house would have a house in Paradise, and he bought land and enlarged the Prophet's Mosque and was now kept from praying in it; and that when the Prophet asked who would equip the army of hardship and no one stood, he did, and the Prophet said nothing Uthman did after that would harm him ([[event/uthman-funds-the-army-of-hardship|Uthman funds the army of hardship]]). These are reports in the speaker's telling; their wording is paraphrased. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:38:30} (needs_source)
- Continuing, the speaker has Uthman ask al-Zubayr whether he heard the Prophet say that Uthman was his beloved in this world and the next, and one of the people of Paradise (al-Zubayr answers that he witnesses it); ask Talha (the transcript writes "Tawha"; the museum reads Talha; not linked) whether he heard the Prophet say, as each took the hand of the one beside him, that Uthman was his companion in this world and the next; and ask whether they heard the Prophet on [[place/uhud|Uhud]], when it shook, say that on it were a prophet, a truthful one and two martyrs, which Uthman takes as Abu Bakr the truthful, Umar a martyr and himself the second martyr. They said yes. The speaker comments on the strength and patience of a man of that age. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:40:30} (needs_source)
- He has Uthman remind Aisha that he used to write the revelation for the Prophet, and ask the besiegers on what ground they would kill him, naming apostasy and an unreturned wrong (the lines are partly garbled). A warning that they would not love one another after his death appears in the first person in a line whose attribution (Uthman's own words or a saying he reports) is unclear. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:41:00} (needs_source)
- When the Companions again offered to defend him, he said, as given, that he would not be the first to shed blood in the community of Muhammad after the Messenger, and asked what he would say to his Lord if Companions were killed over him (the lines are partly garbled). He kept calling on people to stay in their homes. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:42:00} (needs_source)
- As the speaker has it, he sent a messenger to ask the besiegers what they wanted; a partly garbled reply is read as asking him to give up the caliphate and let the people choose another. He refused, saying, as read from the line, that he would not take off a garment that God had put on him, and that the Messenger had made a covenant with him to which he would hold until he met him. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:42:30} (needs_source)
- Before the last moments the speaker recalls what he calls prophetic signs. As he tells it, Abu Musa al-Ashari was with the Prophet when Abu Bakr, then Umar, then Uthman asked to enter; the Prophet told Abu Musa to let each in and give him news of Paradise, and Abu Bakr sat on the Prophet's right, Umar on his left and Uthman, finding no place beside them, on the opposite side; the speaker reads this as a sign about where they would be buried (an intervening phrase is garbled). For Uthman the line adds something about what would befall him, which is garbled, and his answer is read as "I will be patient, God willing". {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:44:30} (needs_source: a report given without a source in the episode)
- On Abdullah ibn Umar's account, as the speaker has it, the Prophet described a fitna in which none would be on the right except a veiled man, Ibn Umar asked whether it was this man, was told yes, and lifted the veil to find Uthman. The transcript has the man "not" killed wronged, which is unclear; the speaker's later gloss is that Uthman was the one wronged. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:45:30} (needs_source)
- On Aisha's account (the attribution is read from a garbled phrase), as the speaker gives it, Uthman came to the Prophet's house, the Prophet told her to step aside and spoke with him privately at length, three times asking whether he understood and had grasped it, then a line says that the ears heard it and the heart and mind kept it, the speaker of the line (Uthman or the narrator) being unclear; the Prophet told him to be patient until he met him. The speaker reads this as pointing to patience; whether he takes it for the covenant he promised to explain at 00:14:00 is not clear in the transcript. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:46:30} (needs_source)
- He also recalls that the Prophet said to Uthman that God might clothe him with a shirt and that, if people sought to make him take it off, he should not; the speaker says Uthman remembered this when asked to resign. Al-Hasan and al-Husayn are told to stay at Uthman's door, the transcript not making clear by whom, and the speaker says he stayed until Thursday night, the eve of Friday. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:47:30} (needs_source)
- He places this in the days after the pilgrimage and gives the date of the killing as 18 Dhu al-Hijja. He says the besiegers began to sense that Muawiya was setting out from Syria with an army, al-Qa'qa' from Kufa and a governor the transcript writes "Abdullah ibn Abi al-Sarah" from Egypt, to protect the caliph; the line that follows is garbled. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:48:30} (needs_source)
- He says Uthman fasted often in the manner of David, alternating days, and stood much of the night reciting the Qur'an (the transcript's age at this point, 98, conflicts with its other figures). On the Thursday night, the speaker says, he saw in a dream (the subject of the line is garbled; his replies are addressed to the Messenger) the Prophet asking whether they had made him thirsty, hungry and besieged, and each time he said yes, and asking whether he would like to fast the next day and break his fast with them, the Prophet, himself, Abu Bakr and Umar, and he said yes. The speaker reads this as Uthman's joy at the thought of being with them. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:50:00} (needs_source: a dream reported in the speaker's account)
- In the morning, as the speaker tells it, he called a servant and said he had once pulled the man's ear years before and asked him to pull his now; the servant hesitated, Uthman swore him to it, he did, and Uthman told him to pull harder, since retribution in this world is lighter than in the next. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:50:30} (needs_source)
- Then, he says, they set fire to his room and the house; Companions and young Muslims came towards the house and Uthman cried to them to leave them and swore them to go home; only al-Hasan ibn Ali and Abdullah ibn Umar are named as staying. His wife, whom the transcript does not name, offered to uncover her hair so that the besiegers would be ashamed, and he answered that he would rather be cut to pieces than have a lock of her hair uncovered. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:52:00} (needs_source)
- The besiegers entered, he says, while Uthman was fasting and reading from the Mushaf, and he says a phrase from a Qur'anic verse was on his lips (the verse is not written or linked, the speaker not naming it). The first to enter struck at him and his hand was cut off; the speaker has him say that this hand was the first to write revelation for the Messenger (the rest of the line, on a letter form, is garbled). His wife came to defend him and her fingers were cut off; another man struck his shoulder with iron, and he said words of praise and glory to God. The speaker says the Mushaf was stained with blood (the closing lines are garbled and not written). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:53:30} (needs_source)
- He says Uthman was killed on Friday, 18 Dhu al-Hijja, and buried at night in al-Baqi ([[place/al-baqi|al-Baqi]]), the transcript naming Ali in connection with the burial in a garbled line and saying that people feared or that someone tried to stop it; the tomb is there still. He adds a personal remark that a visitor feels calm and gladness at it. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:54:30} (needs_source)
- He relates that Ali stood at the grave at night, wept, and asked God that he and Uthman be among those of whom God says that He removed what was in their hearts of rancour, brothers on couches facing one another (the verse is not written or linked, the speaker not naming it). He closes the episode and the series on Uthman and says the next will be on Ali ibn Abi Talib. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep17 00:55:00} (needs_source)

## Left out of this episode 17

Not written from episode 17: contemporary and political remarks at 00:04:00 to 00:05:00 (events of the early twentieth century attributed to the same plot, the name of a modern political figure, and a list of present-day nationalities used as an appeal for unity); characterisations of the besiegers as haters of Islam or as only claiming it (00:15:00, 00:27:30, 00:36:00); a sweeping statement at 00:52:30 about how the killers later died, left out as a claim the museum would have to source; and the participants' remarks at 00:29:00 to 00:30:00 on the capital and on later practice. Garbled lines not written: the first recap's agreement with a foreign state (00:03:00); the troop figures (00:16:00 to 00:16:30); the offer made to each candidate (00:19:00); the lines at 00:20:30 to 00:21:00 on Ali and the groups; the seal on the letter (00:23:00); the words of Ali before God (00:31:30); what water reached the house (00:33:30); the burial (00:54:00); Uthman's age (88, 82 and 98 appear). Repetition loops discarded: 00:20:30 (within a line), 00:23:30, 00:55:30. The wording of the Qur'anic phrases quoted and of the sayings of the Prophet is not reproduced. The dates, numbers and names in this episode are the speaker's.

## Episode 18: Ali ibn Abi Talib begins: his lineage, family, marriage to Fatima, coming to Islam, ages and appearance, as the speaker tells it

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He says the aim of the programme is to bring the Companions close to present life and remove the feeling that they are distant figures who neither laugh nor speak, and that loving them has a great benefit: as he gives the Prophet's saying (the wording is garbled), a person is gathered with the one he loves, and he hopes that he and his audience will be with them in Paradise. He says it is a hope that one may find oneself higher than one's deeds deserve, and be told it was for loving a Companion. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:02:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** On Ali's qualities he says that whoever speaks of him speaks of courage, chastity, high wisdom and steadfastness to the end (some of the other words in his list are garbled), and that he accepted Islam before puberty, before the pen was applied to him, so that the whole of a long life afterwards was giving and sacrifice without a pause. He holds him up as a model for young men and hopes a young listener will say that he too will be one of Islam's honoured sons like Ali, contrasting this with resolve that flags after a few weeks or after Ramadan (the lines are partly garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:04:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He says that in his twenties Ali was already a battle commander and a bearer of the banner of Islam, and gives a supplication asking for martyrdom in God's path after a long life (the transcript does not make clear whose words he is quoting; the lines that follow apply it to Ali). He says Ali lived 63 years, the same as the Prophet, and then died a martyr, and he draws the lesson that one should want to live long to serve Islam rather than to be done with life at once. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:07:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** From the story of the marriage he draws the lesson that parents should not make heavy demands: the Prophet accepted little, he says, and he asks parents to accept little and marry their children early rather than let sins spread in society (the lines are partly garbled). He gives the Prophet's saying that God has bound Himself to help the one who marries seeking chastity (the wording is read from a garbled line), and tells parents not to push up what is asked of a daughter. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:32:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** On Ali's naming of sons Abu Bakr, Umar and Uthman, he says this is a point of great importance: he reads it as showing that there was no ill feeling in Ali's heart towards the three caliphs before him, and says no one can claim otherwise after it (the lines are partly garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:40:30}
- **Remarks the transcript reads as a studio participant's (opinion):** Three sets of remarks in a different voice, which the museum reads as a participant's because the speaker invites the room to offer something on the names: first, a remark on the sons named Muhammad (whether there is a Muhammad the elder; the speaker answers that the first is called al-Akbar, the elder); second, that Umar named three of his sons Abd al-Rahman, the elder, the middle and the younger, and, asked why, said, as the speaker repeats it, that the Prophet taught that the dearest names to God are Abdullah and Abd al-Rahman and that he loved the name Abd al-Rahman; third, that among the names are two of prophets (Muhammad and Yahya), some of the Prophet's wives (the transcript gives the number as four and then as three, naming Maymuna, Khadija and Umm Salama) and some of the great Companions (Abu Bakr, Jafar and Uthman; one more name is garbled). The lines are partly garbled and the speakers are not named. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:39:30}
- **Speaker's closing (opinion):** He says today's episode is a long introduction to Ali and that the next will begin with Ali's stands in the Prophet's raids and in the Prophet's lifetime. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:53:30}
:::

- The speaker opens with praise and says the programme has run more than a year and is nearing two, speaking of many Companions. He recalls that its name is taken from words of Bilal when he was dying: his wife said a word of grief and he answered that she should not say it but say that tomorrow they would meet the beloved, Muhammad and his companions. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:00:30}
- He says there were two aims and gives the first (the second is not clear in a legible line): to tie people to the Companions and to them as models for present life, and to remove the barriers between us and them. He says he tries to bring the Companions close to the audience's own world. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:01:30}
- He introduces today's subject as [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], whom he calls very dear and, perhaps among young men, very much loved. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:02:30}
- He reports a saying that Ali was the first son of Islam, since he was among the first to accept it and did so as a boy; the transcript gives the speaker of the saying as 'سلمان مالك' (not identified and not linked). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:04:00}
- He sets out the plan. He says the series took up the Rightly Guided Caliphs in turn: Abu Bakr (the number of episodes is garbled), Umar seven episodes and Uthman seven. For Ali he plans several: today an introduction to him, in looks, age and one more word that is garbled; next his stands with the Prophet; then, a tender one, Ali and his wife Fatima and the Prophet, how the household lived; then Ali under Abu Bakr, Umar and Uthman, until the last episode, on his caliphate and his death as a martyr. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:05:30}
- He lays out the name and lineage in full: Ali ibn Abi Talib ibn [[person/abd-al-muttalib|Abd al-Muttalib]] ibn [[person/hashim-ibn-abd-manaf|Hashim]] ibn Abd Manaf, and notes that, spread out, it is exactly the Prophet's own line; they meet, through Ali's father, in the grandfather Abd al-Muttalib. (The transcript's first form of Abd al-Muttalib at 00:07:30 is garbled; it is legible from 00:08:00.) (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:08:30}
- He adds that Hashim, whom he calls the Prophet's first grandfather, was buried in [[place/gaza|Gaza]], and that many people there still call it 'Gaza of Hashim'. He says the name Hashim, 'the one who crumbles', is because he used to crumble bread into broth for the people in the pilgrimage season, and that the name he gives as his real one is garbled in the transcript and is not written. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:08:30}
- On [[person/abu-talib|Abu Talib]], Ali's father, he says Abu Talib is known by his kunya, that his real name is said to be Imran or Shayba, and that he was 35 years older than the Prophet; so, as the speaker has it, he was 75 when the Prophet was 40 at the start of the mission, and died in the tenth year of the mission at 85. The figures are the speaker's. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:09:00}
- He says Abu Talib stood beside the Prophet for ten years: Quraysh came to him every day or two asking him to hold back his nephew, and he calmed them with a few words, being the head of Quraysh in honour and standing; while he was there, the speaker says, no one could harm the Prophet's body or try to kill him. He adds that Ali came from Banu Hashim, from the house of Abd al-Muttalib, from the house of Abu Talib, and was raised in the Prophet's house. Abu Talib, he says, did not want to accept Islam (the line is partly garbled) but defended the Prophet strongly. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:10:30}
- He tells that Quraysh brought Abu Talib a young man, whom the transcript names 'Umar ibn al-Walid' (the museum reads Umara ibn al-Walid; not linked), and asked him to keep and raise him as his own son and give up his nephew; Abu Talib refused, saying in effect that he would not hand over his own son to be killed (the lines are partly garbled). Then, the speaker says, Abu Talib felt things had grown too hard and that he could not protect the Prophet any longer, and told him so. The Prophet answered, as given: by God, uncle, if they put the sun in my right hand and the moon in my left to make me leave this religion, I would not leave it until God made it prevail or I died in it. The Prophet then wept, from the loneliness of having no one beside him, and Abu Talib said he would never give him up (the words that follow are garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:12:30}
- He draws from this a lesson on steadfastness: some girls, he says, grow anxious when they feel their friends are turning on them, and some young men lose their faith when they go a fortnight without the religious talk they are used to, and he holds up the Prophet as the one who stayed firm even if the whole world left him (the lines are partly garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:12:00}
- On Abu Talib's death he says the Prophet asked him to say the testimony of faith so that he might (the words are garbled) speak for him before his Lord, and that Abu Talib refused, saying by God the Arabs would not say that he left the religion of his fathers. The speaker says that most scholars of the community hold that Abu Talib died without having accepted Islam, and that he was 85. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:13:00}
- On Ali's mother, [[person/fatima-bint-asad|Fatima bint Asad]], he says she was also of Banu Hashim (the lines naming her father are partly garbled), so that Ali meets the Prophet in a grandfather through his father and in another through his mother; he says Ali was the first Hashimite born of a Hashimite father and mother and the first caliph from Banu Hashim, setting this beside Umar, from Banu Adi (the clan names he gives for the others are cut or garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:14:00}
- He says Fatima bint Asad accepted Islam, migrated and died in [[place/medina|Medina]]; the Prophet prayed over her, lowered her into the grave and gave his own shirt to shroud her. He gives words of the Prophet on her goodness to him, after Abu Talib's, in his childhood (the lines are partly garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:14:30}
- On Ali's kunyas he says Abu al-Hasan comes from his eldest son, [[person/hasan-ibn-ali|al-Hasan]], and that Abu Turab was given him by the Prophet. As the speaker tells it, the Prophet went to Fatima's house and asked where Ali was, and found him asleep in the mosque with his hand under his cheek and dust covering his body; the Prophet sat beside him, wiping the dust off and saying 'Get up, Abu Turab; get up, Abu Turab', and Ali was very pleased and it became his kunya. He says there had been a disagreement between Ali and Fatima, as between any married couple (the lines on it are partly garbled), and that he will come to how a household should handle such things in a later episode. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:17:00}
- On Ali's brothers he says there were four sons of Abu Talib, with ten years between each (the line is partly garbled), in the order Talib, [[person/aqil-ibn-abi-talib|Aqil]], [[person/jafar-ibn-abi-talib|Jafar]] and Ali; the lines on Talib are garbled and not written. Aqil, he says, accepted Islam late and held it well, and was a man of standing; he says about seven of Aqil's sons (the count is partly garbled) died as martyrs with [[person/husayn-ibn-ali|al-Husayn]] at [[place/karbala|Karbala]], and that Aqil's family stood strongly with Ali and his sons. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:18:30}
- He says Jafar, the flier, was martyred at the battle the transcript writes 'مأتمة' (read by the museum as Mu'ta; not linked) after his right hand and then his left were cut off, and that the Prophet said, as given, that he now saw him in Paradise flying with two wings, roaming in it, eating its fruit and drinking from its rivers. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:19:00}
- He goes on with a sister, [[person/umm-hani-bint-abi-talib|Umm Hani]] (the connecting words are garbled, but he says a little later 'these are his brothers and sisters'). She accepted Islam very early, like Ali and Jafar, and, he says, the Prophet was in her house on the night of the Isra and Mi'raj. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:21:00}
- He tells of her at the [[event/conquest-of-mecca|conquest of Mecca]]: some men of Quraysh whose blood the Prophet had made lawful took refuge in her house, and told her they would be killed (the lines, which say they bore witness to the faith, are partly garbled). Ali came, the speaker has it, and said the Prophet had made their blood lawful; she said she had given them her protection and that she was a believer; a line then has someone say by God he would not leave the two alone (the verb is plural and the speaker unclear, so none is named), and she answered that by God she would not let him be until she went to the Messenger. She went to the Prophet on the day of the conquest and said she had protected the two, and he said, as given, 'We have protected whom you protected' (the transcript's form of address is 'يا أمه', read by the museum as Umm Hani). The speaker takes from it how far the Prophet respected a woman's word (the lines after that are garbled and are not written). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:20:30}
- On his wives and children he says Ali's first wife was Fatima, the Prophet's daughter ([[person/fatima|Fatima]]), and that he took no other while she lived; after her death he married many women, some of whom died and some he divorced, and had very many children. He says he married her between the second and third year of the Hijra, after Badr (the transcript writes 'البدو', read as Badr) or after Uhud (written 'أخذ', read as Uhud), and gives his age at the marriage as 18 (the lines around it are garbled); the museum notes that this does not match the 23 he gives later for Ali's age at the Hijra, and neither is endorsed. He says she bore him four: [[person/hasan-ibn-ali|al-Hasan]], [[person/husayn-ibn-ali|al-Husayn]], whom he calls leaders of the youth of Paradise, and two daughters (the transcript names Zaynab; the other name is garbled). He says she lived with him five years in one line and, at 00:24:00, gives a total of about seven and a half or eight years; the figures are not consistent and are not endorsed. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:24:00}
- He says the Prophet, though he bore revelation, the Qur'an, the community and the call, would rise when Fatima came in and kiss her between the eyes. He tells of her coming to him on the last day of his life: she felt that he had not kissed her and wept; he called her near and said something that made her weep more (the wording is garbled), then called her again and said she was the first of his family to follow him, and she laughed. Asked afterwards what he had said, she told it (the first part of her answer is garbled). The speaker says she died six months after the Prophet. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:24:00}
- He tells how Ali came to propose. Two men had asked the Prophet for Fatima, first [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr al-Siddiq]] and then a man the transcript names 'Uthman ibn al-Khattab' (the lines that follow name 'Abu Bakr and Umar', so the museum reads Umar ibn al-Khattab; not linked), and the Prophet said she was young; the speaker reads this as a matter of age. A girl or servant (the transcript's word is 'jariya', unnamed) then told Ali that people had proposed and he should go too; he said he could not, having nothing to marry with. The speaker adds that Abu Talib's house was burdened with dependants and was not rich (the lines comparing it with another clan are not written). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:26:00}
- As the speaker has it, the girl told Ali that if he went the Prophet would marry him to her, and he went; he sat before the Prophet and stayed silent from awe. The Prophet smiled and asked why he had not come to propose for Fatima; Ali answered (the word is garbled). The Prophet asked if he had anything to marry her with, and Ali said by God no; then he named one possession (the word is garbled in the transcript; the museum cannot read it) that he said was worth no more than 400 dirhams. The Prophet said he would marry him to her for it. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:27:30}
- He notes that the Prophet's word for the wedding night is the night of 'building', not of 'entering', and calls it a lovely name. As the speaker tells it, when the night came the Prophet told them to do nothing until he came, then performed ablution, took some of the water and sprinkled it on Ali's face and on Fatima's, who was very bashful; he taught that a groom should put his hand on the bride's forehead, say the name of God and ask God for her good and the good of what she was made for, and seek refuge from her evil and the evil of what she was made for, and (the line is garbled) pray two rak'as. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:31:00}
- He gives an account from the Ansar, as he tells it, that when they entered the house of Ali and Fatima they found only a piece of mat, a pillow stuffed with palm fibre, a water jar and a cup. He says this was the wedding of the Prophet's daughter and says he will return to the household in a later episode, which he calls one of extraordinary love. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:32:30}
- After Fatima's death, he says, Ali married many women, among them [[person/asma-bint-umays|Asma bint Umays]], who he says had married [[person/jafar-ibn-abi-talib|Jafar ibn Abi Talib]], then [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr al-Siddiq]] after each died, and then Ali (the lines on her children are garbled). He gives the number of Ali's children first as 19 sons and 19 daughters and, a minute later, as 14 sons and 19 daughters 'by most accounts'; the figures are not consistent and are not endorsed. He reads out names in this order, saying he has not given them all: al-Hasan, al-Husayn, Zaynab, Umm Kulthum, Muhammad, Abu Bakr, al-Abbas, Uthman, Jafar, Yahya, Awn, Umar, Muhammad al-Awsat, Umm Hani, Maymuna, Umm Kulthum, Fatima, Khadija and Umm Salama. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:34:30}
- He tells an anecdote of his own about a man he met in Mecca a long time ago, who named all his children Abd (servant) because he loved the sense of being a servant of God, so that each name would remind him of it. He says Ali named two sons Muhammad, 'the elder' and 'the middle', out of love for the Prophet, and that the elder, well known as [[person/muhammad-ibn-al-hanafiyya|Muhammad ibn al-Hanafiyya]], was from a wife other than Fatima. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:37:30}
- He tells of a quarrel between Muhammad ibn al-Hanafiyya and al-Hasan, who were upset with each other. Muhammad sent him a message asking him to come and greet him; al-Hasan's reply is partly garbled; it questions being asked to come and greet while they were at odds. Muhammad explained, as the speaker has it, that the Prophet said the better of two is the one who begins with the greeting, and that al-Hasan was better than he, and his mother better than Muhammad's mother, so he should begin the greeting that he might remain the better. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:38:00}
- He draws two notes from the names. The first is that Ali named sons Abu Bakr, Umar and Uthman. The second is that a daughter named Fatima was, as he has it, born to another wife and not to Fatima the Prophet's daughter, and he sees in the other wife's accepting the name a gentle touch (the lines are partly garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:41:00}
- On Ali's coming to Islam he says scholars differ on when, some saying at seven years old and some at nine, and gives the firmest and closest view as ten. As background he says that when Ali was five or six the Prophet and [[person/abbas-ibn-abd-al-muttalib|al-Abbas]] went to Abu Talib, who had many children and found it hard to provide for them, and each offered to take a son: al-Abbas took Jafar and the Prophet took Ali, the youngest. So the Prophet was raised in Abu Talib's house, he says, and Ali in the Prophet's. Ali stayed with the Prophet through the start of the mission, and Jafar with al-Abbas until Jafar accepted Islam, al-Abbas's own Islam coming late. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:42:30}
- He tells of Ali's acceptance. He says Ali, aged ten, came in on the Prophet praying with [[person/khadija|Khadija]] in the first days after revelation began (the line is partly garbled) and asked what this was; the Prophet said it was God's religion, which He had chosen for His servants, that He had chosen him as a messenger to them, and that he called Ali to bear witness that there is no god but God and that he is God's messenger. Ali said it was something he had not known before and asked time to speak to Abu Talib; the Prophet said that if he would not accept he should keep it hidden. Ali asked for time to think, spent the night thinking, and in the morning asked the Prophet to repeat what he had said; then he made the testimony and, in words that are partly unclear, agreed to keep it hidden. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:44:00}
- He says Ali kept his Islam hidden until Abu Talib saw him praying and asked what he was doing. Ali said he had believed in God and followed His messenger and was firm and invited him to it (the transcript gives the form of address as 'Abu Bakr', which the museum reads as a mis-transcription of 'father', since Abu Talib answers next; not linked). Abu Talib said, as given: my son, as for what you do, obey Muhammad and stay with him, for he will lead you only to good; as for me, I cannot leave the religion of my fathers. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:45:30}
- He gives an account from a man the transcript writes 'عكي في الكندي' (garbled; not identified and not linked) of the time before Islam: he went to Mecca to buy clothes and food and sat with al-Abbas ibn Abd al-Muttalib looking at the Kaaba. A man came and faced the Kaaba and stood; a boy came and stood at his right; then a woman stood behind them; the man bowed and the boy and the woman bowed, and he prostrated and they prostrated. The visitor said it was a great matter; al-Abbas agreed and said it was a new religion, that the man was his nephew Muhammad ibn Abdullah, the boy his nephew Ali ibn Abi Talib and the woman Khadija bint Khuwaylid; and he said by God he knew no Muslim on earth but these three. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:47:00}
- From this the speaker says Ali was the first of the boys to accept Islam, before puberty, before the pen was applied, before any sin, lie or wine. He cites a saying he gives to 'Malik' (the transcript's form; not identified and not linked) that revelation came on a Monday, Khadija accepted on the Monday and Ali on the Tuesday. He adds that there is a difference over who accepted first, Abu Bakr or Ali, and that each of them may have believed himself the first without knowing of the other (the reason he gives about secrecy is garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:48:00}
- He gives the ages of Ali's life as he counts them. Ali was five when the Prophet, then 35, took him in, and ten when he accepted Islam, so thirty years younger than the Prophet; he was 23 at the [[event/hijra|Hijra]], 25 at [[event/battle-of-uhud|Uhud]], and 30 at Khaybar (the transcript writes the name garbled, 'تخيب', and the lines about the number of fortresses and of Jews are partly garbled and not written). He says Ali was 33 when the Prophet died and 58 when he took the caliphate, that his caliphate lasted four years and nine months, and that he died at 63. The figures are the speaker's and are not checked. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:49:30}
- He turns to Ali's appearance. He first recalls the earlier caliphs' looks (the lines on whom he describes, and how, are garbled and not written). For Ali he says, with no source named, that he was neither tall nor short but of middle height among men, close to the Prophet's height (the figures he lists are garbled). They say, he reports, that his face was like the moon, round and bright, that his smile was as if one looked at the Prophet's smile, and that his walk was exactly the Prophet's. He describes strong arm and leg muscles, thick hair on the chest and shoulders and thick hands, and says he was among the strongest of people. He says the next episode will bring his feats. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep18 00:53:00}

## Left out of this episode 18

Not written from episode 18: contemporary remarks and asides outside the subject of the series, namely the comparison of people drawn to other civilisations, values and persons as models (00:01:00); the speaker's personal anecdote on naming his own son (00:04:30, garbled); an aside on how some people fear that Islam restricts women (00:20:30 to 00:21:00, garbled); the comparison of two clans in wealth, authority, religion and honour (00:25:30 to 00:26:00, partly garbled; a characterisation of groups); the appeal on the cost of marriage that names present-day nationalities (00:28:00); a remark on school education and the thinking of young people (00:44:00 to 00:45:00); the speaker's picture of how devout people are imagined (00:50:00 to 00:51:00, garbled); and a comparison with people who go to the gym (00:53:00). Garbled lines not written: the second aim of the programme (00:00:30); the names of people the speaker hopes to meet in Paradise (00:02:00); the rest of the list of Ali's qualities (00:02:30 to 00:03:00); the real name of Hashim (00:08:30); what Fatima said to the Prophet about Ali (00:16:00); the lines on Talib (00:18:00); the first words the Prophet said to Fatima (00:23:00 to 00:23:30); a figure of 15 (00:24:30) and a figure of about 26 (00:24:00), whose meaning is unclear; the possession Ali named (00:27:30); Ali's height figures (00:52:00); and the speaker's list of the earlier caliphs' looks (00:51:00). Repetition loops discarded: 00:17:30 and 00:28:30. The wording of the sayings of the Prophet and of the words given to the Prophet, Abu Talib, Ali, Fatima and others is paraphrased and marked as needing a source, not reproduced. The dates, ages, numbers and names in this episode are the speaker's, from a machine transcript that has not been listened to; the speaker's views on Abu Talib's faith and on Ali's naming of his sons are his own, and no other view is given in this episode.

## Episode 19: Ali ibn Abi Talib's stands with the Prophet, from the secret call to Khaybar, as the speaker tells it

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He holds Ali up as a model for young men: he was thirty when the Prophet died, being thirty years younger, and had already done great deeds in his twenties; and he calls young people to aims higher than doing well at school and going out.
