Whoever speaks of courage, chastity, purity (doing no great wrong or major sin and staying firm in obedience), worship (night prayer, daytime fasting, the Qur'an and truthfulness), steadfastness to death in God's obedience, or inner and bodily strength, he says, must name Ali ibn Abi Talib (some words in his list are garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:02:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** Character is built from childhood; he says people have a muscle they never train, the brain, so a young man reaches twenty-five or thirty with a weak level of thinking, and he calls young men and women to develop their thinking and to feel able to affect things and change the course of history (the lines are partly garbled). He recalls that the Prophet employed Ali in a dangerous task before he was twenty, and that 'sayyidna Zayd' (the transcript's form; the museum reads Zayd and does not link, since no father's name is given) led an army at sixteen. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:07:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** No one should despise a small deed for Islam; whoever does something for his religion tastes its sweetness and God helps him to more. His example is teaching three children to read the Qur'an, and he says many became influential after starting with a small deed (the lines are partly garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:13:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** Trust in the Prophet's words: he gives the saying that whoever prays the dawn prayer in congregation is in God's protection for that day, and the saying of a remembrance recited morning and evening that protects its reciter that day (the wordings are paraphrased), and the story of a man the transcript names 'Abu Dhi'a' (Abu Hurayra may also be named in the preceding line; garbled, not linked) who, asked about his house while far away, answered it would not burn because the Prophet had taught him a saying he repeats every morning. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:20:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** Whoever leaves something for God is compensated with better, even if after a while: one who leaves work or profit seeking God's obedience (paraphrased); he calls his audience to deal with the Prophet's sunna with that trust, as Ali did. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:20:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** A sentence he asks his audience to memorise: faith begins with saving the self and ends with saving society; he takes it to mean that the one whose own faith is true then works to better those around him. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:28:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He says speaking of bearing the banner is not a call to war; bearing the banner today is for a young man to excel in his college and work, to benefit his household and society, and to be the best at using the tools of his time. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:29:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** Islam, for him, is a religion of peace; he says the Prophet was keen on a period of calm at Hudaybiyya because people come to Islam in an atmosphere of quiet, while its enemies' interest (the speaker's characterisation) is that the tension remain; and he gives a saying he attributes to the Prophet on the names dearest to God (Abdullah and Abd al-Rahman) and the most hated (Harb and Murra; the wording is paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:41:00}
- **Speaker's closing (opinion):** He hopes each young listener leaves resolved to be the Ali of his college, university and neighbourhood, and that Muslims be the best in every field, the best inventor, engineer and doctor; Ali was about thirty at Khaybar, he says, and the next episode continues the stands with the Prophet. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:53:30}
:::

- The speaker recalls that the last episode was an introduction to [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], his coming to Islam, his qualities and his appearance, and that today's follows his stands with the Prophet station by station through the sira; Ali was thirty-three when the Prophet died (the figures are the speaker's). {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:00:30}
- He recalls the bodily strength he ended the last episode with: the heavy muscles of arm and leg, and he gives a saying, unattributed, that if Ali gripped a man's arm it was as if he had gripped his very soul, so that he could not breathe. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:03:00}
- Ali accepted Islam at ten, he says, and stayed in the Prophet's house learning; two or three years later the verse came down ordering the Prophet to warn his nearest kin (the verse's wording is not written; the transcript gives it garbled). The Prophet gathered Banu Hashim and addressed them one by one to save themselves from the Fire, naming Fatima bint Muhammad, his aunt [[person/safiyya-bint-abd-al-muttalib|Safiyya]] and his uncle [[person/abbas-ibn-abd-al-muttalib|al-Abbas]]; then he asked who would believe in this, and they were silent, and a boy of twelve, Ali, stood up and said he believed in him (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:04:00}
- When the harm to the Prophet and his companions grew, he says, the Prophet began meeting the Companions secretly at the house of [[person/al-arqam-ibn-abi-al-arqam|al-Arqam ibn Abi al-Arqam]], and [[person/abu-lahab|Abu Lahab]], his uncle, followed him about Mecca telling people he was mad and not to believe him. The Prophet needed a trusted, sharp young man to bring him those who wanted Islam, and the task, the speaker says, required reading people and guarding against anyone who might mean to kill the Prophet or the Muslims; Ali, not yet twenty, did it, receiving would-be converts and guiding them to the Prophet at al-Arqam's house, and worked at it for thirteen years (the figures are the speaker's). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:07:00}
- He gives the account of the coming to Islam of [[person/abu-dharr-al-ghifari|Abu Dharr al-Ghifari]], which he says al-Bukhari tells in detail: Abu Dharr sent his brother to Mecca to ask about the Prophet and he came back with not enough news, so Abu Dharr went down to Mecca himself with a waterskin holding food and water (the description is partly garbled); the situation was fearful, since whoever asked after the Messenger of God was harmed, so he sat by the Kaaba asking no one. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:08:30}
- He continues: Ali passed by him and asked whether he was a stranger; he said yes, and Ali, about sixteen, said 'come, let me host you'; he hosted him that night without asking him anything, and likewise the second night, and asked him on the third. Abu Dharr bound him by oath to tell no one, then told him he had come seeking the Prophet of the end of time; Ali said he would guide him to him, and told him to walk behind him, and if he saw him stoop to pick something from the ground he should know someone was lying in wait and turn aside, meeting him at the house; so he brought him to the Prophet and he accepted Islam at his hands (the dialogue is paraphrased). The speaker remarks on Ali's sharpness and caution as a teenager, and that Abu Dharr, though older, followed his plan. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:11:30}
- On the night of the [[event/hijra|Hijra]], he says, the Prophet charged Ali to sleep in his bed, telling him that Quraysh had sworn to kill him. He describes the plan as the speaker understands it: the Prophet would not be seen leaving at night but would walk out among them by day and they would not see him (the speaker calls it a miracle), while Ali would lie in his bed covered with the Prophet's own cloak so that the watchers, peering in, would take him for the Prophet; Quraysh had picked the bravest young man of each clan, each with a sword (the lines are partly garbled); and the Prophet had told Ali to be at ease, they would not harm him (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:17:30}
- He gives Ali's own account of that night, at twenty-three: that he never slept a calmer night in his life, because the Prophet had set him at ease (paraphrased); and the speaker says that when the watchers saw the place they did Ali no harm (the lines are partly garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:21:00}
- The Prophet charged him a second task: not to follow him until he had returned the deposits to their owners. The speaker remarks that those who wanted to kill the Prophet still entrusted him with their goods and found no one else in the town to trust, and asks, as an opinion, how a religion of such faithfulness can be called a religion that hates people (the speaker's opinion; no group is named). Ali, he says, returned every deposit and then left Mecca on foot. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:23:00}
- He describes the walk from Mecca to Medina until Ali's feet swelled, his toenails fell off and they cracked and filled with blood; he reached [[place/quba|Quba]] before the Prophet entered Medina and hid. The Prophet asked for him, and was told he could not walk, so he went to him, saw the swollen feet that could not be set on the ground, spat into his hand and wiped over Ali's feet (paraphrased); the speaker passes on that Ali never complained of his feet again until he was killed as a martyr. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:25:00}
- On the pairing of the Emigrants and the Helpers (see [[event/brotherhood-of-muhajirun-and-ansar|the brotherhood]]) the speaker gives two reports: one that the Prophet paired himself with Ali, and another, weakened by some scholars — he names al-Hafiz Ibn Kathir — that Ali was paired with another Ansari Companion, [[person/sahl-ibn-hunayf|Sahl ibn Hunayf]]. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:26:00}
- Then [[event/battle-of-badr|Badr]], with Ali twenty-five. Before the armies joined, he says, three of Quraysh's greatest came out for single combat: the transcript writes their names 'عد بن ربيعة وشيبة بن ربيعة والوليد بن عد بن ربيعة', read by the museum as [[person/utba-ibn-rabia|Utba ibn Rabi'a]], Shayba ibn Rabi'a (not linked) and al-Walid ibn Utba (not linked). Three of the Ansar, of the Aws and the Khazraj, answered first, and Quraysh said they wanted their own people, of the Emigrants. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:26:30}
- The Prophet called three: the transcript writes 'أبو عبيدة بن الحاجة ابن عبد المطلب' (read by the museum as Ubayda ibn al-Harith ibn Abd al-Muttalib; not linked) and 'عنب بن عبد المطلب' (read by the museum as [[person/hamza|Hamza ibn Abd al-Muttalib]]) and Ali ibn Abi Talib; the speaker draws the lesson that the first the Prophet put forward to sacrifice were his own kin, not strangers. Hamza quickly killed his opponent and Ali killed al-Walid; Ubayda and Shayba exchanged blows until both fell wounded, and Ali and Hamza finished Shayba and carried Ubayda. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:27:30}
- The Prophet, he says, laid Ubayda on his thigh as he was dying, and Ubayda asked whether he was pleased with him; the Prophet raised his hand and bore witness that he was pleased with him (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:28:00}
- The speaker adds, having forgotten it in its place, that the bearer of the Muslims' banner at Badr was Ali; and at [[event/battle-of-uhud|Uhud]] the Prophet was the battle commander and [[person/hamza|Hamza]] the field commander, and when [[person/musab-ibn-umayr|Musab ibn Umayr]], the banner-bearer, was killed, Ali became the banner-bearer — so he carried the banner in the first two battles. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:29:00}
- At Uhud, he says, the enemy's banner was carried by Banu Abd al-Dar, eleven men; Hamza killed one after another up to the fourth (a fifth line has the garbled name 'ibn Abi Tariq', not identified); then Ali took over and dealt with those who came forward, and when the ninth came out [[person/zubayr-ibn-al-awwam|al-Zubayr ibn al-Awwam]] begged leave of Ali and went and killed him. The speaker says this rivalry between Ali and al-Zubayr recurred, and promises another instance in a later battle (the lines are partly garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:32:00}
- When the forty archers disobeyed (the number is as he gives it), he says, the army was routed, the Prophet's tooth was broken and the blood flowed; about twenty stayed with the Prophet, Ali among them. Ali was struck with many wounds, which the transcript reads as about sixteen (the wording is garbled; the number is the speaker's), so that when the battle ended the Prophet searched for him among the slain because of the severity of his wounds, and found him. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:32:30}
- In a raid he mentions after Uhud without naming it clearly, he says a man came out calling himself 'Abu al-Qasim' (the transcript's form; not identified and not linked); Ali answered 'I am he whom my mother named Haydara', that is, lion, and killed him. Asked why he had not taken his armour, he said that when the man was killed his private parts showed and he was shy to look at them; the speaker adds that it is said one of the reasons God honoured Ali's face is that he never looked at a private part (he passes on 'it is said' as it stands). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:33:30}
- At the end of Uhud, he says, when the Prophet's face was running with blood, Ali brought water and poured it on the face and the wound only bled the more (in Ali's own telling, as he relays it); so Ali brought a pebble, set it in the fire and pressed it to the Prophet's wound, and the bleeding stopped. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:34:00}
- Then the [[event/battle-of-the-trench|battle of the Trench]] in year five, with Ali about twenty-eight (the reckoning is the speaker's): after the trench was dug, a man the transcript calls 'Amr ibn Wadd' (read by the museum as Amr ibn Abd Wudd; see [[event/duel-of-amr-ibn-abd-wudd|his duel]]) came out of the polytheists, described by the speaker as the bravest of the Arabs, never beaten in single combat. He crossed the trench on his horse to the Muslims' side and called for a duel, and the Companions were silent; Ali rose three times asking leave, and the Prophet told him to sit, for it was Amr ibn Abd Wudd, and the third time gave him leave (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:37:00}
- The speaker goes on: Ali went down to him with his sword, the Companions watching and the Prophet praying. Amr asked who he was and he said Ali ibn Abi Talib; Amr said his father had been his friend and he did not wish to kill him, and Ali answered that he wished to kill him, and offered him the choice between testifying that there is no god but God and that Muhammad is His messenger, or having his head cut off (paraphrased). Amr grew angry, dismounted, hamstrung his horse and struck it, and they joined in fierce combat; the dust rose until nothing could be seen, then a takbir was heard, and the dust parted on Ali with Amr ibn Abd Wudd's head in his hand, which he threw before the Prophet (the lines are partly garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:39:00}
- The speaker repeats here the same point as in the earlier duel: Ali, asked, answered that when he killed his opponent he saw his private parts uncovered and turned his face away (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:39:30}
- Ali was present at [[event/bayat-al-ridwan|the pledge of al-Ridwan]] and at the [[event/treaty-of-hudaybiyya|treaty of Hudaybiyya]], he says; there is no stand in the sira without Ali in it. He was the scribe of the treaty: when the Prophet dictated the basmala, [[person/suhayl-ibn-amr|Suhayl ibn Amr]] said they knew neither al-Rahman nor al-Rahim and asked that 'in Your name, O God' be written, and the Prophet had Ali erase and write it; when he dictated 'Muhammad, Messenger of God', Suhayl said that had they known he was the Messenger they would have followed him, and asked for his name and his father's, and when the Prophet told Ali to erase it Ali refused, so Ali showed him the place and the Prophet erased it himself (how the erasure was done is garbled in the line). The speaker remarks on Ali, twenty-nine, writing the treaty. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:41:30}
- Then [[event/battle-of-khaybar|Khaybar]]: the Prophet went out with about four thousand, and in [[place/khaybar|Khaybar]] were about ten thousand fighters in five or six fortresses on the hills, with food, drink and arms for a year (the figures are the speaker's and are unchecked). The siege began with the fortress of al-Na'im and lasted fourteen or fifteen days without an opening, and spirits flagged. On the thirteenth day the Prophet was taken by the shaqiqa (the speaker explains it as the one-sided headache that takes half the head, and gives as its cause the blow to the Prophet's helmet at Uhud; the lines are partly garbled) and could not lead the Muslims. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:43:30}
- The Prophet gave the banner to [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], who fought and returned without an opening, and the next day to [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], who returned the same. The Prophet then said he would give the banner the next day to a man through whose hands God would open it, who loved God and His messenger and whom God and His messenger loved (paraphrased). The Companions spent the night hoping and offering themselves; the speaker gives Umar's saying that he never wished for command except that day, and a Companion the transcript names 'Ra'id' (garbled; not identified and not linked) who presented himself, and the Prophet smiled at him and passed on. The speaker says those words restored the army's spirit (he reads it as raising morale). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:45:30}
- After the dawn prayer the Prophet asked where the banner was, then where Ali ibn Abi Talib was; they said he was suffering in his eye (an inflammation so he could hardly see). He was brought, and the Prophet spat into his hand and wiped over Ali's eyes; Ali recounts, as the speaker relays it, that he never complained of his eye again until old age overtook him, and no longer knew which eye had been the sore one. The Prophet gave him the banner and told him to take it and open the fortress, God would open it for him, and not to turn back (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:47:30}
- Ali mounted, he says, then wanted to clarify the bounds of the mission; not turning — obeying the Prophet's 'do not turn back' — he turned the horse itself around and asked the Prophet on what grounds he should fight the people. The Prophet told him to fight them until they testified that there is no god but God and that Muhammad is His messenger (paraphrased), and ordered him to call them to God first, telling him that God guiding a single man through him was better for him than red camels (the speaker explains it as prized red camels; the wording is paraphrased). The speaker remarks on Ali's joining a critical mind that clarifies the mission with complete obedience to the command. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:50:00}
- Ali reached the gate of the fortress of al-Na'im, he says, and a man of the Jews came out and asked who he was; he said Ali ibn Abi Talib, and the speaker relays that the man called to his people, 'You are overcome, by Him who sent down to Moses', because it is written in their Torah that the conqueror of Khaybar is named Ali (the speaker's report alone; needs_source). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:50:30}
- Ali fought and his shield fell (or was struck away; the line is garbled), so he shielded himself with the fortress's door and fought with it — the speaker attributes the report to [[person/ibn-hisham|Ibn Hisham]] and all the sira accounts — until God opened it to him, then threw it down. He gives a man the transcript names 'Abd al-Samad' (garbled; not identified and not linked) who went with others to turn the door over and could not; and the speaker asks whether it was a miracle, God's support, Ali's strength, or both together. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:51:30}
- Then a commander of the Jews' forces came out, whom the transcript names Marhab (see [[person/marhab-al-yahudi|Marhab]]), calling himself the great champion; Ali fought him, saying in the course of it that he was the one his mother named Haydara (paraphrased), and killed him; his brother al-Harith came out and Ali killed him; and when the third came out, [[person/zubayr-ibn-al-awwam|al-Zubayr]] begged leave of Ali and fought and killed him. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep19 00:52:00}

## Left out of this episode 19

Not written from episode 19: the names of the three Ansar who first answered the call to single combat at Badr, which the transcript does not give (00:26:30); and a line on a rivalry between Ali and al-Zubayr in 'another raid' that is not named (00:32:00); the speaker's list of what he hopes to see of Muslim excellence in the sciences and professions (00:52:00 to 00:52:30, part written into the closing). Garbled lines not written or read with caution: the name of the raid at 00:06:00 ('ghazwat al-darda'; not linked); the description of Abu Dharr's waterskin (00:08:00); the name of the boy who believed (00:03:30 to 00:04:00, clear from context as Ali); the name of the fifth banner-bearer at Uhud, 'ibn Abi Tariq' (00:31:00); the duelist 'Abu al-Qasim' and the name of his raid (00:32:30); the owner of the unburnt house, 'Abu Dhi'a' (00:19:30); the Companion 'Ra'id' (00:45:30); the man who could not overturn the door, 'Abd al-Samad' (00:51:30); the number of Ali's wounds at Uhud (00:32:00, garbled); the Khaybar figures (the four thousand and the ten thousand, the fortresses and the days; the speaker's figures, unchecked); and how the Prophet erased the name in the treaty (00:41:00). The wording of the verse, the Prophet's sayings and the reported dialogue is not reproduced but paraphrased and marked as needing a source. The claim of a Torah writing naming the conqueror of Khaybar (00:50:00) is the speaker's report alone and is unverified. Repetition loops discarded: none noted. The dates, figures and names in this episode are the speaker's, from a machine transcript that has not been listened to.

## Episode 20: the rest of Ali ibn Abi Talib's stands with the Prophet, from Khaybar to the Prophet's death, as the speaker tells it

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** His title for the episode is 'we want to love Ali'; he cites a saying he attributes to the Prophet to Ali, that only a believer loves him and only a hypocrite hates him (the wording is paraphrased), and takes love of Ali as a scale by which a person weighs his own faith, not mere fondness for a Companion. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:04:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** The youth of the religion: all of Ali's stands with the Prophet happened between his tenth and thirty-third years, so the religion was carried on the shoulders of young men; he calls each listener to 'do something', to wake those around him, and to show his religion in a lovable appearance that draws people, not a conduct that drives them away. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:18:00}
- **A report the speaker passes on:** He quotes 'the Imam whom we love' (the transcript's form; the museum reads the Imam Ahmad ibn Hanbal, an uncertain reading) that no Companion's virtues were transmitted with sound and good chains as Ali ibn Abi Talib's were. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:02:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** Zeal for the honour of Islam: commenting on Umar's wishing to be the banner-man at Khaybar, he draws a lesson for young people in keenness on the honour of serving the religion, and asks today's generation where such desire has gone. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:11:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** He contrasts a society whose members compete to foster orphans and kin (as Ali, Jafar and Zayd disputed over Hamza's daughter) with one where each man disowns his relative's children, and asks which of the two societies is safer and better to live in. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:26:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** How the Prophet composed hearts: he dwells on the Prophet's words to each of the three (Ali, Jafar and Zayd), and calls young men to pray in the mosques in congregation rather than at home, and asks his listeners what they expect the Prophet will say to them when they meet him. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:31:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** The pure laugh that comes from a heart that loves God and His Messenger is the sweetest and most wholesome of laughs; he reads Ali's date-stone joke with the Prophet in this light. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:50:00}
- **Speaker's closing (opinion):** He announces that the next episode covers Ali's character and his relation with his wife Fatima, and the episode after it Ali's position in the caliphates of Abu Bakr, Umar and Uthman, then Ali's own caliphate. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:50:30}
:::

- The speaker opens by greeting his listeners on the approach of Ramadan, saying that thanking God for worship is more of it, and gives a supplication he attributes to the Prophet asking blessing in Rajab and Sha'ban and to reach Ramadan (the wording is paraphrased); he hopes the episodes of the Rightly Guided Caliphs will be finished by the start of Ramadan. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:01:30}
- He recalls the two previous episodes (the introduction to [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] and his first stands with the Prophet) and announces that today he finishes the stands and then enters the house of Ali and Fatima (with their children al-Hasan, al-Husayn, Zaynab and Umm Kulthum), though he in fact leaves the house for a later episode. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:02:30}
- With the audience he reviews the earlier stands: Ali accepted Islam at ten, so that it was said he is the only one who can be called a son of Islam (the sayer of this is not named); and he was raised in the Prophet's house as the Prophet had been raised in his father Abu Talib's house, which the speaker reads as the depth of their closeness. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:05:00}
- He recalls Ali's sleeping in the Prophet's bed on the night of the [[event/hijra|Hijra]], covered with his cloak, and his age that night: the transcript gives '28 years' in one place and '23' in another (garbled; last episode he said 23); and he repeats Ali's saying that he never slept a calmer night (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:06:00}
- He repeats that Ali was the Prophet's support in Mecca for thirteen years, sensing out those who wanted Islam and bringing them to the Prophet at the house of [[person/al-arqam-ibn-abi-al-arqam|al-Arqam ibn Abi al-Arqam]], and briefly retells Ali's hosting of [[person/abu-dharr-al-ghifari|Abu Dharr al-Ghifari]] for three nights and then guiding him to the Prophet (told in episode 19). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:08:00}
- He repeats that Ali carried the Muslims' banner at [[event/battle-of-badr|Badr]] aged twenty-five, and that at [[event/battle-of-uhud|Uhud]] he was, in the speaker's account, commander of the right wing (a garbled line; the transcript's 'ghazwat akhun' is read by the museum as Uhud), then became banner-bearer after the banner-bearer was killed; the transcript writes the bearer's name 'Musa ibn Amr', read by the museum as [[person/musab-ibn-umayr|Musab ibn Umayr]]. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:09:00}
- He retells the siege of [[event/battle-of-khaybar|Khaybar]] with the figures he gave last episode: thirteen days of siege, ten thousand men armed inside fortresses with a year's provisions, and the Muslims fourteen hundred (the speaker's figures, unchecked); then the Prophet gave the banner on the thirteenth day to [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and it was not opened for him, and the next day to [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] and it was not opened for him, so the Prophet said his famous saying about giving the banner the next day to a man who loves God and His Messenger and whom God and His Messenger love, by whose hands God would open it (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:10:00}
- He retells the night the Companions each hoped for the banner: Umar said, he reports, that he never aspired to command except that day, hoping the Prophet would bear witness that he loved God and His Messenger and that God and His Messenger loved him (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:11:00}
- He retells that Ali was brought with sore eyes, hardly able to see, and the Prophet applied his saliva to them so that they healed until he did not know which had been the ailing one (paraphrased); then he gave him the banner and ordered him to advance and not turn back; Ali walked until he could no longer see the Prophet, then turned (without turning his face, the speaker noting the exactness of his obedience) and asked on what he should fight the people, and the Prophet told him to fight them until they bore witness that there is no god but God and that Muhammad is His Messenger, and that if God guided a single man by him it would be better for him than the world and what is in it — in another telling, than red camels (all paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:13:30}
- He retells that a Jew of the fortress asked Ali his name and, hearing 'Ali ibn Abi Talib', told his people they were defeated, since they had in the Torah that Khaybar's conqueror would be named Ali ibn Abi Talib (the speaker's report alone, unverified), yet they did not accept Islam; and that Ali's shield fell from him as he fought so he took the fortress door and used it as a shield; the transcript says seven men tried after the battle to overturn the door (the number is garbled). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:15:00}
- He retells the duel of the [[event/battle-of-the-trench|Trench]]: Amr ibn Abd Wudd (the transcript's 'Amr ibn Wadd' and 'Amr ibn Ma'd', read by the museum as Amr ibn Abd Wudd al-Amiri as in episode 19) came out challenging the Muslims; Ali asked leave three times before the Prophet allowed him; then the long duel, the rising dust and Ali's takbir, and he returned with the man's head to the Prophet (paraphrased; told at length in episode 19). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:17:00}
- He repeats that it was Ali who wrote the treaty of [[event/treaty-of-hudaybiyya|Hudaybiyya]] with [[person/suhayl-ibn-amr|Suhayl ibn Amr]]. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:21:00}
- Before the [[event/conquest-of-mecca|conquest of Mecca]] he relates that the Prophet concealed his destination from the Companions so as to surprise Quraysh and spare blood in Mecca, and that a man of the Companions wrote a letter to Quraysh's leader [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan]] telling him the Prophet was preparing to fight them, and sent it with a woman (the transcript writes the writer's name 'waradha', garbled; the museum reads [[person/hatib-ibn-abi-baltaa|Hatib ibn Abi Baltaa]] without certainty, and the woman's name is not given); Gabriel came down and told the Prophet, who sent two of the youth of Islam after her, Ali ibn Abi Talib and [[person/zubayr-ibn-al-awwam|al-Zubayr]] (the transcript's 'al-Zubayh'; the museum's reading), and they caught her; she swore she had no letter, and Ali said neither Gabriel nor the Messenger of God had lied, and ordered her to produce it; she drew it from her braided hair and he took it back to the Prophet (the dialogue is paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:23:30}
- Returning from the [[event/umrah-of-qada|umra of qada]] (a year after Hudaybiyya, in his telling), he relates the story of [[person/hamza|Hamza]]'s daughter (her name is garbled in the transcript; her mother in his telling is Salma bint Umays, sister of [[person/asma-bint-umays|Asma bint Umays]], Jafar's wife): Ali took her by the hand and placed her with Fatima, then three men disputed her fosterage — Ali saying she was his cousin's daughter, [[person/jafar-ibn-abi-talib|Jafar]] saying his wife was her maternal aunt, and [[person/zayd-ibn-haritha|Zayd ibn Haritha]] saying she was his brother's daughter (by the brotherhood the Prophet had made between him and Hamza); the Prophet judged that the maternal aunt stands in the mother's place and joined her to Jafar's household (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:26:30}
- He relates that Ali grieved at losing the fosterage of his uncle's daughter, so the Prophet walked to him and told him he was of him and he of him (paraphrased), then walked to Jafar and told him he resembled him in looks and character, and to Zayd that he was his brother and his freedman (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:27:30}
- He mentions the coming down of the purification verse on the people of the house (its wording is not written): the Prophet gathered Ali, [[person/fatima|Fatima]], [[person/hasan-ibn-ali|al-Hasan]] and [[person/husayn-ibn-ali|al-Husayn]] under his cloak and prayed for their purification (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:28:00}
- In the [[event/expedition-to-tabuk|Tabuk]] expedition he relates that the Prophet left Ali in Medina in charge of his family (the transcript has at this point a name reading 'Muhammad ibn Maslama' in the context of the deputy; a garbled line, not built on); words about being left with the women and children hurt Ali, and after the army left, the hypocrites spread in Medina that the Prophet had only left him out of dislike and to lighten himself of him (the speaker's narrative; no one is named); Ali set out after the Prophet with his sword and caught him, and the Prophet asked him: are you not content to be to me as Aaron was to Moses, except that there is no prophet after me (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:30:30}
- He relates from [[person/saad-ibn-abi-waqqas|Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas]] that he counted three things given to Ali, any one of which would have been better for him than the world and what is on it (in substance): the Prophet marrying his daughter to him, giving him the banner at Khaybar so that God opened it at his hands, and the Prophet's saying on the standing of Aaron to Moses (all paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:32:30}
- In year nine of the Hijra he relates that the Prophet sent [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] as leader of the hajj (see [[event/hajj-led-by-abu-bakr|Abu Bakr's hajj with the people]]), then surat Bara'a (al-Tawba) came down, and he sent Ali on his camel al-Qaswa to read it to the people (see [[event/proclamation-of-surat-baraa|the proclamation of surat Bara'a]]); when Ali caught up with Abu Bakr, Abu Bakr asked whether he had come as commander or as one under command, and he said under command; so he stood in the days of tashriq proclaiming to the pilgrims the articles of the declaration: travel the earth four months; no naked one shall circle the House after today; no polytheist shall circle the House; no soul enters Paradise but a Muslim soul (paraphrased), until his voice gave out and another finished the call (the transcript's 'sayyidna wabrik'; read by the museum as Abu Bakr, without certainty). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:34:30}
- He retells the story of [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]] with Banu Jadhima (the transcript's 'Banu Qudayma'/'Jathima'; the museum's reading; told in the Khalid episodes): he killed them after they had pronounced the testimony, supposing it a ruse; the Prophet disowned the act and sent Ali with money to satisfy them, and he paid the blood-money for every slain man and then gave them the rest of the money as a precaution for the Messenger of God, and when he told the Prophet, the Prophet told him he had done right and well (paraphrased) (see [[event/ali-sent-to-banu-jadhima|Ali sent to Banu Jadhima]]). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:36:30}
- He relates that before his death the Prophet took al-Hasan and al-Husayn by the hand before the Muslims and said that whoever loved him and loved these two and their father and mother would be with him in his rank on the Day of Resurrection (paraphrased), and his saying that whoever harmed Ali had harmed him and whoever insulted him had insulted him (paraphrased), which the speaker reads as a strong commendation of the house and a guard for Ali to the last days. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:37:30}
- At the death he relates that Ali was the nearest of people to the Prophet's chamber, and that [[person/aisha|Aisha]] tells that Gabriel asked leave for the Angel of Death (who had never asked leave of anyone before him and would not after him, her saying in his telling), and that the Angel of Death offered him the choice between remaining and meeting God, and he chose the Highest Companion until his hand fell; and that when the Prophet's head grew heavy on her chest she opened the door that gave onto the mosque and cried out his death (all the speaker's telling of Aisha's account, paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:39:30}
- He relates that Ali could not rise until he composed himself, then entered and laid the Prophet's head on his chest, wiping over him and saying: may my father and mother be your ransom, how good you are alive and how good dead (paraphrased); and that it was Ali who washed the Prophet, helped by [[person/abbas-ibn-abd-al-muttalib|al-Abbas]] and his son holding him (the transcript's 'al-Qatn ibn al-Abbas'; read by the museum as al-Fadl ibn al-Abbas without certainty, so not linked) while Usama ibn Zayd poured the water (the transcript's 'Sana ibn Zayd' and 'Asma ibn Ziyad'; read by the museum as [[person/usama-ibn-zayd|Usama ibn Zayd]]), and that it was he who laid him in his grave after finishing the washing. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:41:30}
- He pauses at the sum of the stands: thirteen years in Mecca and ten in Medina, giving after giving and sacrifice after sacrifice, while Ali never said enough or tired — and holds it up against the man who thinks himself a hero for two days of dawn prayer. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:42:00}
- From an audience member he passes two stories of Ali's house: Ali once came asking for food and [[person/fatima|Fatima]] said there was none in the house, so he went to the Prophet, who brought food enough for the household and all the wives (the lines are partly garbled); and Fatima asked for a servant, and the Prophet taught them to glorify God thirty-three times and praise him thirty-three and magnify him thirty-four before sleep, saying it was better for them than a servant (paraphrased), and he says Ali said they never left it all their lives; the speaker leaves the detail of Ali and Fatima's house to the next episode. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:43:30}
- The speaker attributes to Ali verses in which he counts his honoured kinship: the Prophet his father by marriage, [[person/hamza|Hamza]] lord of martyrs his uncle, [[person/jafar-ibn-abi-talib|Jafar]] who flies with the angels his cousin on his mother's side, Muhammad's daughter his shelter and beloved, and his sons by her a share of Ahmad's share (the wording of the verses is not reproduced; the speaker displays them on the screen for whoever wants to memorise them, and dwells on 'my shelter and my beloved' as a husband's loving description of his wife). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:45:30}
- He relates from Ali on his love of the Prophet: when need struck the Prophet, Ali worked a day for a Jewish man of Medina for twelve dates and brought them to him, and the Prophet told him that what moved him to it was his love of God and His Messenger (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:46:30}
- And the second: hunger struck Ali hard, so he went out to al-Awali seeking work and found a woman wanting clay wetted, and he wetted it for her for a wage of dates (the transcript gives twelve or sixteen waterskins of water and sixteen dates as wage; the figures are garbled) until his hands cracked, then he refused to eat before the Prophet and divided the dates between them, eating and setting his stones before the Prophet in jest until the Prophet's pile of stones grew and they laughed together (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep20 00:48:30}

## Left out of this episode 20

Not written from episode 20: the name of Hamza's daughter (00:24:00, garbled); the name of the woman who carried the letter to Quraysh (not given in the text); the name of the Jewish employer in the dates story (not given); an audience member's garbled lines on 'stepping aside' and revenge (00:19:30 to 00:20:30); and the mention of 'Muhammad ibn Maslama' in the place of the deputy in Medina (00:28:30, garbled). Garbled lines read with caution: Ali's age on the Hijra night (28 at 00:05:30 against 23); the name of the letter-writer to Abu Sufyan (read as Hatib ibn Abi Baltaa; 00:22:00); the name of the one who finished the call after Ali's voice gave out ('sayyidna wabrik'; read as Abu Bakr; 00:34:30); the tribe's name ('Banu Qudayma'/'Jathima'; read as Banu Jadhima; 00:35:00); the number of men who tried to overturn the door (seven?; 00:14:30); the name of 'the Imam' of the saying on the abundance of virtues (read as Ahmad ibn Hanbal; 00:02:00); the claim of Ali commanding the right wing at Uhud (00:08:30, garbled); and the figures of the clay-wetting work (00:47:30). Repetition loops discarded: 00:10:30, 00:11:30, 00:15:00, 00:18:30, 00:20:30 and 00:49:00. The wording of the verses, the Prophet's sayings and Ali's poem is not reproduced but paraphrased and marked as needing a source. The dates, figures and names in this episode are the speaker's, from a machine transcript that has not been listened to.

## Episode 21: the house of Ali and Fatima and something of Ali's character, as the speaker tells it

:::perspectives
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** Inflating dowries and marriage demands ruins young people's lives and turns them away from marriage; he cites two sayings he attributes to the Prophet, that the most blessed of women are the least in dowry, and that a suitor whose religion and trustworthiness please should be married lest there be trial in the earth (both wordings paraphrased), and calls on fathers and mothers to look for a man who will guard their daughter rather than for an expensive mahr. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:05:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** A wedding night that opens with ablution, prayer and supplication is better and sweeter than one that opens with sin, wine and dancing; that does not mean cancelling joy but a joy without the forbidden, and he recalls that the Prophet gave Ali and Fatima a wedding feast that gathered the people of Medina and they rejoiced without disobedience. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:09:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** A couple worshipping their Lord together keeps love alive and lightens the burdens of the house; he reads the pre-sleep glorification recipe in this light, and tells for it the story of an Azhari scholar past sixty whom he saw in England buying a gift for his wife and who keeps a weekly Friday sitting of remembrance with his family (the scholar is not named in the transcript), and calls husbands and wives to pray and remember God together. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:22:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** A couple's quarrels should stay inside the house while they can still be solved: Fatima did not tell the Prophet the cause of the quarrel even though he was her father, and the Prophet did not ask her for it but went and solved the situation; he deplores every quarrel reaching the neighbours and relatives. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:27:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** Islam is not a religion that loves war in its origin: the Prophet did not love the name "Harb" (war) for Ali's son, and he cites the saying attributed to the Prophet on the most loved and most hated names (paraphrased); only when there is no way to the religion's honour and preservation but war, then let it be — but the origin is that the religion does not love it. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:11:30}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** True detachment (zuhd) is to hold the world in your hand and not let it enter your heart, not to be incapable of it and call incapacity zuhd; Ali was the caliph of the Muslims owning the world and it never entered his heart. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:42:00}
- **Speaker's view (opinion):** People are moved by a Muslim's character more than by his prayer, his intelligence or his appearance; he reads the Islam of the Jewish man who spat on Ali's face in this light: a man embraced Islam through a trait of character. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:39:30}
- **Speaker's closing (opinion):** He announces that much of Ali's knowledge has still not been told, and that the next episode continues the life with Ali ibn Abi Talib. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:53:00}
:::

- The speaker opens by summing up the three past episodes on [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] (his introduction, then his stands with the Prophet up to his thirty-third year in his telling), and announces that the episode enters the house of Ali and [[person/fatima|Fatima]] and their sons [[person/hasan-ibn-ali|al-Hasan]] and [[person/husayn-ibn-ali|al-Husayn]] from the inside, that its axis is the house and its meanings for young and married people, and that it then moves to Ali's character and traits. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:01:00}
- He relates that Fatima reached about eighteen and some of the Companions came forward seeking kinship with the Prophet: [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], who was told she was young for him, and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]], who was told the same, so the speaker concludes that the Prophet took the age gap into account in his daughter's marriage; then a maid came to Ali and urged him to come forward (not named in the transcript), and Ali hesitated because he had nothing and out of awe of saying to the Prophet, marry your daughter to me. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:02:00}
- He gives their ages at the marriage: Ali about twenty-five or twenty-six, the marriage after [[event/battle-of-badr|Badr]] in about the second or third year of the Hijra (the transcript's "ghazwat al-barida" and "ghazwat tawhid"; the museum reads it as the battle of Badr without certainty, and the date is unchecked); Ali visited the Prophet and sat silent; the Prophet asked him what brought him, and when he stayed silent said: perhaps you have come to ask for Fatima; he said yes. He asked him whether he had anything to make her lawful by; he said no; he asked him about the coat of mail he fought in (the transcript's "your invitation which you armed me with"; the museum's reading) and he said he had it and it was worth no more than four hundred dirhams; the Prophet said he had married her to him for it — so Fatima's mahr was the coat of mail at four hundred dirhams (all paraphrased) (see [[event/marriage-of-fatima-and-ali|the marriage of Ali and Fatima]]). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:04:00}
- He relates that the house of Ali and Fatima held after the marriage only a pillow stuffed with palm fibre, a piece of matting, a waterskin and a mug (the furnishing as the speaker gives it, unchecked), and he deplores the inflated customs and the demand that a wife start at the standard her family reached after thirty years. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:06:30}
- He relates that the Prophet deferred the wedding night and told Ali not to approach her until he came to them (paraphrased); when he came he performed ablution and sprinkled some of the water of his ablution on them, and prayed while they said amen, asking God for her good and the good in her (paraphrased), and they prayed two rakas (the dialogue and the supplication paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:08:30}
- A year after the marriage he mentions the birth of al-Hasan at the end of the third year of the Hijra (the speaker's date), and the Prophet's joy until the Companions, in his telling, said they had not seen his face like a piece of the moon for joy (paraphrased); the Prophet asked what they had named him rather than naming him himself, and Ali said he wanted to name him Harb, so the Prophet said he did not love that name — not in the capacity of a father-in-law but in the capacity of a prophet (paraphrased) — and he was named al-Hasan (see [[event/birth-of-hasan-ibn-ali|the birth of al-Hasan]]); and he mentions of the sunna in his telling: wrapping him in a white wrap after he was brought in a yellow one, the chewing of the date for him, the call to prayer in his right ear and the iqama in his left, and the shaving of his hair with alms of silver of its weight (all the speaker's account). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:12:00}
- He mentions the birth of al-Husayn a year after al-Hasan (the speaker's date), and the material burdens appearing on Ali; he relates that the Prophet visited Fatima and asked "where are my two sons?" (the speaker dwells on "my two sons", not "your sons", as a sign of love) and she answered that there was nothing in the house to eat and that Ali had taken them with him so they would not cry at her side and tire her; so the Prophet went searching for them until he found them (paraphrased); the speaker dwells on Ali's tenderness toward his wife and the existence of these feelings fourteen centuries ago. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:14:00}
- He retells the story of Fatima asking for a servant (told at the end of episode 20): she learned the Prophet had some captives and wanted him to give her one of them as a servant because the housework had worn her out; she found [[person/aisha|Aisha]] and told her; when it reached the Prophet he said no by God, he would not give her while the people of the Suffa found nothing to eat, and had he a slave he would sell him and spend him on the people of the Suffa (paraphrased); then the Prophet went out to their house at night and found them in their bed; Fatima was shy to be seen in bed and covered herself; he told them to stay where they were and sat between them until she felt the cold of his feet (paraphrased), and said shall he not tell her of better than what she asked, and taught them to glorify God thirty-three times and praise him thirty-three and magnify him thirty-four before sleep, which would be better for them than a servant (paraphrased) (see [[event/fatima-asks-for-servant-and-tasbih|Fatima asks for a servant and the tasbih]]); Fatima says in his telling that they never needed a servant from that day, and Ali says he never left it except the night of [[event/battle-of-siffin|Siffin]] when he forgot and remembered at night (all paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:18:00}
- He relates that the Prophet visited them one day and sensed anger between them, and asked "where is your cousin's son?" (the speaker dwells on "your cousin's son", not "your husband"); she said something had happened between them and he had angered her and gone out, and had not come back for his midday nap (the qaylula, the midday rest); the Prophet did not ask her the cause, but told a man to go and look where Ali was and not tell him (paraphrased); he was found asleep in the mosque on the dirt, his cloak fallen from his shoulder and the dirt on him, so the Prophet sat at his head wiping the dirt from his body and saying rise, Aba Turab (paraphrased), and Ali rose smiling and they parted in peace. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:27:00}
- He relates that the Banu Hisham ibn al-Mughira offered to marry their daughter, the daughter of [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]], to Ali (a second marriage, the matter being simple and accepted at that time in his telling); Ali took no step, and the Prophet ascended the minbar and said that the Banu Hisham ibn al-Mughira ask leave to marry their daughter to Ali ibn Abi Talib and he does not permit, then he does not permit, then he does not permit, unless Ali divorces his daughter (paraphrased), then made clear that he neither forbids the lawful nor permits the forbidden, but the daughter of God's enemy should not be joined with the daughter of God's prophet in one house (paraphrased; the description is attributed to the Prophet in the speaker's words); the speaker draws the lesson that a man who wants a second marriage must guard the first wife's feelings, standing and respect. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:30:30}
- He passes comments from the audience before moving to Ali's character: the merit of the kind word inside the house and between parents and children; that the trust the Prophet gave Ali as a young man (sleeping in his bed at twenty-three, and judge over Yemen at twenty-eight in his telling; see [[event/ali-expeditions-to-yemen|Ali sent to Yemen]]) is a call to give the young trust in homes, schools and universities; another question on weddings that carry sins and where true happiness lies; a word on respecting elders; and a call from the listeners to apply Islam in their homes. {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:36:30}
- In Ali's character he begins with forbearance and pardon: he relates that at [[event/battle-of-khaybar|Khaybar]] Ali fought a man of the Jews until he threw him down and sat on his chest and drew his sword to kill him, and the man spat on his face, so Ali rose off him and walked away; when the man asked why he had left him he said he had wanted to kill him for God, but when he spat on his face his killing would have been for himself, and the Messenger of God had forbidden them killing for themselves, so he left him because he could no longer kill him for God (paraphrased); the man then pronounced the two testimonies (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:39:00}
- And his humility: he relates that Ali was asked who is the bravest of people and answered [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] (the questioners expecting his own name), and explained that he saw them in Mecca while the Prophet was among about ten of the leaders of the polytheists pushing him and denying him and insulting him, each of them afraid to step forward, and Abu Bakr came and pushed them away and entered saying: will you kill a man for saying my Lord is God (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:40:00}
- He relates that his son [[person/muhammad-ibn-al-hanafiyya|Muhammad ibn al-Hanafiyya]] (his son from other than Fatima, born after her death in his telling) asked him who is the best of people after the Messenger of God, and he said Abu Bakr, and then Umar; the son tells that he feared if he asked "then whom" he would say Uthman, so he said: then you? And Ali said: no, my son, I am only a man of the Muslims (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:41:00}
- He mentions a third story of humility whose opening is garbled in the transcript, its substance being that Ali said the father of the family is the one most deserving of carrying the flour, and carried the food on his back to his house, seeing no shame in a man returning to his family carrying the food (the start of the story unclear). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:41:30}
- And his detachment: he relates that Ali, while caliph of the Muslims, recited every night before sleep the verse of the abode of the hereafter granted to those who seek neither exaltedness on earth nor corruption, and that the end is for the God-fearing (the wording of the verse is not written), and attributes to [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]] that the most ascetic on the face of the earth after the prophets is Ali ibn Abi Talib (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:42:30}
- He relates from a successor who saw him at night without being seen (the transcript writes his name "Zawq ibn Damgha al-Kinani"; a garbled line, not read) that he stood long in prayer swaying like the bitten (bitten by a snake; the speaker explains the Arabs call the bitten man "salim" in hope of his safety), then took hold of his beard and wept, imploring God, then said: O world, deceive another, I have divorced you thrice (paraphrased); the speaker notes that Ali is the narrator of the saying attributed to the Prophet cursing the muhallil (needs_source), and that he heard him say: ah for the paucity of provision, the desolation of the road and the length of the journey (paraphrased; the speaker explains it as the road of the Resurrection and the journey to paradise). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:45:30}
- And his spending: he relates that the verse about those who spend their wealth by night and day, secretly and openly (its wording is not written), came down concerning Ali because he gave in four ways: by night, by day, in secret and in public (the occasion of revelation as the speaker tells it); and that a beggar stood over him while he prayed so he took something out of his hand or pocket and threw it to him so as not to miss the reward of giving (partly garbled lines). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:46:30}
- His courage, he says, took the three past episodes, so he moves to his certainty: he relates that a man came warning him that some people had agreed to send one of them to kill him, and Ali answered in his telling that no person walks the earth without two angels guarding him by God's command (pointing to the verse whose wording is not written), and that the appointed term is well guarded, coming neither early nor late (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:48:00}
- And of his certainty he relates that he had given Fatima six dirhams for flour, and when a beggar asked him he sent al-Hasan to his mother to bring one dirham of them; she reminded him he had given them to her for flour, and he said in his telling that none of them truly believes until his trust in what is in God's hand is greater than his trust in what is in his own hand (paraphrased), so he gave it to the beggar saying he was certain that whoever brings a good deed has ten like it (paraphrased); then he traded in a sale and a purchase (the transcript: a camel for a hundred and forty dirhams bought on credit, then something sold for two hundred leaving him sixty dirhams; the figures and the steps of the trade are garbled), and returned to Fatima with sixty dirhams, and when she asked where he had got it he answered it was from God's saying on the tenfold of the good deed (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:50:00}
- And his knowledge: he relates the saying attributed to him that there is no verse in the book of God but he knows where it came down and when it came down, by night or day, in plain or mountain (paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:51:00}
- And his judging and sharpness: he relates that while commander of the faithful a pit (a dug well) in Kufa had a lion fall into it, and people crowded around it looking, so four fell in holding to one another and the lion killed them (see [[event/ali-judgment-lion-pit-yemen|Ali's judgment on the four at the lion pit]]; the place of the event is Kufa in the speaker's telling, Yemen in the museum's record); their kin asked for the blood-money, then sixty camels; Ali judged that the first gets fifteen because he held three and so has a quarter, the second twenty because he held two and so has a third, the third thirty because he held one and so has a half, and the fourth the full diyya because he held no one, and that all who crowded the place share in paying it (the speaker's figures and the wording of the ruling paraphrased). (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:52:30}
- He relates that a woman complained to him that her brother had died leaving six hundred dinars and her mother had given her only one dinar of the inheritance, and he said to her: perhaps your brother left a wife and two daughters and a mother and twelve children (the transcript's "16 and 12 boys and girls"; the figures are garbled), meaning one dinar may be her rightful share (paraphrased); the speaker asks where this knowledge came from, and closes that the next episode continues with Ali. (needs_source) {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-amr-khaled wa-nal9a-al-aheba/ep21 00:53:30}

## Left out of this episode 21

Not written from episode 21: the name of the maid who urged Ali to come forward (not named; 00:01:30); the name of the Azhari scholar in the England story (not named; 00:19:30 to 00:22:00); the name of the successor who narrated the night-prayer vision ("Zawq ibn Damgha al-Kinani", garbled; 00:42:30); the name of the Jewish man in the spitting story, the name of the woman who complained of the inheritance and of her brother (not given); a story one of the women present asked about the fostering of al-Hasan and al-Husayn (00:37:00, garbled, not understood and not told); the second name in the saying on the most hated names attributed to the Prophet ("wa-Naskhar", garbled; 00:11:00); and the description of al-Hasan's first wrap ("a yellow wrap" then "a white wrap"; its image garbled; 00:10:30). Garbled lines read with caution: the name of the raid before which the marriage fell ("ghazwat al-barida/tawhid"; 00:02:30); the figures of the trade after the dirham story (the camel at a hundred and forty, the sale at two hundred, sixty remaining; 00:49:00 to 00:50:00); the shares of the four's blood-money (the speaker's figures; 00:51:30 to 00:52:30); the inheritance figures (six hundred dinars, one dinar, the number of heirs; 00:52:30 to 00:53:00); the place of the lion-pit event (Kufa in the speaker's telling; the museum's record places it in Yemen); the start of the flour-carrying story (00:41:00, garbled); and what exactly was given to the beggar during prayer (00:46:00, garbled). Repetition loops discarded: 00:05:00, 00:08:00, 00:41:00 and 00:50:00 (repeated characters without meaning). The wording of the verses, the Prophet's sayings, Ali's words and the wedding-night supplication is not reproduced but paraphrased and marked as needing a source. The dates, figures and names in this episode are the speaker's, from a machine transcript that has not been listened to.
